# ShoppyDrive Audit Remediation Tracker

Operational source of truth for remediation status. The original findings are defined by two reports: `docs/SHAPPYDRIVE_FORENSIC_AUDIT_REPORT.txt` (CRIT/HIGH/MED/LOW IDs, 2026-11-01) and `docs/SHOPPYDRIVE_FULL_SYSTEM_FORENSIC_AUDIT_REPORT_2026-11-02.md` (FSA-01–FSA-24). `docs/MASTER_FIXING_TRACKER.md` remains the record of earlier remediation work (PROMO-LOY-001, PAY-RETURN-001, …).

**Reconciliation (2026-11-06):** `origin/main` = `df39721`. Local `main` was fast-forwarded to it, then gained local, unpushed commits from this session (listed in the rows they touch). Every row below that used to say `UNCOMMITTED`/`COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED` (FSA-06–FSA-24, MED-18, LP-INVOICE-STATUS-001 and all 2026-11-05 audit rows) was checked: each recorded commit is an ancestor of `8193cfb`, and each "uncommitted" fix's code exists in `8193cfb`'s tree. "Deploy to Hostinger" run `42046689296` succeeded on `8193cfb` (2026-11-05 14:36 UTC), so those rows are now `DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED`. The previous status text is kept in each cell. **The latest push, `df39721` (Prettier-only, 3 files), did NOT deploy:** run `42047873099` failed its required checks on the TEST-FLAKE-001 flaky test (`BuyNowCheckoutTest`), so production is at `8193cfb`. No production functional verification was performed; nothing is `VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION`.

**Update (2026-11-06, later):** the owner committed and pushed `012bcc8` (OPS-002 heartbeat crash fix) on top of this session's first five local commits, so `origin/main` = `012bcc8` and production runs it (deploy run `42115488048` succeeded: 'Nothing to migrate', health check passed on attempt 1). TEST-FLAKE-001, SEC-022, SEC-017 and INFRA-003 are therefore deployed or running in CI; see their rows. This session's later commits (SEC-019 record, BIZ-011, BIZ-032/BIZ-034 and this update) are local and unpushed.

**Update (2026-11-06, pushes 2 and 3):** the owner approved two pushes in this session. `012bcc8..c11d06b` (SEC-019 record, BIZ-011, BIZ-032/BIZ-034, INFRA-001; run `42127506278`) and `c11d06b..218a8e8` (BIZ-012, BIZ-009 record, FE-001, FE-008, FE-007; run `42131081441`) both succeeded, with maintenance mode and health checks passing. FE-012 and later commits are local and unpushed.

**Update (2026-11-11, tester reports; FIXED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED, not pushed):** (1) *Report #23, deleted product still in cart* — BE-002's named "no longer available" message was lost for a deleted product that had variants: its variant rows are deleted with it, so `PlaceOrderRequest`'s `items.*.variant_id` rules failed first as a silent field error and checkout just reloaded. `PlaceOrderRequest::prepareForValidation()` now clears the variant on a line whose product no longer exists, so the existing cart-issue dialog names it; lines whose product exists validate as before. Test added to `UnavailableCartItemMessageTest`. (2) *Oversized price → `QueryException`* — `Admin\ProductRequest` had no upper bound on `decimal(10,2)` price columns (or the 32-bit integer stock columns); added `max` rules (99999999.99 / 2147483647) for price, compare/cost price, variant prices, stock, low-stock threshold and `free_delivery_min_qty`. New `tests/Feature/Admin/ProductNumericLimitsTest.php`. (3) *Stock decrement timing report* — investigated, not reproduced: stock already decrements at order creation and restocks on return approval, cancel and refund; no code change. Tests run: `tests/Feature/Admin` (964 passed, 13 skipped), `UnavailableCartItemMessageTest`, `CheckoutCodDuplicateOrderTest`, `CheckoutPaymentMethodValidationTest`, `InventoryEnforcementTest`, `BundleCheckoutTest`, `CheckoutAccessGuardTest` (all passed).

**Last updated (previous baseline, kept for history):** 2026-11-03. **Git baseline:** `origin/main` = `17a1501`, level with local `main`. The push of 2026-11-03 moved it from `0ef9d9a` to `17a1501` with `8d17320` (FSA-13), `8cb8ff7` (FSA-09), `1d16a71` (FSA-11), `9c840f7` (FSA-03, FSA-12), `985d73b` (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05), `d1f258c` (tracker documentation) and `17a1501` (handoff documentation). One stash (`stash@{0}`, untouched); no tags; no other branches. Pushing `main` deploys to Hostinger and runs `migrate --force`.

**Deployment record (2026-11-03, push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`):**

- **Verified from the workflow and logs:** "Deploy to Hostinger" run `41575932713` succeeded, including `Required checks (Pint + Pest)` and `Build & Deploy`. `migrate --force` printed "Nothing to migrate", so no migration ran in this deploy. The runtime check reported `APP_ENV=production` with `APP_DEBUG=false` before and after the sync. The health check passed on attempt 1.
- **Verified by smoke check:** `https://shoppydrive.com/`, `/up` and `/products` returned 200. `/` returned the ShoppyDrive page with Inertia page data. `/order/SMOKE-NOT-REAL/confirmation` returned 404. This is availability only: no order, payment, login or admin action was exercised.
- **Non-gating workflows that failed on `17a1501`:** `Run Tests` failed only on `BuyNowCheckoutTest` ("buy now places an order without ever creating a cart row"). The test buys 2 units of a product with random factory stock from 0 to 500, so it fails when the stock is 0 or 1; the same suite passed in the deploy's required checks. `Lint` failed on Prettier in `hero-slides/create.tsx`, `hero-slides/edit.tsx` and `Home.tsx`, none of which the seven commits changed. Neither is fixed yet.
- **Production database (owner-supplied pre-check through Laravel's PDO connection):** MariaDB 11.8.9, not MySQL; `READ-COMMITTED`; `innodb_snapshot_isolation` ON; 0 invalid `pending_checkouts.checkout_data` rows; 74 tables (73 expected; the extra table is not yet identified).
- **Pending functional verification:** FSA-05 product visibility impact, the expected unique indexes, `job_batches`, and COD, mobile-banking and Google-login smoke tests. The read-only production checks had not been run when this deploy happened. No finding below is `VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION` on this evidence.

**Deployment evidence (2026-11-03, read-only `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml`):** "Deploy to Hostinger" succeeded for `1db8482` (2026-11-02 08:24 UTC, first push containing `18125d4` and `3254bac`), `84aa1c0` and `0ef9d9a` (10:27 UTC). The run logs did not show `migrate` output, so which migrations ran and with what result (including whether MED-06 skipped an index) is **not verified**. Code on `main` is therefore `DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED`, never `VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION`, until production evidence exists.

**Statuses (from 2026-11-03):** OPEN, IN_PROGRESS, FIXED_LOCALLY, TESTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION, BLOCKED, PARTIALLY_FIXED, ACCEPTED_RISK (plus NOT_APPLICABLE for LOW-07, kept as recorded). Never mark progress without evidence. Old statuses in the historical sections below are kept verbatim; the table below supersedes them. Historical vocabulary (pre-2026-11-03): NOT_STARTED, INVESTIGATING, IN_PROGRESS, BLOCKED, PARTIALLY_COMPLETED, COMPLETED, ACCEPTED_RISK, NOT_APPLICABLE; `COMPLETED (LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION)` meant verified locally and not deployed at the time. Completed findings are not reopened without new evidence.

| BIZ-005 | High | Paid orders can still be cancelled and then cannot be refunded | **REOPENED, then FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, PUSHED, DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (2026-11-09, commit `f28eea4`, in push `ee3711d..915600e`; owner confirmed the deploy run succeeded).** Tester skipped this ("admin needs to handle this manually as refund isn't automated" — true of every refund in this app, not a new finding). Reproduced the tester's exact check directly anyway (create a paid pending order, PATCH account.orders.cancel) and found a real, different dead end the 2026-11-05 fix didn't cover: the order correctly reaches CANCELLED+PAID, but admin refund was then refused with "Courier verification is required... No current courier verification readiness attestation exists" — for an order that never touched a courier at all. Previously recorded: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`5c8f9bf`; deploy `42046689296`, 2026-11-05) for the original "cancelled order cannot be refunded" refusal, which this builds on, not replaces | Root cause: `CourierEvidenceChain::isCourierInvolved()` treated any order whose status wasn't PENDING/PROCESSING as courier-involved regardless of actual evidence, so a plain cancelled order got forced through courier-readiness verification — an unrelated admin chore that goes stale on every deploy. Owner confirmed (asked first, since this file is explicitly flagged "approved, check before changing"): judge CANCELLED/REFUNDED by actual evidence (consignment IDs, raw status, submission/evidence rows — all permanent once set, never cleared) instead of by status; SHIPPED/DELIVERED deliberately kept fail-closed by status alone, preserving the existing "even with readiness" protection for those two, since reaching either implies a courier should have physically handled the order. `NO_COURIER_STATUSES` widened from `[PENDING, PROCESSING]` to include `CANCELLED, REFUNDED`. See BIZ-005 notes | Updated the existing parametrized `OrderRefundTest` case (now asserts the widened safe set, not just PENDING/PROCESSING) and added a new end-to-end test: customer self-cancels a paid mobile-banking order via the real `account.orders.cancel` route, admin refunds it with zero `CourierVerificationReadiness` rows in the database. Full `OrderRefundTest.php`: 45/45. Full Courier/Refund/LostParcel/DeliveryVerification/Return sweep: 399 tests, 376 passed, 23 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. | BIZ-002 | High | A cart containing a complete bundle can never be checked out | **FOLLOW-UP DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (pushed with owner approval in `c842c5b`; deploy run `42719140927` success, live home 200, 2026-11-10). Previously FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (2026-11-10, tester item 44: an admin could still type more than the stock).** The server already refused such a save (13/13 in `ProductBundleManagementTest`), but the box accepted any number until Save. Owner decisions, 2026-11-10: the quantity stops at the stock as it is typed, and a live bundle whose stock has fallen below its recipe stays visible as "Out of Stock" and returns by itself when refilled. `create.tsx`/`edit.tsx`: `cappedQuantity()` applies on quantity and variant changes, and a note shows the stock and how many bundles it fills; a recipe already above stock when the edit page loads is left alone until that row is changed, and the server rule still refuses it. `BundleService::availableSets()` mirrors `InventoryService::assertAvailable()` and is shared as `available_sets` on the home and Bundle Deals pages; `BundleCard` disables Add and caps the set stepper at it. Checkout still enforces stock. Tests: 8 new dataset cases in `HomepageBundleDealsTest` failed before the change; 41/41 across the home-deals, admin, pricing, checkout and auto-assemble bundle files. Pint, ESLint and Prettier clean; full suite not run. Headless Chrome on a disposable SQLite database: typing 10 on stock 5 gives 5; typing 9 on a 5-stock variant gives 5, then switching to a 2-stock variant gives 2; an over-limit recipe loads unchanged and caps when edited; a sold-out bundle shows a disabled "Out of Stock"; a 2-set bundle stops at 2. Not yet retested by the tester. Earlier record: **REOPENED, then FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, PUSHED, DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (2026-11-09, commit `0faaf9f`, in push `ee3711d..915600e`; owner confirmed the deploy run succeeded).** A tester found a different, new way to hit the same symptom: an admin can set a bundle component's quantity higher than the product's actual stock — e.g. a recipe needing 10 units of a product that only has 5. Since nothing lets a customer buy less than one full bundle set, that bundle can never be bought, period. Checkout itself already handled this correctly (a clear "Only N unit(s) left in stock" refusal, confirmed by reproducing it directly — not the generic "Some prices have changed" the tester's note implied); the actual gap was that the admin was never stopped from creating that unfulfillable recipe to begin with. Covers both `store()` and `update()` (shared request class). `Admin\ProductBundleController::searchProducts()`/`edit()` now also select `stock_quantity` so the admin UI can show it; `create.tsx`/`edit.tsx` display each component's live stock next to its quantity field and flag it in red before submission. See BIZ-002 notes | New: 4 cases in `Admin/ProductBundleManagementTest` (quantity over a plain product's stock refused; over a specific variant's stock refused; accepted within stock, then a later update past it refused the same way; a variant-less pick of a product that has variants refused regardless of quantity): file 13/13. | SEC-021 | Medium | Back button after logout shows account data | DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`876640e` + `7addfdf`; on `origin/main`, included in successful deploy run `42046689296` of `8193cfb`, 2026-11-05 — reconciled 2026-11-06). Previously recorded: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`876640e`, follow-up fix `7addfdf`), NOT PUSHED (2026-11-05) | Two parts: (1) `routes/account.php` and `routes/admin.php`'s authenticated groups now carry a `history.encrypt` middleware (Inertia's built-in history encryption, gated — see follow-up below). (2) A new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (bound in `FortifyServiceProvider`, same pattern as the existing `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`) calls `Inertia::clearHistory()` for customer logout; `AdminAuthController::logout()` calls it directly. Both deliberately call it *after* `session()->invalidate()` — that flushes all session attributes, including the `clearHistory` flag itself if set any earlier (e.g. a naive `Logout` event listener, which fires *before* `invalidate()` — tried first, found broken, reverted). **Follow-up fix (`7addfdf`, owner-reported):** the original cut used inertia-laravel's bare `inertia.encrypt` middleware unconditionally — history encryption needs `window.crypto.subtle`, which doesn't exist without HTTPS (or localhost), and Inertia's client throws ("Unable to encrypt history") rather than skipping quietly, as an uncaught promise rejection that stops the page visit from completing. Every `/admin` and `/account` page visited over plain HTTP (any local/LAN dev visit) got stuck "loading" and never rendered — reported against the admin flash-sales page specifically, but not specific to it. New `ConditionallyEncryptHistory` middleware (alias `history.encrypt`) only calls `Inertia::encryptHistory()` when `$request->secure()`; production is unaffected, since `RedirectToHttps` already forces HTTPS there first. New `AuthenticateAdminSession` middleware (alias `admin.session`, added to `routes/admin.php`'s authenticated group) mirrors the same password-hash-fingerprint approach for `admin` specifically, keyed by **admin id + hash** (not hash alone — a session switching to a *different* admin, which `Auth::login()` itself doesn't guard against, and which is exactly what `actingAs()` does between two test calls, is a new identity to adopt, not a stolen session to punish). `AdminController::update()` rotates `remember_token` on any password change and resyncs the *current* session's own fingerprint on a self-edit, so changing your own password doesn't also log out the session that changed it. See SEC-005 notes | New `Admin/AdminSessionSecurityTest.php` (5 tests: stays valid normally; signed out when the password changes elsewhere; self-change resyncs without self-logout; switching identity is adopted, not punished; fingerprint is id+hash, not hash alone). | PERF-001 | Medium | Product listing ran a query for every variant | **REOPENED, then DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (2026-11-11; fix `cd8c952`, pushed with owner approval in `ce8d2cd` (2026-11-11, `035823c..ce8d2cd`); deploy run `42895410141` success — `checks` (Pint + full Pest) passed, production `migrate` ran, health check passed, live home 200; awaiting the tester's retest).** Previously: FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED (2026-11-11). Tester re-failed it 2026-11-10: "found that product giving out so much information leading the conclusion that it really does run multiple queries for a single product". The tester was right about the product page. Measured on this code (SQLite feature test): `/products` was already flat (12 products × 1 or × 30 variants = 32 queries; 33 with a flash sale), and home was flat too (34 either way). The product detail page was not: 30 queries with 1 variant, 59 with 30, and **91 with 30 variants on an active flash sale**, because `ProductController` called `FlashSaleItem::findActiveFor()` once per variant (one `flash_sale_items` query plus one `flash_sales` eager-load each). That is the audit's own second half ("a 30-variant product page costs about 90 extra, and each variant adds flash-sale lookups"; recommendation: "resolve flash prices in a batch"), which the 2026-11-05 fix never covered. `LandingPageController::serializeProduct()` and `AiAssistantTools::checkProductAvailability()` had the same per-variant loop. Fix: new `FlashSaleItem::findActiveForVariants()` loads every candidate for the product and its variants in one query and picks per variant using the same candidate set, stock-exhaustion filter and ordering as `findActiveFor()` (the sort closure moved unchanged into a shared private `compareCandidates()`; `findActiveFor()`'s query is otherwise untouched, and checkout pricing/stock enforcement still call it). All three per-variant loops now use it. After: the product page runs the same number of queries for 1 or 30 variants on sale (old code in the same test: 25 vs 83). Previously: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`bd8c4e7`; on `origin/main`, included in successful deploy run `42046689296` of `8193cfb`, 2026-11-05 — reconciled 2026-11-06). **Retest (2026-11-09):** tester's note was cut off ("need", no actual content) under a Fail. Re-confirmed directly: the `variants.media` eager-load is still present in `ProductController`, `ProductListingController` and the other call sites the original fix covered (grep-verified); `ProductListingTest` (7/7), which mirrors the audit's own 12 products × 6 variants repro and asserts under 40 queries, still passes; live `/products` returns 200 in ~0.58s. No code change. Previously recorded: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-05) | **2026-11-11:** `app/Models/FlashSaleItem.php` (new `findActiveForVariants()`, shared `compareCandidates()`), `app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/LandingPageController.php`, `app/Services/AiAssistantTools.php` — per-variant `findActiveFor()` loops replaced by one batched lookup. **2026-11-05:** `ProductVariant::$appends = ['image']` reads its own `media` relation during every serialization; without `variants.media` eager-loaded, that's one query per variant (the audit measured 101 queries for 12 products × 6 variants). Beyond the finding's own 3 files, the same pattern existed anywhere else a product's `variants` were serialized without `.media`: `WishlistController`, `LandingPageController`, `BrandController`, `SearchController`, `FlashSaleController` (both query sites), and `Admin\ProductController::edit()` — fixed all of them for consistency (grep-verified: the remaining bare `'variants'`/`with('variants')` call sites all build a hand-picked array that never touches `->image`, so they don't have this bug). Previously recorded: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-05) | Three sub-fixes, matching the recommendation exactly. (1) `config/mail.php`'s `smtp.timeout` was `null` (no limit) — now `env('MAIL_TIMEOUT', 15)`, so a hung connection fails fast instead of occupying a worker. (3) New `Admin\FailedJobController::retryAll()` + `admin.failed-jobs.retryAll` route retries every currently-retryable failed job in one request (same per-row lock+allow-list check as the single retry, so a job that became unsafe to retry mid-loop is skipped, not retried twice); `failed-jobs/index.tsx` gets a "Retry All" button next to the existing per-row one | New tests in `FailedJobManagementTest.php` (retries every eligible job, skips non-allow-listed ones, reports "nothing retryable," view-only staff forbidden) and the existing `DrainQueueCommandTest.php` assertion updated for the new backoff value. New `POST /api/sms/heartbeat` (`SmsTestController::heartbeat()`, same bearer-token auth as `/sms/receive`, refactored into a shared `tokenIsValid()`; new `sms-forwarder-heartbeat` rate limiter mirroring `sms-forwarder`'s "valid token never throttled" shape). `ReceivedSmsController::poll()`'s `forwarder_seems_down` now delegates to it (admin sidebar badge unchanged in behavior, just faster). Please use Cash on Delivery." `PlaceOrderRequest::withValidator()` and `LandingPageController::placeOrder()` independently reject a `mobile_banking` submission server-side whenever the forwarder is down (defense in depth against a stale page). **Android app** (`~/AndroidStudioProjects/ShoppyDriveSMSForwarder`, not part of this git repo): `SmsForwarderService`'s foreground service now also pings `{endpoint}/api/sms/heartbeat` every 90 seconds via a self-rescheduling `Handler`, started in `onStartCommand` and stopped in `onDestroy`; the heartbeat URL is derived from the existing configured `endpoint_url` (its sibling in the same `/api/sms/` path), so nothing new for the owner to configure. Fire-and-forget: a failed ping is exactly the signal the server should see, not an app-level error | New `SmsForwarderHeartbeatTest` (auth, recording, 5-minute down threshold, fallback behavior before any heartbeat, heartbeat taking priority over a recent SMS). (3) New `users.abandoned_cart_reminders_enabled` column (migration), checked the same way. (4) New signed (no login required) `cart.reminders.unsubscribe/{user}` route + `AbandonedCartUnsubscribeController`, linked from the email, flips that column off. Earlier part (quantity max:100 crash fix): PARTIALLY_FIXED — implemented part DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`c3dc9f3`; on `origin/main`, included in successful deploy run `42046689296` of `8193cfb`, 2026-11-05 — reconciled 2026-11-06). The rest of the finding (captcha/Turnstile, a per-product pending-COD cap, phone OTP above a threshold) is explicitly marked "Business choice" by the audit itself and was deliberately not added — it needs a product decision (which challenge provider, what threshold, what UX cost to real customers) and likely a new dependency, not a code-only fix. See BIZ-011 notes **2026-11-06 additions:** new `App\Services\PendingCodLimit` (`MAX_UNITS_PER_PRODUCT = 10`, a code constant and not admin-editable) sums `order_items.quantity` per product for COD orders with this `shipping_address->phone` that are `pending`/`processing`, not trashed, and have no Pathao/Steadfast consignment. Courier-held orders don't count, because Steadfast orders stay PROCESSING for the whole delivery (BIZ-007). It locks the order's product rows in id order first, so concurrent orders for the same phone and product serialize. It is called inside the COD transaction before any stock decrement, in `CheckoutController::completeCheckout()` and `LandingPageController::placeOrder()`, and throws the new `InsufficientStockException::forPendingCodLimit()`, reusing every path's existing rollback and error display (the FSA-01 convention). Mobile banking (prepaid), admin manual orders and incomplete-order conversion are not limited. Honeypot: new `resources/js/components/honeypot-field.tsx` (off-screen, `aria-hidden`, `tabIndex=-1`, `autoComplete=off`, field `sd_confirm_ref`) on the checkout form and the landing-page order form; the server rejects a non-empty value with `prohibited` in `PlaceOrderRequest` and the landing-page validator. | New test in `CheckoutOrderPlacementTest.php`: quantity 3000000000 now fails validation (`items.0.quantity`) instead of reaching the database; no order created. | BE-002 | Low | A deleted product in a guest cart gives a bare 404 at checkout | **REOPENED, then FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, PUSHED (2026-11-09, commit `f37c051`), deploy run outcome not yet confirmed.** Tester failed it: "need clear message" — correct. The deployed fix's premise (a soft-deleted product keeps its name via `withTrashed()`) doesn't match real admin behavior: `Admin\ProductController::destroy()` always `forceDelete()`s (never soft-deletes — matches the project's established "no product soft-delete" convention), so the row is genuinely gone and the message fell back to generic. Investigating this surfaced two further, more severe bugs under the same root cause. Previously recorded: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`e9eb696`; deploy `42163043460`, 2026-11-06) | **(1) Guest fix:** Checkout.tsx now also submits `product_name` per item (display only); `CheckoutController::store()`'s missing-product branch falls back to it when even `withTrashed()` finds nothing. **(2) Logged-in customers — a worse bug found while fixing (1):** `cart_items.product_id`/`variant_id` were `cascadeOnDelete`, so force-deleting a product (or just destroying its variants, which `products.id`'s own cascade does as part of the same delete) silently destroyed the cart_item row itself — the item just vanished from the customer's saved cart with zero message, ever. New migration makes both columns nullable with `nullOnDelete`, and adds `cart_items.product_name` (same snapshot convention as `order_items.product_name`), written by `CartManager` on every create/update. `collectCartIssues()` falls back to it when the product is gone and `withTrashed()` also finds nothing (true once product_id itself is null). **(3) A deeper, pre-existing bug found while fixing (2):** even with (2)'s schema fix, `CartManager::replaceItems()`'s cleanup step deleted *any* existing row absent from a resubmitted items list — but an unavailable item was never visible to the client (`validItems()` already filtered it out of the `cart` prop), so its absence from a realistic resubmission (Checkout.tsx always sends its full list) was never the customer choosing to drop it. This silently erased the row *before* `collectCartIssues()` ever ran, for both soft- and force-deleted products, predating today's cascade fix entirely — the original fix's own test suite only ever exercised an unrealistic "items key omitted" path that never actually goes through this cleanup. Fixed by excluding currently-unavailable existing rows from that deletion. `itemKey()` (used to detect/dedupe matching rows) widened to accept a null product/variant id, disambiguated by the row's own id so two orphaned rows are never treated as duplicates of each other | **Migration verified directly against real MySQL** (disposable `shoppydrive_test_be002`, migrated fresh, schema inspected, a real force-delete on both a plain and a variant product confirmed `SET NULL` + the row survives with its name — db dropped after). | SEC-011 | Low | Password reset link is built from the request Host | **PUSHED (2026-11-10, commit `67a72bd`, as part of a combined push `bd6e6b9..6af2deb` with SEC-023/BIZ-024 and a teammate's UI commits; deploy run outcome not confirmed — no `gh` CLI in this session; status corrected 2026-11-10 after a commit-history cross-check found this row still said "pending push" although the push had already happened).** a tester actually sent `POST /forgot-password` with `Host: evil.example` and got back a reset link built from it — `trustHosts()` alone was not reliably rejecting a forged Host. Confirmed live with read-only requests: `curl -H "Host: evil.example" https://shoppydrive.com/forgot-password` got connection-reset (Hostinger's TLS/SNI layer, not this app — doesn't cover port 80 at all), but `curl -H "Host: evil.example" http://shoppydrive.com/forgot-password` (plain HTTP) returned a plain 200, proving the app itself does not reject it. Root cause understood: `Illuminate\Foundation\Application::runningUnitTests()` is literally `environment() === 'testing'` — `trustHosts()`'s own skip condition depends on that, so nothing in this codebase's test suite could ever exercise its *real* production-enabled behavior end-to-end; it was asserted only in isolation (`Request::getHost()` called directly), not through an actual HTTP request. Previously: pushed with owner approval in `e1ebd4b`; deploy run `42268895645` succeeded | New `App\Http\Middleware\RejectUntrustedHost`, prepended first in the `web` group (ahead of `RedirectToCanonicalDomain`/`RedirectToHttps`/`BlockBlockedIp`): in `production` only, compares the *raw* `Host` header (never `$request->getHost()`, which is exactly the mechanism that wasn't reliably engaging) against `config('app.url')`'s host and its subdomains, `abort(400)` on a mismatch. Fails open (passes the request through) only if `APP_URL` itself can't be parsed — same escape hatch `RedirectToHttps` already uses, never guessing at a host to allow. `trustHosts()` kept as-is, genuine defense in depth | New `RejectUntrustedHostTest`, 10 tests — real end-to-end HTTP requests, not isolated calls: a forged Host (plain HTTP), a domain merely containing the real one as a prefix/suffix, the real domain and subdomains let through, a full password-reset submission with a forged Host asserted to reach the controller at all (`Mail::assertNothingQueued()`), inert outside production, fails open on an unparseable `APP_URL`, and independent of any Setting row. Unlike the original test, this one *can* exercise production mode for real, since it doesn't depend on `runningUnitTests()`. Full SQLite suite 2773 tests, 2698 passed, 0 failed from this change (3 pre-existing unrelated), 63 skipped; Pint clean | The exact reason `trustHosts()` itself silently failed on the live server (rather than just in theory) was not root-caused beyond the `runningUnitTests()` test-blind-spot finding — this fix doesn't depend on that mechanism at all, so it doesn't need to be | Pushed 2026-11-10 (`67a72bd`), as part of a combined push; deploy run outcome not confirmed | None |

The tester recommended reconsidering whether the FK fix is even needed, since "a brand containing a product cannot be deleted" — but that block (`Admin\BrandController::destroy()`) only counts *active, non-trashed* products via `$brand->products()` (Eloquent's default soft-delete scope excludes trashed rows); a brand whose only products are trashed is still deletable, which is exactly the gap this finding closed and still needs the fix for. No live admin path currently creates a trashed product at all (confirmed: no `$product->delete()`/`Product::destroy()` call anywhere outside `forceDelete()`) — this remains a defensive fix for any product trashed by another means (a future screen, direct DB access, or an older row), exactly as the original finding and its own test (which creates the trashed product directly via factory, not through the UI) already accounted for. | DB-006 | Medium | A customer admin-deleted (anonymized) can never register again with the same email, with no way back | **NEW FINDING (2026-11-09, a tester's freeform report — "why is that?").** FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, PUSHED (2026-11-09, commit `b917344`), deploy run outcome not yet confirmed. Root cause: `Admin\CustomerController::destroy()` calls `User::anonymize()`, which deliberately leaves `email` untouched (CLAUDE.md's documented DB-001 order-history decision) but randomizes the password to something nobody knows — so the email is permanently occupied and the real person can neither re-register (`CreateNewUser` sees the row exists) nor sign back in. Offered the owner a `hasRetainedHistory()`-gated hard-delete (matching what customer self-deletion already does for a no-history account) or an admin-triggered undo; owner chose **keep always-anonymize for admin deletions, add an undo instead**, and asked to keep the snapshot indefinitely (not purged after a window) | `User::anonymize()` now captures everything it's about to destroy — current name/phone/avatar/password hash/remember_token, every address's redacted fields (id + the 7 fields actually redacted; label/country/is_default are never touched so aren't snapshotted), and every social-account row's full data (it deletes them outright, not just redacts) — into a new nullable `anonymization_snapshot` JSON column (`$hidden`, so it never reaches any response; the password hash inside it is restored via `forceFill()` unchanged, since the `hashed` cast never re-hashes an already-hashed value). New `User::restoreFromAnonymization(Admin $causer): bool` reverses it in one transaction: restores the user's own fields, updates each address row by id back to its original fields (skipped gracefully if the row is gone), recreates each social account (skipped if the customer already reconnected that exact provider themselves, so a live reconnection is never clobbered by a stale restore), clears `anonymized_at`/the snapshot, and logs a `customer.restored` activity entry. New `Admin\CustomerController::restore()` (`POST customers/{user}/restore`, same `customers`/`delete` permission and mother-password re-entry as `destroy()`) calls it. `show()` now exposes a `can_restore` boolean (true only when anonymized *and* a snapshot exists) instead of ever exposing the raw snapshot. New `RestoreCustomerDialog` mirrors the existing `DeleteCustomerDialog`'s mother-password-confirm pattern; `admin/customers/show.tsx` shows "Restore Customer" in the same slot "Anonymize Customer" occupied, once anonymized. `DeleteCustomerDialog`'s copy no longer says "this cannot be undone" — it names the restore path instead | New tests in `CustomerDeletionTest.php` (10 added, 24/24 total): name/phone/email/password restored; every redacted address field restored exactly, label/country/is_default untouched throughout; a deleted social account is recreated; restoring never overwrites one the customer already reconnected themselves; wrong mother password rejected, nothing changes; restoring a never-anonymized or snapshot-less (pre-feature) customer is refused, not crashed; the activity event is `customer.restored`, distinct from `customer.anonymized`; the raw snapshot never reaches the Inertia response, only `can_restore`. Migration verified directly against real MySQL (disposable `shoppydrive_test_restore`, migrated fresh, dropped after). Full Customer/User/Address/SocialAccount/Admin sweep: 1261 tests, 1233 passed, 28 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Full SQLite suite: 2678 tests, 2603 passed, 3 failed (pre-existing local-`.env`-only `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` mismatch, unrelated), 72 skipped. Pint, tsc, ESLint (0 errors) and Prettier clean | Not verified in a live browser (no browser tool in this session). The MySQL-only concurrency race tests were grepped, not re-run — none call `anonymize()`/`CustomerController` directly, so judged unaffected. A customer anonymized *before* this migration has no snapshot and so cannot be restored (handled: refused with a message, not a crash) — this only protects anonymizations from here on | Pushed; deploy run outcome and functional verification pending | Owner's own design: keep always-anonymize (reject the hasRetainedHistory()-branch alternative offered), add undo instead, snapshot retained indefinitely (reject the 30-day-purge alternative offered) |

Forgot-password's request step never checked `anonymized_at`, so it still mailed a reset link to an anonymized account's (still-live, per DB-006) email. `ResetUserPassword::reset()` already refuses to *complete* such a reset ("This account is no longer active"), so the link could never actually be used — mailing it anyway only revealed to whoever reads that inbox that the account still technically exists, and, now that DB-006 also exists, would have let a customer bypass an admin's anonymization entirely via self-service password reset | `User::sendPasswordResetNotification()` (already the app's own override of Fortify's default) now no-ops when `anonymized_at !== null`, before ever queuing `QueuedResetPassword`. `GenericPasswordResetLinkResponse`'s reply is unaffected — it never depended on whether a notification was actually sent, so no SEC-015 enumeration regression | New test in `PasswordResetTest.php`: an anonymized account's forgot-password request sends nothing (`Notification::assertNothingSent()`) while the response stays the same generic message. | DB-008 | Low | "Send Reset Link" could be spammed with no feedback before the real server-side limiter eventually refused it | | **REOPENED AND DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (pushed with owner approval in `c842c5b`; deploy run `42719140927` success, live home 200, 2026-11-10). Previously FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (2026-11-10): the tester reported the button could still be spammed after `b917344` deployed successfully (run `42621266834`).** Root cause: the cooldown never worked outside tests. `StampPasswordResetRequestedAt` stored a date object, `config/session.php` uses `serialization => json`, so the next request read a string, the `instanceof CarbonInterface` check failed and `resendAvailableAt` was always null. Reproduced on a real local server with the file session driver; the array session used in tests keeps the PHP object and hid it. Fix: the session now holds a Unix timestamp, read with `is_int()` (same pattern as `OrderConfirmationAccess` and `OrderPermanentDeletionService`), and the page's clock ticks from page load so the countdown starts on the right number. Tests: new `PasswordResetTest` case that JSON-round-trips the stored value, failed before the fix and passes after; `PasswordResetTest.php` 14/14; Pint, ESLint and Prettier clean on the changed files; full suite not run. Not yet retested by the tester. Mirrors the email-verification page's own existing resend-cooldown pattern (`resendAvailableAt` + a live countdown), which this didn't have yet | New `App\Http\Middleware\StampPasswordResetRequestedAt`, added to `config('fortify.middleware')` (runs globally on every Fortify route, like the existing `ThrottleFortifyActions`) — stamps `session('password_reset_requested_at')` on every `password.email` hit, regardless of outcome, so even a throttled or validation-failed attempt still starts the cooldown and timing can't reveal whether the email existed (SEC-015). `Fortify::requestPasswordResetLinkView()` shares `resendAvailableAt` as that timestamp + 90 seconds (the middle of the tester's "1 or 2 min" ask). Session-backed rather than a user column, since a forgot-password request has no account row to persist against for a nonexistent or not-yet-looked-up email. `forgot-password.tsx` gets the exact same countdown button `verify-email.tsx` already uses | New tests in `PasswordResetTest.php` (4): no cooldown on first load; a real request sets one ~90s out; the cooldown is set the same way for a nonexistent email; it reads as past once elapsed. Caught a real bug while testing: `now()` returns `CarbonImmutable` in this app (`Date::use(CarbonImmutable::class)`), which is not an instance of `Carbon\Carbon` — an `instanceof Carbon` check silently always failed and never shared a deadline; fixed to check `CarbonInterface`, which both implement. | SEC-013 | Low | No HSTS or CSP on the live site; API answers over plain HTTP | **REOPENED, then DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (2026-11-09; `65408da`, pushed with owner approval, deploy run `42514363138` success, health check passed; live home sends an enforced `content-security-policy` and no report-only header, and all 7 executable scripts carry the header nonce; owner confirmed 2026-11-09 that a `facebook.com/tr` request returns 200 on the live site under this policy; awaiting the tester's retest and a logged-in test order):** a tester failed this because the live CSP was report-only and allowed `'unsafe-inline'` scripts, confirmed from live headers. The owner asked for it to be fixed, which moves on from the earlier report-only decision. `SecurityHeaders` now sends an enforced `Content-Security-Policy` in production with a per-request nonce: `script-src 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic' 'self' https:`, no `'unsafe-inline'` or `'unsafe-eval'` for scripts, plus `object-src 'none'` and `blob:` for upload previews. The nonce is on the two inline layout scripts, Ziggy `@routes` and Vite tags, and new `App\Support\CspNonce` adds it to Custom Header/Footer scripts at render, so saved code does not change. `style-src` deliberately keeps `'unsafe-inline'`: styles cannot run code, and rich-text content and UI libraries use inline styles. `connect-src`, `frame-src` and `img-src` allow `https:` so vendors added through Tag Manager keep working without a deploy. HSTS and the plain-HTTP API refusal were already live and are unchanged. Tests: 4 net new in `ProductionTransportSecurityTest`; full SQLite suite once, 2,574 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed. Browser check: production build in headless Chrome with the policy enforced, 18 guest and admin pages, 0 violations, the real GTM container `GTM-PRKGGNHM` loaded and its Meta Pixel tag defined `fbq` (Facebook hosts blocked in the test so no events were sent); a control script without a nonce was blocked and reported. Not checked: logged-in customer account and checkout pages (the dev database has no eligible customer), real Facebook delivery, and Safari or Firefox. Live checks after deploy (public requests, 2026-11-07): `strict-transport-security: max-age=86400`, `permissions-policy` and `content-security-policy-report-only` present; POST `http://…/api/sms/receive` → 426; POST `https://…/api/sms/receive` → 401. Closes the MED-16 / MED-18A pending HSTS/CSP decision | `SecurityHeaders`: `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=(), payment=()` on every response (nothing in `resources/js` uses them). Production only: `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (self, inline scripts/styles, Google Fonts, https images and media, `frame-ancestors 'self'`, `report-uri /api/csp-report`), and `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=86400` on HTTPS responses only, with no `includeSubDomains` or preload. Root cause: this machine's local `.env` has `APP_URL=http://192.168.0.111:8001` (a LAN IP, for testing the site from other devices), not the default `http://localhost`. `TrustHosts` only trusts whatever domain `APP_URL` names, and these two tests hardcode `localhost` as the simulated host — so on this machine only, `localhost` doesn't match the LAN IP and gets rejected with a 400 before reaching the controller. Confirmed with `APP_URL=http://localhost php artisan test tests/Feature/ProductionTransportSecurityTest.php`: all 8 pass. CI and production are unaffected — CI's `.env` comes from `.env.example` (`APP_URL=http://localhost`) and production's is `https://shoppydrive.com`, both of which the tests' hardcoded `localhost` already matches. | BIZ-027 | Medium | Steadfast status relies on unverified webhook assumptions with no fallback | **FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, PUSHED (2026-11-10, `b92dae2`):** the OPEN RISK below is resolved — the owner supplied Steadfast's own official API docs (portal.packzy.com/api/v1), and `STEADFAST_MAP` was rebuilt on the real vocabulary from its "Status reference" section. The old keys (`pending_parcels`, `waiting_approval`, `delivered_parcels`, `partial_parcels`, `cancelled_parcells`) never appeared in Steadfast's real API at all — only `in_review` happened to be real — so a genuine `delivered`/`cancelled` report would have been silently ignored forever, never advancing the order. New map: `pending`, `in_review` → PENDING; `delivered`, `partial_delivered` → DELIVERED; `cancelled` → CANCELLED. Every `*_approval_pending` value, `hold`, `exceptional` and `unknown` are deliberately left unmapped (recorded, never settles the order), per the docs' own instruction not to settle books on an approval-pending value. The webhook signature check was also compared against the docs and needed no change — HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body, hex, `X-Signature` — it already matched exactly. Pushed to `main`; deploy workflow triggered but **not confirmed** — no `gh` CLI or GitHub API access from this session to check the Actions run result, owner should confirm the `checks`/`deploy` run succeeded. Previously: DEPLOYED, CONFIRMATION DEFERRED (2026-11-07) — pushed in `1c2d7b3`, deploy run `42298871698` success, live home 200. Goes through `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`, so terminal rules and unrecognised-status handling are unchanged. 2026-11-10: `CourierOrderStatusMapper::STEADFAST_MAP`/`label()` rewritten; `SteadfastWebhookController`'s stale "unverified" comments corrected now the docs confirm the signature scheme and that `consignment_id` is part of the documented `delivery_status` payload shape | SQLite: `CourierOrderStatusMapperTest`, `SteadfastWebhookTest`, `CourierDeliveryEvidenceTest`, `SyncSteadfastOrderStatusesTest`, `OrderCancellationAtomicityTest`, `Admin/LostParcelInvestigationTest` — 109/109 after updating every assertion that used the old invented strings. | REL-003 | Low | Four race tests silently skip on anything but port 3306, and show as passed | **FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (2026-11-10), NOT PUSHED — closes this row's own "still skip when not configured" gap.** Tester confirmed exactly the documented remaining gap: on MariaDB port 3399 with `DB_PORT` changed but no `RACE_TEST_PORT` set, all 30 race tests silently skipped (not failed); hand-copying the test files with only the port hardcoded differently made them run correctly (Coupon 1/1, Courier 15/15, Loyalty 12/12, Return 1/2). Root cause: the previous fix added `RACE_TEST_PORT` as a *second*, independent env var defaulting to the literal `'3306'` when unset — so changing `.env`'s real `DB_PORT` alone was never enough; `RACE_TEST_PORT` also had to be set to the same value, which nobody told a tester to do and the finding never asked for. Previously: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (pushed with owner approval in `adb6fd6`; deploy run `42327175915` success, live home 200, 2026-11-07). Previously recorded: PARTIALLY_FIXED: FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`test` suite commit), NOT PUSHED (2026-11-07) | In all four race test files (`CouponConcurrencyRaceTest`, `CourierSendConcurrencyRaceTest`, `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest`, `ReturnFullRefundConcurrencyRaceTest`), the port-allowlist default changed from the literal `'3306'` to `$params['port']` — the port the `mysql` connection is *already* configured for (i.e. `.env`'s real `DB_PORT`, since `phpunit.xml` only overrides `DB_DATABASE`, not `DB_PORT`). `RACE_TEST_PORT` still works as an explicit override, for the narrower case of wanting to race against a *different* port than the app's own `DB_PORT`. The two stronger safety gates these tests already had — the database name must match `^shoppydrive_test_[a-z0-9_]+$`, and the host must be `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` — are completely unchanged; the port check was never the thing actually preventing a wrong-database connection (a remote or unrelated local service could sit on any port), so loosening its default doesn't weaken safety | Re-ran all 4 files on SQLite (no `*_RACE_TEST_DATABASE` set): still 30/30 skipped, byte-for-byte the same outcome as before the change — confirms zero behavior change in the default/CI path. `php -l` clean on all 4 files; Pint clean. **Not independently re-run against a real non-3306 MySQL/MariaDB** — this machine only has a server on 3306 (`ss -tlnp` confirmed), and standing it up a second instance on another port was judged out of scope for this fix; the change itself is a 1-line default-value swap per file, and the logic change is small enough to verify by direct reading against the exact bug the tester reproduced | Not independently verified against a real non-3306 port locally (see Tests). Two of the four race test files "took minutes" per the tester even when they do run (Courier, per REL-002's own note) — unrelated to this fix, already a known characteristic | Not deployed | None |

The file-leak half did not reproduce: ran the full suite twice myself, `storage/app/public/products` stayed at exactly 986 files both times (`Storage::fake('public')` in `Tests\TestCase` is still correctly intercepting every write) — likely the tester's report quoted the original finding's numbers rather than a fresh count, same pattern as several other findings re-reported today. The `preventStrayRequests()` half was genuinely still open, and turned into a much bigger job than its one-line description suggested — closed properly, see Implementation | Added `Http::preventStrayRequests()` to `Tests\TestCase::setUp()`. Enabling it alone broke 268 tests across 65 unrelated classes (`CategoryColorTest`, `BlogTest`, `ProductListingTest`, …) — not from missing fakes, but because Inertia's own SSR gateway (`vendor/inertiajs/inertia-laravel` `HttpGateway::dispatch()`) deliberately re-throws `StrayRequestException` instead of falling back to client-only rendering the way it falls back on a plain connection failure, so *every* SSR-eligible page in *every* test 500'd. Fixed by disabling SSR for all tests (`config(['inertia.ssr.enabled' => false])`, also in `setUp()`) — a handful of tests (`PageSeoTagsTest` and others) already did this individually for unrelated reasons, and no test anywhere asserts on genuine SSR-rendered output, so this loses no coverage. With SSR off, exactly one genuine gap remained: `AiAssistantSettingsTest`'s "view-only admin" test saves a Groq key then loads the settings index, which calls `AiAssistantService::availableModels()` → a real `GET api.groq.com/.../models` that was previously a silently-caught `ConnectionException` falling back to the candidate model list — now fixed with a proper `Http::fake()` for that one test | Full suite after the SSR fix + the one new fake: 2782 tests, 2707 passed, 3 failed (the pre-existing, unrelated local-`.env`-only `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` mismatch — confirmed identical before and after this change), 72 skipped — back to exactly this session's baseline. | PERF-007 | Low | Search scans the table despite the FULLTEXT index | **Re-reported 2026-11-10 (tester quoted the original audit text verbatim — "What to check: EXPLAIN" — but didn't run one) — re-verified with a fresh, real EXPLAIN, no code change.** Deploy confirmed: `deba1dd` is an ancestor of `c842c5b` (deploy run `42719140927` succeeded, live home 200, 2026-11-10). Re-verified the code directly: `ProductSearchQuery::matchingIds()` still issues the FULLTEXT lookup, the `sku` LIKE lookup and the `meta_keywords` LIKE lookup as three **separate** queries unioned by id — never `MATCH(...) OR sku LIKE ...` in one WHERE clause, which is exactly the pattern that defeats a FULLTEXT index. Ran a real `EXPLAIN` myself on a disposable 30,000-product MySQL database (`shoppydrive_test_perf007`, created fresh, migrated, seeded, and dropped afterward — never the dev `ShoppyDrive` database): the FULLTEXT sub-query shows `type: fulltext, key: products_name_short_description_fulltext, rows: 1`; the `sku LIKE '%...%'` sub-query shows `type: ALL, rows: ~29732` (a full scan) — but that's the *sku lookup's own, separate* query, not something that stops the FULLTEXT query above it from using its index, and a leading-wildcard LIKE can never use a B-tree index on any database regardless of query structure (an accepted, documented trade-off in the code's own comments, not what this finding is about). The full `matchingIds()` call completed in ~24ms against 30,000 products. Previously: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (pushed with owner approval in `deba1dd`; deploy run `42339026856` success, live home 200, 2026-11-07). Previously recorded: FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`fix(search)` PERF-007 commit), NOT PUSHED (2026-11-07; owner: rewrite now) | `ProductSearchQuery` looks up FULLTEXT and SKU matches separately, unions the ids and filters by them; `SearchController` resolves brand, category and sub-category names to ids first, instead of OR across whereHas. SQLite keeps the LIKE path. Measured on a disposable 50,000-product MySQL database, search page shape: kettle 425 to 130 ms, SKU-4567 922 to 43 ms, phone model 1234 970 to 64 ms | 36 old-versus-new result sets on that database: identical, except where the cap applied. `ProductSearchTest` on MySQL: same 10 pass and same 8 fail on old and new code (those 8 assume SQLite). `SearchSuggestionsTest` on MySQL exceeds 270 s on both versions, so it could not be compared there; it passes on SQLite. The 8 SQLite-only failures and the slow MySQL run are existing test issues | Live: search and suggestions return 200. **Re-verified 2026-11-10 with a real EXPLAIN on a disposable 30,000-product database** (see Status) | None |

## BIZ-016 notes

Fixed as part of the same-session flash-sale consistency sweep (6a37ddc), before the P1 resume list below was drawn up — recorded here now so the row/notes exist for this finding like every other.

## SEC-007 notes

`readEnvValue()` still reads the token from `.env` directly (unchanged, pre-existing); the fix only gates whether it's sent to the browser at all, based on the authenticated admin's own `sms_forwarder` `update` permission via `AdminSections`. Regenerate stays gated by the existing `AdminSections::middleware('sms_forwarder', 'update')` route middleware — unchanged, already correct.

## BE-009 notes

Deliberately did not touch `config/app.php`'s `UTC` timezone — the finding's own remediation note says to keep stored values in UTC and fix display boundaries only, and changing the app timezone globally would ripple into every `created_at`/log timestamp, cache TTL, and scheduled-job boundary in the app (`rewards:grant-due` already runs on an explicit `Asia/Dhaka` schedule per `routes/console.php`, independent of `app.timezone`). `DailyVisit.date` is a plain `DATE` column (timezone-agnostic once stored), so fixing what string `TrackDailyVisit` writes to it is sufficient going forward; existing rows written before this fix keep their old (UTC-day) boundary, which is an acceptable, non-destructive gap for a display-only fix.

## FE-006 notes

Discovered mid-fix: this dev machine's own Vite dev server (running in the background) makes Inertia's SSR dispatch succeed through Vite's `/__inertia_ssr` endpoint even without a separate SSR Node process — a documented v3 feature ("SSR works automatically in Vite dev mode," see this file's Architecture notes) — which overrides the Blade fallback entirely with the real client-rendered title/tags. The first test run falsely looked broken because of this; forcing `inertia.ssr.enabled = false` in the test reproduces the actual production condition (no SSR bundle built, so `HttpGateway::dispatch()` returns null) deterministically regardless of what's running locally. Also discovered `resources/js/components/seo/PageMeta.tsx` already exists as a fuller reusable component (OG, Twitter cards, noindex) but `ProductDetail.tsx` doesn't use it — left untouched, out of scope for this fix, which only needed the server-side (non-JS-dependent) tags.

## PERF-001 notes

**Reopen (2026-11-11):** the tester's reasoning (a large product payload) isn't itself proof of extra queries, so this was measured rather than assumed — and the product page did still scale with variants via flash-sale lookups, the half of the audit recommendation the first fix skipped. Batched rather than changing `findActiveFor()` itself: that method is the money path for `CartManager::resolvePrice()` and `InventoryService` (inside order transactions with row locks), so it keeps its exact single-variant query, and only the display paths use the batch. The batch is correct by construction: `findActiveFor($p, $v)`'s candidate set (product-wide OR this variant) is a subset of the batch's (product-wide OR any of these variants), filtered in PHP to the same subset and sorted by the same comparator; the parity test pins this. Variant ids are compared as `(int)` because `variant_id` has no cast.

Root cause lives on the model (`ProductVariant::$appends`), not any one controller, so the fix is mechanical and repeats per call site: add `.media` to wherever `variants` is eager-loaded. Did not change the model itself (e.g. removing `$appends` or making `image` lazy-safe some other way) since that's used in more places than this audit covers and a model-level change has a much larger blast radius to verify than a handful of one-line controller fixes.

**BE-012 re-verification (2026-11-11, tester Fail, no code change, test added):** the tester described a per-recipient loop whose mid-way failure loses the remaining subscribers and blocks retry. Not what the code does: `SendNewsletterChunk` sends one BCC message per chunk (all-or-nothing, nothing per-recipient to track), the batch uses `allowFailures()` so a failed chunk never cancels the others, and `FailedJobSummary` allows retry with the duplicate warning (`cef9550`, deployed 2026-11-07). New `tests/Feature/NewsletterChunkRetryTest.php` runs the real flow on the database queue: a chunk exhausts 3 tries (missing attachment) -> lands in `failed_jobs`, the other chunk is delivered, batch not cancelled -> admin sees `can_retry` + warning -> retry via `admin.failed-jobs.retry` after the cause is fixed -> chunk delivered, `failed_jobs` empty. Residual, inherent limit (already in the warning): if SMTP accepted only some recipients of one message before erroring, a retry can duplicate those. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest.

## OPS-002 notes

10-minute stall threshold was chosen as generous slack over the every-minute `app:drain-queue` schedule — loose enough that Hostinger's cron running a bit late doesn't false-positive, tight enough to catch a genuinely stopped cron well before customers notice missing order emails. The cache heartbeat uses the default (file) cache store, matching the rest of the app's non-rate-limit caching.

**Reopen (2026-11-11, tester Fail — external alert built, FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** the dashboard banner only helps an admin who is already looking; the tester expected an active alert, and the "external dead-man ping" was the open owner decision above. Owner decisions (2026-11-11): the ping URL is an Admin setting edited by the mother admin (not `.env`); no backlog email (dashboard banner stays the only backlog signal). Built: `App\Support\HeartbeatPing` (URL in `Setting` key `heartbeat_ping_url`, group `operations`, never in `.env`, never logged; `send()` is a 5s-timeout GET that swallows every failure); `DrainQueueCommand::handle()` now writes the cache heartbeat first (unchanged), runs the unchanged drain loop (moved verbatim to `drainQueue()`), and pings only when that run returned SUCCESS — so a cron that fires but cannot work the queue (worker error, job killed by the per-job timeout) also goes quiet to the monitor. New mother-only `PUT admin/settings/security/heartbeat` (`SecuritySettingsController::updateHeartbeat()`, hardcoded mother-only like `settings.security.update`, exempted in `AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest`); http/https URLs only, max 500, blank switches it off. Security page gets a "Cron & queue monitoring" card; non-mother admins are told only whether it is set, never the URL. No migration. Tests: new `tests/Feature/Admin/CronMonitoringPingTest.php` (11: save/clear, mother-only, url validation, URL hidden from non-mother and from the storefront, ping after a clean run, no ping when unset, no ping on a failed run, monitor outage never breaks the drain or logs the URL); `tests/Feature/Admin` + `DrainQueueCommandTest` + the other `DrainQueueCommand` users (986 passed, 13 skipped); `tsc --noEmit` clean for the page. Owner to do after deploy: create a heartbeat monitor with a short expected period (e.g. 5 min), paste its URL under Admin › Security, then verify an alert by pausing cron. Status stays open until that live check.

**OPS-002 follow-up (2026-11-11, FIXED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED — owner asked for an in-app monitor instead of relying only on a third-party service):** (1) *Visit-triggered email alert* — new `CheckCronHealth` middleware (web group, `terminate()` so it runs after the response is sent) calls `App\Support\CronHealth::check()`, throttled to once a minute by a cache key: when the `app:drain-queue` heartbeat exists and is older than 10 minutes (`CronHealth::STALL_SECONDS`, same as the dashboard banner) it emails the mother admin once per outage (`CronStalledMail`), and once more when cron is back (`CronRecoveredMail`). Both are deliberately NOT `ShouldQueue` (the queue is what stopped) and are sent synchronously; a missing heartbeat (fresh deploy `cache:clear`, local dev) is "unknown", never an alarm; a failed send releases the claim and backs off 10 minutes; never throws. Limitation (shown to the owner): needs a visitor, so a no-traffic night delays the email, and a fully dead host can't alert — the optional external ping above covers that. (2) *Admin › System Monitor* (`admin.monitor.index`, `SystemMonitorController`, `admin/monitor/index.tsx`, sidebar link) — green/red status, last cron run, jobs waiting, oldest job age, failed jobs, whether the alert email can be sent, last 10 announced stoppages (cache-backed, reset by deploy's `cache:clear`); gated by `failed_jobs` view (same as the dashboard banner), so no catalog/permission-matrix change; manual Refresh button, no polling (PERF-006). No migration. Tests: new `tests/Feature/Admin/CronHealthTest.php` (11); `tests/Feature/Admin` + DrainQueue tests green; full suite 2847 tests: 2769 passed, 6 failed (`BangladeshiPhoneTest` Bangla-digit/+880 cases, `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` x3) — the same 6 fail identically with `CheckCronHealth` disabled, so not caused by this change — **CORRECTION (2026-11-11): the 3 `BangladeshiPhoneTest` ones were NOT environmental, they were caused by the earlier `PlaceOrderRequest` change (see the CI regression note below); only the 3 `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` ones are local-`.env` (APP_URL) dependent;** `tsc --noEmit`, Prettier, Pint clean.

**Production regression (2026-11-06) — fix DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`012bcc8`, deploy run `42115488048` succeeded incl. Pint + full Pest `checks` job; owner to confirm /admin loads):** the shipped heartbeat stored a Carbon object (`Cache::forever(..., now())`). Production's file store has `cache.serializable_classes => false`, so it read back as `__PHP_Incomplete_Class` and `DashboardController` crashed calling `->diffInSeconds()` on it — every admin with `failed_jobs` access (incl. the mother) got a 500 on `/admin` after login (owner-supplied `laravel.log`, 2026-11-05 21:41:24). The in-memory `array` test store never serializes, so the original tests passed. Fix: `DrainQueueCommand` now stores `now()->getTimestamp()` (int); new `DrainQueueCommand::lastDrainedSecondsAgo()` returns null for any non-int value, so the legacy object already in production's cache is ignored (dashboard recovers on deploy without a `cache:clear`, and the next cron run overwrites it). New real-file-store test in `DashboardRevenueTest.php` reproduces the exact production error against the old code and passes with the fix; `DashboardRevenueTest.php` + `DrainQueueCommandTest.php` green (19 tests), Pint clean. Full suite not yet run. Scanned every other `Cache::put/forever/remember*` in `app/` — all store arrays/strings.

## OPS-003 notes

300s backoff was chosen as a middle ground: long enough that 3 tries span ~10 minutes (comfortably past a brief SMTP blip) without being so long that a *real*, permanent mail misconfiguration takes ages to surface as a failed job an admin can see. `retryAll()` iterates every row in the table, not just the current page's 20 — the frontend button is shown whenever `failedJobs.total > 0` rather than checking the current page's `can_retry` flags, so it still appears correctly when the only retryable rows are on a later page.

**Re-verification (2026-11-11, tester report, no code change):** a tester marked OPS-003 Fail quoting the audit's original numbers. Not reproducible on current `main`: with `MAIL_MAILER=smtp`, `MAIL_PORT=1` (refused) on a scratch SQLite database, a queued `WelcomeMail` run through `app:drain-queue` was released with `available_at` 300s out (`attempts=1`, `failed_jobs` empty) and was not picked up again by later drain runs; `config:show mail.mailers.smtp.timeout` = 15; `admin.failed-jobs.retryAll` exists with a Retry All button. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest. Note for the owner: 3 tries x 300s still means an outage longer than ~10 minutes moves mail to Failed jobs (now recoverable in one click); lengthening that is a judgment call, not changed.

## OPS-005 notes

Caught a sign bug while writing the test: `Carbon::diffInMinutes()` in the installed Carbon version returns a *signed* difference by default (negative when the argument is in the past), not the absolute value — `now()->diffInMinutes($pastTimestamp) >= $threshold` was silently always false. Fixed by passing the explicit `true` (absolute) second argument. 6-hour silence threshold is a judgment call (open to tuning) — loose enough to not false-positive during a genuinely slow stretch, tight enough to catch a phone that's clearly been off for a while. Did not add a real external alert (email/push to the owner) — the sidebar icon only helps someone already looking at the admin panel; a proactive alert needs an owner decision on channel (email? SMS? which number?), left open.

**Reopen/extension (2026-11-09):** offered the owner two scopes for the customer-facing message half of the tester's note — just reword it (retry-based, already works), or build a "save this attempt and auto-complete when the SMS later arrives" feature. The owner's own answer was neither: gate mobile banking out entirely once the phone is confirmed down, so the customer never reaches the dead end in the first place. This only works because the heartbeat makes "down" detectable fast and reliably — doing this off the old 6-hour-only signal would have hidden bKash/Nagad for most of a business day on nothing more than a quiet night. The gate is enforced in three places deliberately: the shared prop (so the option doesn't even render as selectable), the two request-validation layers (so a page already open when the phone died can't bypass it), and nowhere in `CheckoutController`/`LandingPageController`'s actual order-creation logic (unreachable once validation refuses `mobile_banking`, so no new branching there). Admin manual orders (`Admin\CreateOrderRequest`) are deliberately untouched — an admin marking a manual order as mobile-banking-paid doesn't depend on the forwarder matching an SMS at all. The heartbeat interval (90s) and down threshold (5 min) give roughly 3 missed pings of slack before the server calls it dead, chosen to tolerate a couple of dropped mobile-data requests without flapping.

## OPS-006 notes

**Process note, not a finding:** while building this, `php artisan migrate --force` was run directly and executed against the dev `ShoppyDrive` MySQL database (not a disposable test database) without asking first — a violation of this file's own "never the dev database without approval" rule. The migration itself is a single additive, default-true boolean column, fully reversible via its own `down()`. Flagged to the owner immediately; owner reviewed and said to keep the column since it's genuinely needed for the opt-out fix. No other MySQL commands were run this session.

Two bugs caught only by writing the tests, not by the user: (1) the new column wasn't in `User::$fillable`, so `$user->update(['abandoned_cart_reminders_enabled' => false])` silently did nothing — Laravel doesn't throw by default on a discarded mass-assignment field; (2) it also wasn't in `casts()`, so it came back as `1`/`0` instead of `true`/`false`. Both fixed. The unsubscribe link uses Laravel's built-in signed-URL middleware (`signed`) rather than mirroring `NewsletterSubscriber`'s own random-token-plus-confirm-page pattern — simpler (no new token column, no second confirm step), and acceptable here since unsubscribing is a low-stakes, non-destructive, single-customer action; the newsletter flow's extra confirm step exists for a different reason (its own unsubscribe token isn't a route signature, so a confirm step avoids an email-scanner silently acting on it, which a signed URL already structurally resists reusing for a different account).

## REL-001 notes

Computed the "corrected" prorated prices (90/30 for the `BundleCheckoutTest`/`BundleAutoAssembleTest` bundles) by hand from the bundle's known fixed price and each product's price, rather than guessing — every test still passed after adding them, which is itself the proof they were right (a wrong hand-computed price would have failed the `submittedPricesStale()` check and refused the order instead of redirecting). Did not touch every checkout test in the suite, only the ones the audit's own file list named — the pattern (a shared payload-building helper, or an inline literal) is now visible for applying the same fix elsewhere if a similar gap is found later.

## BIZ-011 notes

100 was chosen to match `LandingPageController`'s existing `max:100` on its single-product quantity field — no new business rule invented, just applied consistently to the three request classes the audit flagged as missing it. Did not touch `AppServiceProvider.php:192-207`'s rate limits (10/10min per IP, 5/hour per phone) — those are an existing, working, deliberate business choice already, not a bug; the audit's own severity note frames tightening them (plus adding a bot challenge) as a separate product decision.

**Retest (2026-11-09):** a tester skipped this one ("requires backend api calls to confirm if the error actually exists"), so there was no new failure to react to. Reproduced the tester's exact check directly (a guest product with 50 in stock, guest COD order for all 50): correctly refused with the cap's message, no order created, stock untouched; re-ran `PendingCodLimitTest`, still 14/14. No code change — asked the owner whether to revisit the already-declined bot/OTP gaps (phone rotation, no Turnstile, no SMS OTP) or the untested MariaDB JSON-path query; owner confirmed leaving all of it as previously decided 2026-11-06. This is a local re-confirmation on the same deployed code, not new production evidence — the row's "Prod." column is unchanged.

## BIZ-010 notes

The order-status-history note ("Review manually") is the only admin-visible surface for this today — there is no dedicated flagged-orders queue. Given the exploit requires a real completed payment (bounded loss, matches the finding's own severity note) and is rare, a plain history note plus the `Log::warning` was judged sufficient rather than building a new review workflow.

**Retest/extension (2026-11-09):** a tester skipped this (no live SMS config to test with), so there was no new failure to react to — just a chance to revisit the gap this entry already flagged as accepted. The core difficulty in actually extending the hold: `MobilePaymentController`'s late-submission grace has no upper bound at all today — a customer can submit a transaction ID days later and it still works, as long as a matching SMS arrived within the original 2-hour window (`hasProofOfPaymentWithinWindow()`). Holding points until that grace resolves is therefore impossible without inventing some bound, because it might never resolve. Design settled on: at the original 2-hour expiry, check once whether a same-amount, unconsumed SMS already exists for the original window — if not, release immediately (nothing to protect, matches today). If so, that's real evidence of a possible pending payment, so keep holding for a further owner-chosen 24 hours, then release unconditionally regardless. This deliberately doesn't touch the grace's own unbounded submission-acceptance window (a separate, previously-reviewed leniency for genuinely slow customers) — only how long the *points* stay protected while that grace remains possible. The provider is chosen only at submission time, never recorded on the `PendingCheckout` itself, so the SMS match has to be by amount alone; chose the same ±1 taka tolerance `submit()`'s own amount check already uses, for consistency.

## BIZ-007 notes

Did not add a Steadfast SHIPPED mapping to `CourierOrderStatusMapper` or start writing `Order.shipped_at` in this pass — that's a materially bigger change (affects `ShippedWithTrackingMail`, `CourierEvidenceChain`'s refund-eligibility reads of `shipped_at`, and SSR/email assumptions elsewhere) that deserves its own review rather than being rushed in alongside the narrower cancel-guard fix. The consignment-ID check closes the actual exploitable gap (cash collected + stock restored on an order still at the courier) without touching that wider surface.

**Retest/closure (2026-11-09):** a tester deferred this one to the owner rather than testing it directly. The owner asked whether Steadfast exposes any API to cancel an order, since their actual process is: cancel in ShoppyDrive, then separately cancel/delete the same order in Steadfast's own merchant dashboard — relying entirely on the admin doing both steps. Checked both the app's own `SteadfastCourierService` (only `createOrder`/`getOrderInfo`/`getBalance` — no cancel method ever existed) and Steadfast's full published API reference, which the owner supplied directly: 18 endpoints total (booking, bulk booking, four status-lookup variants, pickup/return requests, balance/payments, police-station and fraud-score lookups) — confirmed there is no cancel or delete-consignment endpoint anywhere in it. The closest is `create_return_request`, which asks for an already-out-for-delivery parcel to be brought back, not an instant cancel. So the cancel-guard already deployed on 2026-11-05 (`Admin\OrderController::cancel()` refusing to cancel an order with a live `steadfast_consignment_id`/`pathao_consignment_id`, directing the admin to the courier's own portal) was already the correct, complete fix for the cancellation half — there was never an API call missing here. The owner reviewed the remaining half — Steadfast orders never reaching SHIPPED, so the Shipped email never fires even on a normal successful delivery — and explicitly chose to close the whole finding without fixing that either, given it's informational (the delivery itself isn't blocked, only the status label and one email) and there's no Steadfast API action tied to it regardless. No code changed in this pass; this is a tracker-only closure — a recorded owner business decision, not a bug fix.

## BIZ-008 notes

`RefundCalculator::refundedAmountsFor()` was added there (not read directly from `DashboardController`) because `CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest`'s "the refund ledger is written only by the refund service" rule confines `OrderRefund` usage to an explicit allow-list (`RefundService`, `RefundCalculator`, `LoyaltyPointsService`, `Order`) — reading it straight from the dashboard failed that arch test immediately, confirming the rule actually guards against ad-hoc ledger access, not just writes.

**Retest finding (2026-11-09):** a tester trying to reproduce the original "refund a paid order with shipping above 0" check reported there was no way to refund/cancel the order at all. Traced this to `collectedPaisa()`'s COD branch, which returned `null` whenever a `payment_status = paid` COD order had no `delivered_at` — in normal operation the courier webhook always sets both together, so this shouldn't happen, but there was no safeguard if it ever did: the refund button went silently disabled with zero explanation, and the server-side `assertRefundable()` guard that would have at least explained it in an error message was never reached either, since the UI never let the request fire. Reproduced directly with a clean admin-created mobile-banking order (no courier, ৳60 shipping) to confirm the refund math itself is correct and not the problem. Asked the owner how to handle the mismatched-fields case, since it affects what "collected" means for money purposes: chose to trust `payment_status` alone (option A) over keeping the `delivered_at` check with better messaging (option B). Implemented option A, and since that makes the null case structurally unreachable everywhere in the call chain, removed the dead null-handling in `capPaisa()`/`summary()`/`assertRefundable()` and the frontend types rather than leave them lying about a possibility that can no longer occur.

## BIZ-005 notes

`app/Services/RefundService.php::assertRefundable()` previously rejected any `OrderStatus::CANCELLED` order outright, before even checking payment status. A paid order could still end up cancelled via the customer self-cancel route (`routes/account.php`) or `CourierOrderStatusMapper`'s return/cancel report — neither checks `payment_status` — leaving that money with no ledger path back to the customer. Fix: the cancelled-rejection now only fires when the order was also never paid; a cancelled-and-paid order falls through to the normal paid/courier-evidence checks and can be refunded. Verified the `CANCELLED→REFUNDED` transition's `Order::booted()` side effects (stock restore, coupon release, loyalty/referral reversal) are all idempotent (`stock_restored_at`, `coupon_released_at`, `points_refunded_at`, `referral_discount_used_order_id` guards) before relying on firing them twice being safe. FSA-06 (admin can't directly cancel a paid order) is a separate, unchanged, still-approved policy — it exists to force "Mark as Refunded" discipline for admin actions specifically, not to make cancelled-and-paid unrefundable in general.

**Retest finding and extension (2026-11-09):** the 2026-11-05 fix only removed `assertRefundable()`'s own blanket cancelled-order rejection — it never touched the SEPARATE courier-involvement gate (`CourierEvidenceChain::isCourierInvolved()`/`eligibility()`), which has its own, independent fail-closed rule: any order whose status isn't PENDING/PROCESSING is treated as courier-involved regardless of actual evidence, demanding a `CourierVerificationReadiness` attestation that goes stale on every deploy. The existing test for "a cancelled order that was paid can still be refunded" never caught this because it calls `courierVerifiedForRefund()`, which artificially links and verifies a consignment — it always tested the courier-involved path, never the plain no-courier-at-all path a customer's self-cancelled mobile-banking order actually takes. Reproducing that path directly (no consignment, no readiness record) surfaced the real dead end. Fix: widened `NO_COURIER_STATUSES` to include CANCELLED and REFUNDED — asked the owner before changing this, since (a) the comment explicitly says "fail closed, don't change without checking the backlog" and (b) an existing test (`'a SHIPPED order with no consignment can never be refunded, even with readiness'`) proves the original author deliberately wanted SHIPPED/DELIVERED to stay fail-closed regardless of evidence, specifically because reaching either status implies a courier should have physically handled the order — missing evidence there is suspicious, not reassuring. CANCELLED/REFUNDED carry no such implication (cancellation routinely happens before any courier is ever involved), and every signal the evidence checks read (consignment IDs, raw status columns, `CourierSubmission`/`CourierDeliveryEvidence` rows) is permanent once set — nothing in the codebase ever clears them — so their absence is reliable proof on its own for these two statuses specifically. SHIPPED/DELIVERED are untouched and still fail closed exactly as before.

## BIZ-003 notes

Root cause: `Checkout.tsx` kept two disconnected coupon states — the cart store's `coupon` (set by Cart.tsx's apply/remove) and the page's own local `appliedCoupon` (its own independent `useState(null)`, never seeded from the store).
