A generic "Discount" row read the store's discount for display; a separate "Coupon (CODE)" row read the local state; only the local state's code was ever submitted. A guest who applied a coupon on `/cart` saw the discounted total at checkout (from the store) but was charged full price (the local state, and thus the submission, never had it). A logged-in customer re-applying at checkout got both rows simultaneously — double-subtracted. Fix: `appliedCoupon` now seeds from the store on mount (`buyNowItem` excluded, matching the old `discount = buyNowItem ? 0 : …` exclusion) and every apply/remove syncs back to the store; the redundant "Discount" row/variable was removed entirely. No PHP changed. **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available; recommend a manual check (apply a coupon as guest on `/cart`, confirm `/checkout` shows one discount line matching the charge) before relying on this fully.

## BIZ-001 notes

Root cause: `CheckoutController::store()` called `syncUserCart()`/`syncSessionCart()` (→ `CartManager::replaceItems()`) *before* `revalidatePrices()`. `replaceItems()` always overwrites every item's stored price to the current resolved price, regardless of what the client submits (items never even included a price field before this fix) — so by the time `revalidatePrices()` ran, it was comparing the current price against itself and could never detect a change. A product price edited (or a flash sale starting/ending) between checkout page load and submission was silently charged at the new price with no warning. Fix: `Checkout.tsx` now submits each line's displayed `price`; `PlaceOrderRequest` validates it (`nullable|numeric|min:0` — optional, so every other order-creation path that never sends it is unaffected); new `CartManager::submittedPricesStale()` compares it against the live resolved price *before* the sync runs, refusing with the same "Some prices in your cart have changed" message if they disagree. The submitted price is never used to decide the charge — only whether to refuse before anything is written; `resolvePrice()` still decides the actual charge, exactly as before. Rewrote `FlashSalePricingTest`'s `'a client-submitted price field is ignored'` test: previously a mismatched client price was silently dropped and the order still succeeded at the correct price; now it's refused outright (safer — the scenario is indistinguishable from legitimate staleness), so the test now asserts refusal instead of silent correction, plus a new test for the stale-then-retry-at-corrected-price flow.

**Retest finding (2026-11-09):** the fix above stopped the overcharge, but a tester found the "try again" the error message asks for never actually worked. Root cause: `Checkout.tsx` never reads the server's `cart` prop for what to show or submit — it reads its own zustand `cartStore`, which only gets the item's price at add-to-cart time and otherwise never refreshes. `submittedPricesStale()` returning early meant `replaceItems()` never ran, so the cart's stored price in the database was *also* never corrected — unlike the separate `revalidatePrices()` path (run when no `items` are submitted at all), which already did persist the correction, just not somewhere the client would ever see it. Net effect: a customer who hit this got stuck resubmitting the identical stale price indefinitely, with no path to actually complete the order short of manually removing and re-adding the item to their cart. Fix: sync the cart unconditionally (staleness is still decided from the *pre-sync* submitted values, so BIZ-001's original fix is untouched), and return the corrected cart as `priceUpdates` in the flash data; `Checkout.tsx` adopts it into its cart store on mount, so the order summary updates immediately and a second "Place Order" click succeeds at the real price.

## BIZ-002 notes

Root cause: `Checkout.tsx`'s submitted items never included `bundle_id`/`bundle_unit_quantity` at all, even though `PlaceOrderRequest`, `CartManager::replaceItems()`/`normalizePayloads()`, and `BundleService` already fully supported both (confirmed via the pre-existing `tests/Feature/BundleCheckoutTest.php`, which passed against the backend alone before any frontend change). Sync stripped the bundle grouping from every submission, `BundleService::reconcile()`/`autoAssemble()` re-detected "a price changed" on every attempt, and the customer could never get past "Some prices have changed." Fix: added both fields to the submitted payload, the same `items.map()` BIZ-001 also touches. Added one new test combining both fixes' fields (`price` + `bundle_id` + `bundle_unit_quantity` together) to prove they don't interact badly — a bundled line's submitted price must equal the *prorated bundle price*, not the plain product price, or BIZ-001's stale-price check would incorrectly refuse a correct bundle checkout.

**Retest finding (2026-11-09):** a tester described a bundle that can "never be checked out" when a component's quantity is set above the product's actual stock (e.g. the recipe needs 10, only 5 exist). Reproduced directly with the exact payload `BundleCard.tsx`'s "Add Bundle" button would send (`bundle_unit_quantity` = the full recipe quantity, price = the live prorated price) — checkout already handles this safely: `collectCartIssues()` → `InventoryService::assertAvailable()` refuses with "Only 5 unit(s) of ... left in stock," not the generic "Some prices have changed," and no order is ever created. So the actual gap wasn't checkout at all — it was that nothing ever stopped an admin from creating or saving that unfulfillable recipe in the first place: `ProductBundleRequest`'s `items.*.quantity` rule was only `required|integer|min:1`, with no ceiling, and the admin UI's search/edit screens never even fetched stock numbers to show. Fix: a new closure rule on `items.*.quantity` compares it against the actual stock for that exact (product, variant) pair — reusing `InventoryService::assertAvailable()`'s own convention that a variant-less pick of a product that has variants has no stock of its own, so that case is refused too, at any quantity. Existing bundles saved before this fix aren't retroactively re-validated (a migration wasn't considered necessary — checkout already fails them safely), only new creates/edits.

## BIZ-006 notes

Root cause: nothing checked stock before staging a `PendingCheckout` for mobile banking (cart checkout's `CheckoutController::initiateMobileBanking()`, or the landing-page equivalent in `LandingPageController::placeOrder()`) — `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` only ever runs once the customer has already paid and submitted their TrxID (`MobilePaymentController::submit()` / `LandingPageController::submitPayment()`). An out-of-stock item still showed bKash/Nagad payment instructions; money was taken with no way to fulfill the order. Fix: new `InventoryService::assertAvailable()` — deliberately read-only and unlocked (no order/transaction exists yet to lock within) — mirrors `decrementForOrderItem()`'s ordinary-stock and flash-sale-cap checks without writing anything, called right before each of the two `PendingCheckout::create()` call sites. This can't close the race entirely (stock can still run out between this check and actual payment minutes later) — `decrementForOrderItem()` remains the one authoritative, locked check at real order creation — but it closes the common, avoidable case the finding describes. The audit's secondary suggestions ("share the grace logic" between cart and landing-page flows, "add a pending-checkout to the SMS reconciliation screen") were not done — out of scope for this pass, the core "check stock before showing payment instructions" fix was prioritized.

## BIZ-004 notes

Root cause: `Admin\IncompleteOrderController::placeOrder()` created `OrderItem`s directly, with no call to `InventoryService` at all — the one order-creation path (COD, ZiniPay, mobile banking, landing page, admin manual order all call `decrementForOrderItem()`; this didn't). Stock was never actually taken, yet later cancelling the resulting order still ran `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` via `Order::booted()`, which restocked quantities that had never been decremented — inflating stock above its pre-conversion count. Fix: added `InventoryService $inventory` as a method-injected dependency and call `decrementForOrderItem()` per item inside the existing transaction, wrapped in the same `try`/`catch (InsufficientStockException)` → `back()->withErrors(['items' => …])` pattern `Admin\OrderController`'s manual-order flow already uses. Also added `VariantBelongsToProduct` to `ConvertIncompleteOrderRequest`'s `items.*.variant_id` rule (previously missing — the audit's "validate the variant owner" note; this class of bug is already closed everywhere else, e.g. FSA-01). Existing tests in `tests/Feature/Admin/IncompleteOrderManagementTest.php` relied on `ProductFactory`'s default `stock_quantity` (`fake()->numberBetween(0, 500)`) — harmless when stock was never checked, but a ~0.4%-per-test flake risk now that it is — so every `Product::factory()->create()` call in that file now sets an explicit, sufficient `stock_quantity`.

## BIZ-013 notes

Root cause: `CheckoutController` refuses with `withErrors(['coupon_code' => …])` or `withErrors(['order_token' => …])` in three places, none paired with the `error` session flash. Checkout.tsx's `flash?.error` banner is the only thing on this page that renders a refusal that isn't tied to a named shipping-address input — `form.setError()` sets the Inertia errors bag, but nothing reads `errors.coupon_code`/`errors.order_token` for display (the same gap BIZ-001/BIZ-006 already fixed for `items`). A coupon that expired/was deactivated between being applied and Place Order was silently rejected: the submit button just reset with the stale discount still showing. Fix: added the same `.with('error', $message)` to all three refusals. Also: Checkout.tsx's `onError` handler now calls `applyCoupon(null)` (the BIZ-003 sync wrapper) when `coupon_code` is the rejected key, so the UI stops showing a discount the server just refused. The `order_token` lock-timeout path (two near-simultaneous submissions of the same token) has no automated test — it needs a second process genuinely holding the `Cache::lock()`, not practically simulable in a single-process test run.

## SEC-021 notes

Root cause: Inertia keeps every visited page's data in `window.history.state` for its own back/forward handling — normally invisible to the user, but on a shared PC, pressing Back after a customer or admin logs out re-shows that stored state (orders, addresses, whatever the page last rendered) with no server round-trip, since there's nothing server-side to intercept a client-side history pop. Confirmed in the audit's own browser evidence. Inertia's own answer is history encryption, which has two independent pieces, both previously missing: (1) opt in to encryption for the sensitive route groups — otherwise the stored state is plain JSON regardless of anything else — and (2) rotate the encryption key on logout, so even a page visited *during* the session that's about to end becomes unreadable once it's over, not just future ones. Implementation note: the first attempt wired (2) via an `Illuminate\Auth\Events\Logout` listener in `AppServiceProvider` — wrong, because that event fires *during* `Auth::guard(...)->logout()`, which both logout paths call *before* `$request->session()->invalidate()`, and `invalidate()` flushes all session attributes, silently destroying whatever the listener had just written. Moved to firing after invalidate instead: a new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (customer, bound via Fortify's `LogoutResponse` contract, builds its response after `AuthenticatedSessionController::destroy()`'s own invalidate call) and a direct call in `AdminAuthController::logout()` (after its own invalidate/regenerateToken calls). **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available; the test suite proves the session flag and middleware wiring are correct, but an actual back-button check would add confidence. This gap is exactly how the follow-up bug below reached the user instead of being caught here.

**Follow-up (`7addfdf`):** owner reported the admin flash-sales page stuck "loading" and a console error, `Uncaught (in promise) Error: Unable to encrypt history`. Root cause: history encryption needs `window.crypto.subtle`, a secure-context-only API (HTTPS, or the `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` exception) — the owner's dev server is reached over plain HTTP via a LAN IP (`vite.config.ts`'s `192.168.0.111:8001`), which is neither. Confirmed by reading `node_modules/@inertiajs/core/dist/index.js`: `encryptHistory()` calls `createKey()`, which returns `null` (not a throw, just a `console.warn`) when `crypto.subtle` is undefined — but `encryptHistory()` itself then explicitly throws `"Unable to encrypt history"` on that null key, as an uncaught promise rejection inside the visit-completion chain, which is why the *page* got stuck, not just the console. Fix: new `ConditionallyEncryptHistory` middleware only calls `Inertia::encryptHistory()` when `$request->secure()`. `Inertia::clearHistory()` (the logout half) was confirmed separately *not* to touch any crypto API client-side, so it needed no equivalent gating.

Two implementation gotchas hit while fixing this, both worth keeping in mind:
- Registering the fix under the *same* alias name inertia-laravel uses (`inertia.encrypt`) doesn't override it — tried first, verified broken via `app('router')->getMiddleware()['inertia.encrypt']` still resolving to the package's own class. The package's service provider registers that alias from its own `boot()`, which runs after `bootstrap/app.php`'s middleware configuration and wins the conflict. Used a different alias name (`history.encrypt`) instead, referenced directly in `routes/account.php`/`routes/admin.php`.
- Testing "a secure request" by passing `withServerVariables(['HTTPS' => 'on'])` to a plain `route()`-generated `http://` URL doesn't work — confirmed via `Log::debug()` inside the middleware that `$request->secure()` still read `false`. Symfony's `Request::create()` derives `HTTPS` from the URL's own scheme and explicitly `unset()`s whatever was passed in `$server` if the URL isn't `https://` (`vendor/symfony/http-foundation/Request.php`, the `create()` method). The URL itself has to be `https://` (`str_replace('http://', 'https://', route(...))`) for the simulated request to actually read as secure.

## BIZ-029 notes

**Retest (2026-11-09):** a tester skipped this ("cant find ways to return a product") — not a bug, but how the feature is designed: the storefront's "Return Items" button only shows once an order's status is `delivered` (`OrderDetail.tsx`'s `canRequestReturn = order.status === 'delivered'`), and their test account had no order in that state. No new evidence of a problem. Re-confirmed the original fix directly: `git log` shows `5762cbb` (the fix itself) is still the last commit to touch `resources/js/pages/admin/returns/index.tsx`, and the code reads exactly as the original entry describes — `handleReject()` returns before calling `router.patch()` when `prompt()` returns `null` (Cancel). Backend `ReturnRequestTest.php` (37/37) still passes, including the reject+email and double-reject-sends-one-email cases. No code change.

## BIZ-015 notes

**Retest and reopened scope (2026-11-09):** a tester confirmed the per-customer limit enforcement itself works, but flagged that the guest-facing rejection message was unhelpful — "This coupon isn't available for this order" instead of a real reason. That vagueness was never an oversight: `routes/commerce.php`'s `/coupon/validate` deliberately special-cased it with a comment explaining why — an anonymous guest who knows a coupon code could otherwise probe arbitrary phone numbers against this endpoint and learn which ones have already redeemed it, a privacy/enumeration leak (the same class of concern SEC-012 separately closed for the coupon-record-exposure angle). Asked the owner directly, since this is a real security-vs-clarity tradeoff, not a plain bug: keep the protection, or give guests the same specific message logged-in customers already get. Owner chose clarity. `Coupon::eligibilityError()` already accepted an optional `$guestPhone` parameter and already built the specific "already used the maximum number of times" message from `redemptionCount($couponId, $userId, $guestPhone)` — it just was never called with `$guestPhone` from the guest preview path, which short-circuited to a generic message before reaching it. The fix only removes that short-circuit and threads `$guestPhone` through; `eligibilityError()` itself is unchanged. Net effect, now that this is live: a coupon code alone is enough to learn whether a given phone number has used it, by trying `/coupon/validate` as a guest with that phone — an intentional, owner-approved tradeoff, not an oversight to fix later.

## BIZ-017 notes

**Retest (2026-11-09):** a tester reported "admin converting an incomplete order doesn't add any delivery charge" and "no option to add more product." Investigated both directly rather than assuming: `ConvertIncompleteOrderRequest` requires `city`/`state` (`required`, not `nullable`), so the backend refuses submission without them; `Setting::shippingRateFor(null, null)` would default to the outside-Dhaka rate anyway (never literally ৳0) even if they were somehow blank. `IncompleteOrderConversionPricingTest`'s `'the preview shows the same line prices and delivery charge the order will use'` test already asserts `shipping_cost` is greater than 0 and that the created order's `shipping_cost` matches the preview exactly — re-ran it, still passes. Separately, `convert.tsx` already has a "Search products to add…" box (in the Products card, calling `admin.orders.incomplete.products.search`) that lets the admin add more lines before converting — not conditionally hidden, always rendered. Could not reproduce either complaint. Owner confirmed: mark confirmed-working, no code change — most likely the tester didn't fill in district/city before judging the Delivery line, or didn't scroll to the Products card.

## BIZ-033 notes

**Retest (2026-11-09):** a tester reported a Fail different from the original finding: approving a review in Admin broke that specific product's detail page ("something went wrong") every time afterward. Investigated directly rather than assuming: reproduced the real end-to-end flow twice via the actual routes — (1) a plain review with no order, (2) a verified-purchase review that also exercises `LoyaltyPointsService::grantReviewBonus()`'s full path (locking the review and user, writing the `ReferralPointTransaction`, `points_awarded_at`) — both with `withoutExceptionHandling()` so nothing could be silently swallowed. Neither reproduction crashed the approve action or the subsequent product page load. Also checked the dev server's `storage/logs/browser.log` around the tester's reported time for a matching client-side error; found none. Asked the owner for more specifics (exact error text/stack trace, or which product) to narrow it further; the owner instead reported the tester retested and it's now fine. Left as unresolved/transient (most likely a stale dev build or HMR artifact, not a code defect) — no code change made, since nothing reproducible was found to fix. If this recurs, the next useful step is the actual `storage/logs/laravel.log` entry from the moment it happens, or the specific product's id/slug.

## BIZ-030 notes

Pure frontend label fix, no backend change — `order.payment_status` was already shared to this page. `OrderConfirmation.tsx` now shows "Total Paid" only when actually paid, "Total Refunded" when refunded, and "Total Due on Delivery" for an unpaid COD order (previously always "Total Paid" regardless, risking courier-door disputes when a COD customer believed they'd already paid). Copy/display-only change — no test added, per CLAUDE.md's exemption for pure copy/styling changes.

## SEC-003 notes

**Follow-up, 2026-11-09 (tester reported FAIL).** The `1d03b77` fix was correct but incomplete, because it only covered `Settings\ProfileController::update()`. Reproduced with failing tests before changing code:

- Fortify's `PUT /user/profile-information` was still enabled, and no frontend code used it. `UpdateUserProfileInformation` changed the email and then called `sendEmailVerificationNotification()`, which does nothing during the 60-second resend cooldown, so a code mailed to the old address stayed valid for the new one.
- The same route changed a verified account's email without the code to the current address, bypassing SEC-009.
- Nothing tied a code to the address it was sent to, so safety depended on every write path remembering to clear it.
- Two races existed: verification could read the row before an email change committed, and an email change could overwrite nothing when a verification landed after it loaded the row (an in-memory null is not dirty), leaving the new address verified.

Changes: removed `Features::updateProfileInformation()`, `UpdateUserProfileInformation`, its binding and its limiter entry. `User::hashEmailVerificationCode()` hashes the code with the address (sha256 first because bcrypt ignores input past 72 bytes). `User::consumeEmailVerificationCode()` checks and writes under a row lock. `User::changeEmailTo()` is the single locked email-change path, used by both `ProfileController` branches. No migration. Codes already in flight when this deploys stop working and need a resend.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/Auth/EmailVerificationCodeAddressBindingTest.php` (7 tests; the 3 attack tests failed before the fix). `tests/Feature/Auth`, `tests/Feature/Settings`, `LoyaltyPointsTest` and `OrderNotificationTest`: 160/160 on SQLite. Fixtures that hard-coded the old hash format were updated. Full suite run twice on SQLite: 2,570 passed, 72 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed; Pint and `tsc` pass. Not run: MySQL or real concurrent requests, so the row locks are covered only by sequential stale-read tests. An account verified through this hole before the fix stays verified and cannot be told apart from a normal one.

Root cause confirmed by reading `EmailVerificationCodeController::store()`: the submitted code is checked only against `$user->email_verification_code` (a hash, bound to the user ROW, not to any specific email address). `Settings\ProfileController::update()` nulled `email_verified_at` on an email change but left the pending code fields untouched, so a code issued for the OLD address stayed valid and could be replayed against the user's row after it now points at a different (unproven) address. Fix is the minimal, root-cause one: clear all three code fields (`email_verification_code`, `_expires_at`, `_sent_at`) in the same `isDirty('email')` branch that already clears `email_verified_at`. Verified this actually closes the exploit, not just the DB-state symptom: the test captures a genuine code via a faked mail, changes the email, then submits that exact code and confirms it's rejected (`assertSessionHasErrors('code')`), not merely that the column reads null.

## SEC-002 notes

`SettingController::settingsProp()` called `Setting::all()` for every one of the six settings pages — the per-page `admin.can:<section>,view` middleware controls who can *open* a page, but every page that successfully opened received the entire settings table regardless, including Pathao/Steadfast credentials and Google/Facebook OAuth secrets. The authorization half of this was already correct and already had the right shape to reuse: `FIELD_GROUPS` (used by `authorizeFieldGroups()` to gate `update()` per field group) already lists exactly the fields each of 5 pages needs. Added one more constant, `GENERAL_FIELDS`, for the 6th page (`edit()`, the one not in `FIELD_GROUPS` because its fields fall back to the generic `settings` permission) — derived by reading exactly which `settings.*` keys `edit.tsx` references, the same method used for each other page. `settingsProp()` now takes that list and queries `Setting::whereIn('key', $keys)` instead of `all()`. No page's own fields were affected — confirmed by the full existing settings-pages test suite (108 tests) passing unchanged, plus the new test proving the leak itself is closed (a limited admin's about-page response no longer contains the courier/OAuth secrets).

## SEC-006 notes

Same bug FSA-03 already fixed once (the order-confirmation page sent the raw model) had two more instances: `OrderTrackingController::show()` (reachable by anyone who has an order number plus the phone or email on the courier's own parcel label — no login) and `LandingPageController::confirmation()` (reachable by whoever holds the unguessable `guest_token`, lower severity but the same raw-model habit). Both built an explicit allow-list from reading exactly what `TrackOrder.tsx`/`LandingConfirmation.tsx` use (same method as SEC-002's per-page field lists). The most concrete harm closed: `OrderStatusHistory.notes` sometimes embeds the acting admin's real name (`RefundService::record()` writes `"Marked as refunded by {$admin->name}: …"`), which `OrderTimeline.tsx` renders verbatim — anyone with an order number and the phone off the parcel label could see a staff member's name with zero authentication. Deliberately left alone: `account/OrderDetail.tsx`, the *logged-in* customer viewing their own order, uses the same `OrderTimeline` component against the same unfiltered notes — same underlying issue, but a different, narrower one (the question there is whether a customer should see internal staff names on their own order, not whether a stranger can), and it wasn't in this finding's scope.

## SEC-004 notes

`SocialAccount::token`/`refresh_token` had no `$hidden` and nothing in the app ever reads them back (Socialite's tokens are used once, at the OAuth callback, then stored but never retrieved — confirmed by grepping for `->token`/`->refresh_token` reads anywhere other than the write site in `SocialAuthController`). The subtle half of this finding: `Settings\ConnectionsController::edit()` does `$user->socialAccounts->map(...)` — and `$user` there is `$request->user()`, the *exact same* object `HandleInertiaRequests::share()` later assigns to the globally-shared `auth.user` prop. Eloquent caches a lazy-loaded relation on the instance it was loaded onto, so visiting `/settings/connections` would have left the shared `auth.user` prop (rendered on literally every subsequent page in that response, not just this one) carrying the full, unhidden `SocialAccount` rows — even though the page's *own* `connections` prop already mapped to a safe `{id, provider, connected_at}` shape. `$hidden` fixes both at once since it's enforced at serialization, not at the call site.

## SEC-005 notes

Confirmed by reading Laravel's actual `AuthenticateSession::handle()`: it calls `$request->user()` with no guard argument and compares against `session('password_hash_'.$this->auth->getDefaultDriver())` — both hardcoded to the app's *default* guard (`web` here), with no supported way to parameterize it for a second guard via the plain `auth.session` alias. First design attempt wired the admin side's rotation through an `Illuminate\Auth\Events\Logout` listener in `AppServiceProvider` — wrong, because that event fires *during* `Auth::guard(...)->logout()`, which both `AdminAuthController::logout()` and Fortify's `AuthenticatedSessionController::destroy()` call *before* `session()->invalidate()`, and `invalidate()` flushes all session attributes — so anything the listener had just written was destroyed moments later, before any subsequent request could read it. Moved the write to *after* invalidate instead: a new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (bound via Fortify's `LogoutResponse` contract, same pattern as the existing `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`) for customers, and a direct call in `AdminAuthController::logout()` for admin.

Second design issue, found by running the full `tests/Feature/Admin/` suite after the first cut (comparing hash alone): 9 tests failed, all sharing one pattern — `actingAs($adminA, 'admin')` then `actingAs($adminB, 'admin')` within the same test method, which shares one session without ever calling a real logout. `Auth::login()` itself doesn't guard against this either (logging in as a different account on a browser already authenticated as someone else, without an explicit logout first, is a real if unusual path). Fixed by keying the stored fingerprint on **admin id + password hash**, not the hash alone: a session that starts tracking a *different* admin than it last saw adopts the new identity silently (no logout), and only "same admin id, different hash" — the actual SEC-005 scenario, this exact account's password changed since this session last confirmed it — triggers the forced logout. This resolved all 9 failures without touching any of those test files.

## SEC-010 notes

All four sub-fixes read current behavior before changing it, to avoid assuming: (1) `Password::min(8)` → `Password::default()` — read `Password::default()`'s own source (`vendor/laravel/framework`) and confirmed it falls back to `Password::min(8)` whenever the registered factory callback returns `null`, which `AppServiceProvider::configureDefaults()`'s factory does outside production — so this fix is behaviorally invisible in every environment except production, where the factory instead returns the full strengthened rule. (2) `current_password:admin` only gates a **self**-edit with a password field filled; `AdminController::update()` already distinguishes self-edit (`$currentAdmin->id === $admin->id`) for the unrelated permissions-mutation guard, reused the same flag. (3) Read `FortifyServiceProvider`'s customer-facing `'login'` limiter for comparison — it has the *identical* gap (email+IP and IP-only, no email-only), so this is a pre-existing, sitewide limitation, not something specific to the admin guard; the finding scoped itself to admin specifically (higher stakes — "admin takeover allows full store control"), so only `admin-login` was changed here, not the customer limiter. (4) Logging only fires after `attempt()` has already failed, and logs the email + IP, never the password.

## FE-005 notes

Read `AppServiceProvider::configureErrorPages()` first: `if ($status >= 500) { Inertia::flushShared(); }`, with the comment explaining why — "the database or shared props may be exactly what failed." Confirmed server-side with a test (`ErrorPagesTest.php`, `.missing('auth')` added to the existing 500-page assertion) that `auth` is genuinely absent from a 500 response's props, not present-as-null. `resources/js/app.tsx`'s `router.on('navigate', ...)` handler read `props.auth?.user?.id ?? null` — optional chaining happily reduces "the key doesn't exist" and "the key is `{user: null}`" to the exact same `null`, so a 500 on *any* page during a logged-in session looked indistinguishable from that customer logging out, and `applyLogout()` cleared their cart (and the empty cart then synced to the server, deleting the real rows too — confirmed by reading `CartManager::replaceItems()`'s tail, though no code there needed to change; it correctly did what an empty `items` payload told it to do, the payload itself was the bug). Fixed by checking `'auth' in props` before the whole block, not just the logout branch — an error page should leave cart/wishlist/auth-tracking state exactly as it was, not partially update some of it. **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available.

**FE-005 follow-up (2026-11-11, tester Skip "couldn't really test", FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** re-read the client against the audit's recommendation ("run applyLogout only when auth is present and null"). The `router.on('navigate')` handler was guarded by `6dbf2ba`, but the module's startup block still read `props.auth?.user?.id ?? null` from the server-rendered page, so a *full page load* of a 500/503 error page (direct URL, refresh, plain link, anything that isn't an Inertia visit) was again read as a logout: `applyLogout()` cleared the local cart, wishlist and `LAST_USER_KEY`. It did not sync an empty cart to the server (`activeAuthUserId` is still null at startup, so the cart store's subscriber stays idle) and the next page re-hydrated from the server, but unsynced local edits (FE-011 retry queue) and the wishlist were lost and the next page took the guest-login merge path. Fix: new `initialPageCarriesAuth()` in `resources/js/app.tsx`; the whole startup block now runs only when the page's props include `auth` (unreadable page data keeps the old behaviour). No server change. Verified: decision table of the helper run in Node (logged-in, guest, 500-page, malformed, empty); `tsc --noEmit`, ESLint, Prettier and `npm run build` clean; `ErrorPagesTest` (7) green. **Not verified in a live browser** — there is no browser-automation library in this project and none may be added without approval; the project has no JS test runner. Owner/tester check after deploy: log in, add an item, type a URL that returns a 500 with APP_DEBUG off, go home, confirm the cart and wishlist survive.

**FE-002 re-verification (2026-11-11, tester Skip, no code change):** the tester could not test because they suspected no free-delivery threshold exists. It exists as a setting (`HomepageSetting` `announcement_bar` metadata `free_delivery_amount`, edited at Admin › Homepage › "Free Delivery Minimum Amount (৳)", 0 = off), read identically by `ShippingCostCalculator::freeDeliveryThreshold()` and `HandleInertiaRequests` (shared as `free_delivery_threshold`). Re-checked the audit's three claims against current code: (1) the drawer uses `resources/js/lib/delivery.ts::isDeliveryFree()` (threshold, product free-delivery with minimum quantity, free-delivery bundles) and `lib/coupon.ts::getCouponDiscount()` counts only the coupon's eligible items; (2) `Checkout.tsx::calcShipping()` uses the same helper with `active_bundle_recipes` and checks it before the address (9dd80c8); (3) `HandleInertiaRequests` no longer carries a rule, only the inputs and `Setting::shippingRateFor()` for the base rate. Ran the real `delivery.ts` in Node (`--experimental-strip-types`) over 11 scenarios mirroring `ShippingCostCalculatorTest` (threshold reached / just below / 0 = off, free-delivery product with and without minimum quantity, boundary, free-delivery bundle incl. via shared recipe, fixed-price bundle, mixed cart, empty cart): all agree with the server's expectations. `ShippingCostCalculatorTest`, `ShippingRateCheckoutTest`, `LandingPageShippingTest` (15) green. Final charges are always set server-side. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest with the threshold configured.

**FE-007 re-verification (2026-11-11, tester Skip "requires 2 hours", no code change):** the tracker's open item was exactly this live check. Done without waiting for a real expiry: ran the app locally (`php artisan serve` on a scratch SQLite DB, `APP_DEBUG=false`, port 8765, since stopped) and sent requests carrying no valid session/CSRF token, which is what an expired session produces. (1) Inertia form POST (`X-Inertia`) to `lp/{slug}/order` -> `302` back to the Referer (no error page, so the form keeps its state); following the redirect with the same cookie jar, the next page's props carry `flash.toast = {type: error, message: "Your session expired for security. Your details are still here — please press the button again."}`. (2) Plain non-Inertia POST to `newsletter/subscribe` -> `419` rendering the themed `errors/ErrorPage` ("Session expired — Your session timed out for security reasons. Refresh the page and try that again."). `ExpiredSessionFormTest` + `ErrorPagesTest` (10) green. The 419 page's Refresh button only appears in case (2), which no storefront form uses (all are Inertia; fetch() calls get JSON 419s). Not run in a real browser, and a real 2-hour expiry was not waited for. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest (tester can delete the site's cookies in dev tools to simulate expiry).

**BE-003 re-verification (2026-11-11, tester Fail from code inspection, no code change):** the tester reported "no `app()->isProduction()` guard"; `DatabaseSeeder::run()` has had `app()->environment('production')` -> `RuntimeException` as its first statement since `e1ebd4b` (deployed 2026-11-07). Real run, scratch SQLite database (never MySQL/production) with a marker category: `APP_ENV=production APP_DEBUG=false php artisan db:seed --force` printed "Refusing to seed demo data in production: it would wipe the product catalogue, overwrite settings and create a test login." (`DatabaseSeeder.php:29`); afterwards the marker category was still there and `test@example.com` did not exist. `APP_ENV=local php artisan db:seed` on the same scratch DB still seeded normally (11 categories, 36 products, test user). Searched `composer.json`, `.github`, `app`, `routes`, `bootstrap`: nothing runs `db:seed`; no other seeder truncates, deletes or sets a password. `ProductionSafetyGuardsTest` (3) green. Known limit, unchanged: the guard keys on `APP_ENV` being exactly `production` (the deploy's runtime check reports it is); a differently named production environment would not trigger it. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending the tester re-reading the file.

**BE-009 re-verification (2026-11-11, tester Fail citing `config/app.php` = UTC, no application code change, test added):** `config('app.timezone')` staying `UTC` is the finding's own recommendation (storage stays UTC), not a miss; the day boundaries were what moved (`dad977d`). Re-read all three places against the tester's example (order at 03:00 Dhaka on 5 Nov = 21:00 UTC on 4 Nov): `DashboardController` (Dhaka midnight -> UTC range for today/yesterday, 7-day chart grouped by the order's Dhaka date), `TrackDailyVisit` (`now('Asia/Dhaka')`), `invoices/order.blade.php` ("Date Issued" via `->timezone('Asia/Dhaka')`). Dashboard and visitor-counter Dhaka-boundary tests already existed; the invoice date had none, so added `InvoiceDownloadTest` 'BE-009: the invoice date is the Dhaka calendar day…' (3 cases: 21:00 UTC -> 05 Nov, 17:59 UTC -> 05 Nov, 18:00 UTC -> 06 Nov). Mutation check: removing `->timezone('Asia/Dhaka')` from the invoice view fails 2 of the 3 cases (the third is the same day in both zones); view restored, unchanged in git. `InvoiceDownloadTest`, `TrackDailyVisitTest`, `DashboardRevenueTest` (54) green, Pint clean. Not changed, by design: `config/app.php` stays UTC. Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest (an order placed between 00:00 and 06:00 Dhaka).

**Feature request (2026-11-11, from a tester + owner; IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED) — configurable order notification emails:** the tester wanted the shop to be told when an order is cancelled; the owner asked for two Settings fields choosing which shop address receives (a) the new-order alert and (b) a cancellation alert. Findings before building: the *customer* already received `OrderPlacedMail` and `OrderCancelledMail` (`Order::booted()`, covered by `OrderNotificationTest`/`CustomerOrderCancellationTest`) — unchanged; the shop-side new-order alert (`NewOrderAlertMail`) always went to the mother admin's login email, and a cancellation sent nothing shop-side. Owner decisions: one address per field; a configured address *replaces* the default; empty = main admin. Built: Settings keys `order_notification_email` and `order_cancellation_email` (group `notifications`, on the main Admin › Settings page under a new "Order Notification Emails" card, `email|max:255|nullable`, saved under the existing `settings` update permission, deliberately NOT in `LayoutCache::PUBLIC_SETTING_KEYS`); `App\Support\OrderNotificationRecipients` (configured valid address, else mother admin); new queued `CancelledOrderAlertMail` + `emails/admin/cancelled-order.blade.php` (order, total, payment method, reason, items, customer, link), sent from the same `Order::booted()` status-change hook as the customer's cancellation mail, so once per transition and for admin- and customer-initiated cancellations alike; `Order::dispatchAdminMail()` now takes the resolved recipient; the new mailable is on `FailedJobSummary`'s retry allow-list. Behaviour change to note: with the cancellation field empty the main admin now receives cancellation alerts (previously nothing). No migration. Tests: new `OrderNotificationRecipientsTest` (17); existing order-notification, cancellation, duplicate-order and failed-job tests (93) green; full suite 2868 tests: 2793 passed, 3 failed — the `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio that depends on the local `.env` `APP_URL` (pass with `APP_URL=http://localhost`); `tsc`, Prettier, ESLint, Pint clean. Not exercised against a real SMTP server or in a browser.

**OPS-005 follow-up (2026-11-11, owner report: phone app off, bKash/Nagad still offered on the live site; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** cause (from code, production not inspected): `SmsForwarderHeartbeat` kept the last ping in the *default* cache (file store), and every deploy runs `php artisan cache:clear`, so each deploy erased it; with no ping on record `isDown()` drops to the original fallback (no SMS at all for 6 hours; never, if no SMS was ever recorded), so a phone that was already off kept bKash/Nagad available after each of today's deploys. Fix: the ping is now stored in the durable limiter store (`config('cache.limiter')`, the database cache in production, which `cache:clear` does not empty; falls back to the default store if unset; the array store in tests). Also new `SmsForwarderHeartbeat::status()` and a status card on Admin › SMS Forwarder (online / OFFLINE with bKash+Nagad hidden / "no ping received yet — using the 6-hour rule", minutes since the last ping, last SMS received), so staff can see why instead of guessing. No migration; thresholds and the fallback rule unchanged. Tests: `SmsForwarderHeartbeatTest` +2 (heartbeat survives `cache:clear` and an off phone is still detected — fails against the old default-store code, verified by mutation; the admin page's status props through online → stale); heartbeat + payment-gate tests (14) green; full suite 2870 tests: 2795 passed, 3 failed (the local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, Pint clean. Note: right after the deploy there is still no ping on record until the phone's app next pings (open it once); from then on deploys no longer reset it. Verify live: open the forwarder app (page shows "online"), close it, after 5 minutes the page shows OFFLINE and checkout has no bKash/Nagad; deploy again and confirm it stays OFFLINE.

**OPS-005 second follow-up (2026-11-11, owner decision; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** after the durable-heartbeat push the live admin card said "No ping from the phone received yet" yet bKash/Nagad were still offered — by design of the old fallback (no ping on record -> hide only after 6 hours with no SMS at all; never if no SMS was ever recorded). Owner chose (asked, 2026-11-11): *no ping on record counts as offline and hides bKash/Nagad*. `SmsForwarderHeartbeat::isDown()` is now `lastHeartbeat === null || stale (>= 5 min)`; the 6-hour fallback and its constant were removed; `status()` now reports `has_heartbeat` instead of `mode`; the Admin › SMS Forwarder card is red "OFFLINE" with a "no ping received yet, open the forwarder app" explanation when there is none. Effects to expect: right after this deploys bKash/Nagad stay hidden until the phone's app pings once (open it with the correct server address and token); if the app can never reach `/api/sms/heartbeat` they stay hidden — COD is unaffected. Tests: `Tests\TestCase::setUp()` now records a fresh ping so every other test sees a healthy phone; `SmsForwarderHeartbeatTest` reworked for the new rule (no ping = down regardless of SMS history, stale ping = down, first ping restores, admin card props, home page `mobile_banking_available` flips with the ping); `ForwarderDownPaymentGateTest` unchanged and green; full suite 2871 tests: 2796 passed, 3 failed (the local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, Pint clean. Not verified on the live site or with the real app.

**PERF-006 follow-up — web push keys from the CMS + instant delivery (2026-11-11, owner report: no instant notification and the Received SMS table never refreshes by itself on the live site; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** root cause: `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` were never set in the production `.env` (not deployed, and nothing in the repo or tracker records them ever being provisioned), so the `vapid_public_key` shared prop was empty, the "Enable SMS notifications" banner (which requires it) never rendered, no browser could subscribe, and no push was ever sent — and the table's auto-refresh depends on that push reaching the open tab. A second, independent delay: `NewSmsReceived` was queued, and the queue only drains when cron runs `app:drain-queue` (once a minute), so even a working push was up to a minute late. Owner asked to configure the keys from the CMS instead of `.env`. Built: `App\Support\WebPushConfig` (keys in `Setting` rows `webpush_public_key`/`webpush_private_key`, group `notifications`; **owner decision: CMS only — no `.env` fallback, `VAPID_*` removed from `config/services.php` and `.env.example`, a value left in `.env` is ignored**; `generate()` creates a pair with `Minishlink\WebPush\VAPID::createVapidKeys()` and, because old subscriptions stop working with a new pair, removes them in the same transaction); Admin › SMS Forwarder gets an "Instant SMS Notifications" card (status, mother-only "Generate notification keys"/"Create new keys" with confirm, and an "Enable notifications on this browser" button that does not depend on the dismissible banner) backed by `POST admin/settings/sms-forwarder/push-keys` (`admin.mother` + `throttle:mother-actions`, audit-logged, exempted in `AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest`); the private key is never sent to any browser, neither key is in `LayoutCache::PUBLIC_SETTING_KEYS`; `WebPushChannel` and the `vapid_public_key` shared prop read `WebPushConfig`, and the channel skips quietly (warning log) when no keys exist. Delivery: `NewSmsReceived` is no longer `ShouldQueue`; `SmsAdminNotifier::notifyNewSms()` sends it from a once-only terminating callback, i.e. right after the forwarder's webhook response has gone out (instant, cron-independent, never delays the phone, never throws). No migration. Tests: new `Admin/WebPushKeysTest` (13: mother generates and keys are valid-shaped, regenerate replaces keys and clears subscriptions, ordinary admin forbidden, `.env` values are ignored, blank = not configured, admins get only the public key and the private one never appears in the page, no key on the storefront, notification is not queued, no-key send is a quiet skip) + `SmsAdminPushNotificationTest` (3) + route wiring test green; generated keys accepted by the real `WebPush` client; full suite 2882 tests: 2807 passed, 3 failed (local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, Pint, `npm run build` clean. Not verified with a real browser push service. Owner steps after deploy: Admin › SMS Forwarder › Generate notification keys, then Enable notifications in each admin browser; test with Check Connection in the Android app.

**PERF-006 second follow-up — make web push failures visible and fix the sign-up path (2026-11-11, owner report after deploying the CMS keys: keys generated, "Enable" pressed, still no notification and no table auto-update; card showed "Keys are set up", "1 browser(s) enabled", yet this browser still offered "Enable"; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** the earlier tests used `Notification::fake()`, so the real send path had never run. Ran it for real against a stand-in push service on a scratch SQLite DB (real P-256 subscription keys, generated VAPID pair): `NewSmsReceived` -> `WebPushChannel` -> the push library produced a correct signed (`Authorization: WebPush <JWT>`), encrypted (`aesgcm`, 2838-byte body) POST — the server logic is sound; production's failure was silent either way. Found/fixed: (1) *Silent server failures* — new `App\Support\WebPushSender` (shared by the channel and the test button) returns and records the real outcome (delivered / rejected with `HTTP <status>: <reason>` / expired and removed / connection error / keys missing), never throws; `WebPushConfig::recordResult()/lastResult()` keep the last outcome in the durable limiter store and Admin › SMS Forwarder shows "Last push (test|SMS, time): delivered … | FAILED - …". (2) *Browser sign-up bugs in `useWebPushSms.enableNotifications`* — it never checked the server accepted the sign-up POST (set "subscribed" regardless), did not wait for an active service worker (`navigator.serviceWorker.ready`) before `pushManager.subscribe()`, would throw on a leftover browser sign-up made with an older key pair (now unsubscribed first), and swallowed every error; it now returns `{ok, message}` and the card shows the reason ("Notifications were not allowed… set Notifications to Allow", server HTTP status, or the browser's own error). (3) New "Send test notification" button + `POST admin/settings/sms-forwarder/push-test` (`sms_forwarder` update permission + `throttle:mother-actions`): sends one real push to the signed-in admin's own browsers and flashes the real result. No migration. Tests: new `Admin/WebPushDeliveryTest` (9: accepted -> delivered and remembered, 403 reported with status, 410 removes the sign-up, unreachable service reported not thrown, no keys, no browser, test-button messages, real failure surfaced, view-only admin forbidden) with a mocked PSR-18 client; earlier push tests green; full suite 2891 tests: 2816 passed, 3 failed (local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, Pint, `npm run build` clean. NOT verified against a real browser push service or on Hostinger (outbound HTTPS to the push service, PHP extensions) — the new card output after deploy is the diagnostic for that.

**PERF-006 third follow-up — push failed on a host without GMP/BCMath (2026-11-11, owner's live "Send test notification" result: `ErrorException: It is highly recommended to install the GMP or BCMath extension to speed up calculations…`; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** the diagnostics added in the previous follow-up paid off. Cause: `minishlink/web-push` v11's `Utils::checkRequirementExtension()` raises an `E_USER_NOTICE` (not an error — the library is designed to continue with a slower calculator) when neither the `bcmath` nor the `gmp` extension is loaded, and with no PSR-3 logger passed it uses `trigger_error()`; Laravel converts every PHP notice into an `ErrorException`, so every send aborted on Hostinger's PHP, which has neither extension (this machine has bcmath, which is why nothing showed locally). Fix: `WebPushSender` now passes the library a logger, `App\Support\WebPushLogger` (PSR-3), which drops that one advisory and writes any real warning (missing curl/openssl/curve/cipher) to the normal log. Verified by reproducing the exact error under a PHP with `bcmath` and `gmp` removed (`PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR` copy of the CLI conf.d without them): old code fails (library script and 3 of 9 `WebPushDeliveryTest` cases), fixed code delivers in ~0 s (the crypto is OpenSSL, not the missing math extensions) and all 23 push tests pass under that PHP; also pass normally. New test for the logger (advisory dropped, real warning forwarded). Optional, not required: enabling `bcmath` or `gmp` in Hostinger hPanel (Advanced > PHP Configuration > Extensions) only silences nothing now and gives no measurable gain. No migration.

**PERF-006 fourth follow-up — "0 browser(s) enabled": make the browser sign-up unable to hang silently (2026-11-11, owner on the live site: after generating keys and pressing Enable the card still says 0 browser(s) enabled and the test button says no browser is enabled; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** the count is the number of `push_subscriptions` rows, which exist only once a browser finishes the 5-step sign-up (permission -> service worker -> `pushManager.subscribe()` -> POST); "0" means no browser has completed it. Ruled out by testing in real headless Chrome (CDP): the production `Content-Security-Policy` (`strict-dynamic`, no `worker-src`) does NOT block registering `/admin-push-sw.js` (registers fine with and without `worker-src 'self'`) — hypothesis disproved, no CSP change made. Observed in the same Chrome: `pushManager.subscribe()` never settles when the browser cannot reach its push service, i.e. the previous client code could sit at "Enabling…" forever with no message. Fix: `useWebPushSms.enableNotifications(onStep)` now reports 5 progress steps (shown under the button) and every step has a timeout with a specific reason (permission popup unanswered after 60 s -> look for the address-bar bell/lock; worker file not loadable; worker not active; push service silent after 25 s -> Chrome must reach Google's push service / check network, firewall and "Use Google services for push messaging"; server did not answer); the small bottom-right banner's Enable now alerts the reason instead of failing silently. No server change, no migration. Verified: `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, `npm run build` clean; real-Chrome probes above. NOT verified end to end against a real push service (the sandbox Chrome cannot reach one). Next step depends on the message the owner sees after pressing Enable on the live site.

**Tester reports FE-013, FE-004 and landing-page labels (2026-11-11):** (1) *FE-013 (cookie banner), tester Fail, no code change:* the toggles the tester describes were removed in `f4800f6` (deployed 2026-11-07). Re-verified in real headless Chrome (CDP) against the current build on a scratch SQLite database: banner is the honest notice, 0 toggles/checkboxes, buttons "Got it" + "Privacy policy", dismissing stores `{necessary:true, analytics:false, marketing:false, timestamp}` and the banner does not return on the next load. A first visit sets `XSRF-TOKEN`, `shoppydrive-session`, `sd_traffic_source`, `sd_traffic_referrer` and `sd_last_seen` before any click, which is the owner's recorded 2026-11-08 decision (no consent gate) and is what the notice discloses; Google Fonts still loads from Google (recorded limit). The screenshot the owner sent was labelled FE-003 but shows FE-013; worked as FE-013. (2) *FE-004 (lost-parcel screen), tester Fail, no code change:* the screen exists (`LostParcelDialog.tsx` + `orders/show.tsx`, `7083f65`, deployed 2026-11-07) and calls the route through `route('admin.orders.confirmLostParcel', …)`, so a text search of `resources/js` for the URL finds nothing. Verified in real Chrome as the main admin on an order with an unrecognised Steadfast status: amber banner, "Confirm parcel lost" button, dialog with labelled note and password fields. Only offered to the mother admin and only for that banner state (server also requires a not-delivered verification). (3) *Landing-page order form needs input labels (tester feedback, FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):* fields had only placeholders (plus screen-reader `aria-label`s), so nothing named a field once typing began. `resources/js/pages/landing/LandingPage.tsx` now has a visible `FieldLabel` tied to each input by `id`/`htmlFor` (Full Name*, Phone Number*, Email (optional), Address*, Area / Landmark (optional), City / Upazila*), District* and Area* via the shared `Select`'s own `label`/`required` (aria + asterisk only, no native `required`, so submit behaviour is unchanged), a visible "Transaction ID" label on the payment step, and "Decrease/Increase quantity" names on the +/− buttons; placeholders became example hints and the `aria-label`s were dropped (the label now names the field; two names would conflict). Verified in real Chrome: every visible field has a label, clicking a label focuses its input, no unlabeled inputs, an empty submit still shows all five field errors and creates no order; screenshot checked. `tsc`/Prettier clean (the 2 ESLint warnings at the old `useEffect` are pre-existing). No server change, no migration.

**FE-010 browser verification (2026-11-11, tester Skip "couldn't find any react error… how exceptions are handled", no code change):** closes the gap the FE-010 row recorded ("the boundary's fallback rendering is not exercised by any test… needs a browser check"). Method: temporarily made `pages/Home.tsx` throw when `localStorage.__force_crash === '1'` (backed up first; restored byte for byte, sha256 verified identical, rebuilt without it, `git status` clean), ran the app on a scratch SQLite database with `LOG_CHANNEL=stderr`, and drove real headless Chrome over CDP. Results: a normal page renders; the crashing home page shows the boundary ("Something went wrong on this page. Your cart is safe. Please reload the page or go back to the home page.", buttons "Reload page" and "Go to home page") instead of a blank page; one `POST /client-errors` reaches the server and logs `Browser page crash` with the message, `path:"/"` and the component stack (twice here only because the dev build runs React StrictMode; this machine's Vite dev server was serving the assets); with the flag cleared, "Go to home page" recovers to a normal page. `ClientErrorReportTest` green. The boundary only catches errors thrown while React renders; errors in event handlers or async code are not boundary errors (they go through the existing Inertia form-error and toast handling). Admin variant and the Inertia-navigate reset were not separately driven in the browser (code-read only). Status stays DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED pending a live retest, which is a developer check, not something a tester can trigger.

**FE-003 follow-up (2026-11-11, tester Skip "helpful button doesn't exist in review section's frontend"; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** the missing button is the owner-approved removal (`322aa51`, deployed 2026-11-07; `grep -i helpful` in `resources/js` finds only the unused `helpful_count` types and one option). That one option was a leftover the first fix missed, and the audit's own file reference (`ProductDetail.tsx:1227-1230`) points at it: the review "Sort by" `Select` on the product page had no value, handler or state, so none of its four options (Newest First, Highest Rated, Lowest Rated, Most Helpful) did anything, and "Most Helpful" named a removed feature. Owner decision (asked, 2026-11-11): make the sort work and drop "Most Helpful". `pages/catalog/ProductDetail.tsx` now keeps `reviewSort` state and a memoised `sortedReviews` (`newest` = server order; `highest`/`lowest` = stable sort by rating, so ties stay newest first); the `Select` is controlled and offers only the three real options. Known limit: `ProductController::show()` passes only the newest 50 approved reviews (`->latest()->limit(50)`), so for a product with more than 50 reviews the sort applies to those 50. No server change, no migration. Verified in real headless Chrome (CDP) on a scratch SQLite database with 4 reviews (ratings 5/1/3/5, different dates): default newest-first; Highest Rated -> 5(newer), 5(older), 3, 1; Lowest Rated -> 1, 3, 5(newer), 5(older); back to Newest First restores it; the open list shows exactly the three options; "helpful" appears nowhere on the page. `tsc` clean, ESLint 0 errors (3 old warnings), Prettier, `npm run build`; review/product tests (35) green. The unused `helpful_count`/`not_helpful_count`/`user_voted` fields in `types/index.ts` and the `review_votes` table are left alone (no behaviour, removal is a separate cleanup).

**Admin route exposure check, and a record-probing gap found and fixed (2026-11-11, owner question "admin routes in the home page JS — security issue? are you sure admin routes aren't exposed without login?"; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** *Part 1 — exposure (no change needed, verified):* the main script the owner screenshotted (`app-*.js`, 13 "admin" mentions, same count as the live file) holds a Ziggy table of 93 customer/storefront routes and 0 admin URIs or admin-prefixed names (SEC-016: `config/ziggy.php` `except` + the lazy `admin-pages` chunk); the 13 mentions are chunk file names (`admin-login`, `admin-pages`), `startsWith('admin/')` path checks, `/admin` links on the crash page, the lazy loader, and one `route('admin.products.edit')` string in the toast component that only admins ever run. Verified again on a running copy as a visitor: the home page HTML inlines 103 routes, none admin; the only non-customer name in the list is `steadfast.webhook` (`POST /api/steadfast/webhook`, HMAC-signed, 401 on a bad/missing signature, throttled). Note: the `admin-pages-*.js` chunk is still a public static file by design (SPA code cannot be hidden; its only protection is the server-side access control below). *Part 2 — access (verified):* all 214 admin routes (226 method/route requests) sent as a visitor with a valid session + CSRF token but no admin login: none answered 200. *Part 3 — the gap (FIXED):* 91 of those answered 404 and the rest 302, i.e. Laravel looked the record up from the URL (`SubstituteBindings`) BEFORE the `admin` middleware ran, so a visitor could tell whether an order/customer/product ID exists (`/admin/orders/1` -> 302 when it exists, `/admin/orders/999` -> 404 when it does not) and so count the shop's records; likewise an admin lacking a section got 404 vs 403. No data was ever shown and nobody got in. Fix in `bootstrap/app.php`: `prependToPriorityList(before: SubstituteBindings::class, …)` for `RedirectCustomerFromAdmin`, `EnsureAdmin`, `AuthenticateAdminSession`, `EnsureAdminPermission`, `EnsureMotherAdmin` (in that order), so the login/permission checks run first. New `Admin/AdminAccessBeforeLookupTest` (6; 5 failed before the fix, all pass after): visitor gets the same login redirect for an existing and a missing order/customer/product, a staff admin without the section gets 403 either way, an admin who may see the section still gets 200 for an existing record and 404 for a missing one, a logged-in storefront customer gets no hint. Re-ran the sweep on the running copy: all admin routes redirect to login for a visitor (the 2 `failed-jobs` UUID routes only 404 on a non-UUID URL because of their `whereUuid` constraint; with a real UUID they redirect too). Full suite 2898 tests: 2823 passed, 3 failed (local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); Pint clean. Not done (offered to the owner): also dropping `steadfast.webhook` from the public route list.

**Customer profile/settings consolidation (2026-11-11, tester report: no /settings link in the user menu, email field not disabled although changing it "doesn't work", /profile already has most of it; owner decisions asked first: one place `/account`, keep email change with the code, remove Appearance; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED):** *Analysis:* the storefront header menu (`components/layout/Header.tsx`) only had My account / My orders / Log out; the starter-kit `/settings/*` area was linked from nowhere a customer sees (its only link was in the unused starter-kit `user-menu-content.tsx`; the social-login callback redirected into `/settings/connections`), so features that existed only there were undiscoverable: the **email change** (SEC-009 code flow, which worked, but the code box only appears after Save, which looked like "nothing happened"), the **cart-reminder switch** (OPS-006), **delete account** (DB-001) and **connected Google/Facebook accounts**; `/settings/security` duplicated `/account/password`; `/account/profile` (name, phone, photo) showed the email read-only with "Contact support" and a hard-coded "Email Verified" box. *Change:* everything lives under `/account` now. `AccountProfileController` owns `edit`, `update` (name/phone/avatar + the email flow: a verified account gets the 6-digit code sent to the CURRENT address and the email stays unchanged until confirmed; an unverified account changes straight away and must verify the new address), `confirmEmailChange`, `cancelEmailChange`, `updateCartReminders`, `destroy`; new `AccountPasswordController` (`PUT /account/password`); `ConnectionsController` renders `account/Connections`; `Settings\ProfileController` and `Settings\SecurityController` deleted. Routes: `routes/account.php` gets a second group `auth, auth.session, account.active, history.encrypt` WITHOUT `verified` for the profile, email confirm/cancel, cart-reminder and connections routes (same access the old settings pages had, so an unverified customer can still correct a mistyped email), while `DELETE /account/profile` and the password routes stay `verified`; `routes/settings.php` is now only GET 301 redirects (`/settings`, `/settings/profile`, `/settings/appearance` -> `/account/profile`; `/settings/connections` -> `/account/connections`; `/settings/security` -> `/account/password`); the old PATCH/PUT/DELETE settings endpoints are gone. Route names: `account.profile` (GET), `account.profile.update`, `account.profile.email.confirm|cancel`, `account.profile.cart-reminders.update`, `account.profile.destroy`, `account.connections[.password.update|.destroy]`, `account.password.update` (replacing `profile.*`, `connections.*`, `security.edit`, `user-password.update`, `appearance.edit`); limiters unchanged and now asserted in `SecurityRateLimitsTest`. Frontend: `account/Profile.tsx` has an editable email with a hint, the pending-code panel right under the form, a verification-aware Trust & Safety box (amber + resend link when unverified), the cart-reminder switch and delete account; new `account/Connections.tsx`; "Connected Accounts" in the Account sidebar; "Profile & settings" in the header menu; Appearance page, settings layout/pages and `appearance-tabs` removed (the storefront is light-only; `use-appearance`/`initializeTheme` untouched, still used by the toaster). Tests: all old-route tests moved to the new names; `Account/ProfileAreaTest` (12: legacy redirects, old endpoints gone, guests redirected, unverified customers can open profile/connections but not orders/password, unverified email typo fixed directly, delete account still needs a verified email, page props); full suite 2910 tests: 2831+ passed with only the 3 local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` failures (the 4 route-limiter tests that listed old names were fixed); `tsc`, ESLint, Prettier, Pint, `npm run build` clean. Verified in real Chrome on a scratch database: header menu now lists "Profile & settings"; the profile page shows an editable (not read-only, not disabled) email, saving a new email shows the code step and leaves the real email unchanged, the sidebar has Connected Accounts, `/settings/profile` lands on `/account/profile`, connections page loads, an unverified customer reaches the profile page and sees the amber warning with a resend link. CLAUDE.md line about `settings.php` corrected. The 6-digit code email itself and a real Google/Facebook connect were not exercised in the browser (covered by the feature tests).

**Buy Now is separate from the cart (2026-11-11, customer/tester report: after Buy Now the checkout also listed the cart's products, the product seemed to go into the cart, and the cart was emptied by the Buy Now order; owner decisions asked first: Buy Now buys only the Buy Now quantity even if the product is also in the cart, and show a short notice on the Buy Now page; FIXED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, includes a migration):** *How it worked before (verified, not assumed):* `Checkout.tsx` deliberately merged the Buy Now item with the cart store (two `BuyNowCheckoutTest` cases asserted it) and submitted the merged list; `CheckoutController::store()` wrote that list into the stored cart (`syncUserCart`/`syncSessionCart`) and, after the order, deleted every submitted row — so the cart's products were ordered and the cart emptied, the Buy Now product sat in the cart until the order finished (and stayed there if the order was refused, e.g. out of stock), and for a product that was also in the cart a probe showed the whole cart line was lost (cart of 3 + Buy Now of 1 -> order of 1, cart line deleted). *Now:* Buy Now = that one product, alone, and the cart is not read, written or emptied. `Checkout.tsx` `items` is `[buyNowItem]` when present (otherwise the whole cart, unchanged) and submits `buy_now: true`; `PlaceOrderRequest` adds a `buy_now` rule and refuses a Buy Now with anything but exactly one non-bundle line; `CheckoutController::store()` is now a thin wrapper around `placeOrder()` (the previous body) with a `finally` that always calls `CartManager::discardBuyNowCart()`; for Buy Now, `placeOrder()` builds the order from a temporary, guest-style basket (`CartManager::buyNowCart()`, `session_id = 'buy-now:<order_token>'`, `user_id` null, so no user/session cart lookup can ever return it) instead of syncing into the real cart, so every existing step (stock/price checks, locks, coupons, referral, points, COD and mobile-banking branches) runs unchanged on that basket; a stale Buy Now price just refreshes the page (no `priceUpdates`, which would have overwritten the client's cart store); mobile banking stages `PendingCheckout` with `cart_id = null` and `checkout_data.is_buy_now`, so the payment step has no cart to remove paid lines from; `index()` bases the referral preview on the Buy Now subtotal only. New migration `2026_11_11_111502_add_is_buy_now_to_orders_table` (`orders.is_buy_now` boolean, default false; additive, set by COD and mobile-banking order creation); the confirmation route passes `keepCart` and `OrderConfirmation.tsx` skips `clearCart()` for Buy Now orders (the browser copy of the cart would otherwise have been emptied and synced back as an empty cart). Notice on the Buy Now checkout page (only when the cart has items): "You are buying only this item. Your cart (N items) is unchanged." with "Checkout my cart instead". Tests: `BuyNowCheckoutTest` rewritten/extended to 14 (Buy Now only that product and cart untouched, same product in the cart keeps its own quantity, normal cart checkout unchanged and empties the cart, refused Buy Now and stale price leave no basket and the cart alone, more than one line refused, duplicate submit = one order, guest Buy Now, mobile-banking Buy Now end to end, confirmation `keepCart` flag); mutation check: with the new behaviour switched off 4 of them fail (refusal residue, stale price, same-product line lost, mobile-banking cart link); 480 checkout/payment/coupon/referral/inventory/bundle/landing tests green; full suite 2922 tests: 2847 passed, 3 failed (local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors), Prettier, Pint clean. Verified in real Chrome on a scratch database: logged-in customer with a product in the cart clicks Buy Now on another -> checkout shows only the Buy Now product with the notice and a "Checkout my cart instead" link, header cart badge unchanged; placing it opens the confirmation; /cart still lists the cart product, the DB order has only the Buy Now product with `is_buy_now = 1`; then checkout from the cart shows only the cart product, no notice, and the cart is empty afterwards; no leftover baskets (0 carts). **Observed, not changed (pre-existing):** typing `/checkout` directly into a freshly opened browser (full page load) sends the customer to the home page even with a full cart, because the page's empty-cart check runs before the cart store is filled; reaching checkout from the cart page's button works. **Deploy note:** includes a migration (additive, default false), so the deploy's `migrate --force` will run it on production.

**Order confirmation removes only the purchased cart lines (2026-11-11, owner request from a ChatGPT-reviewed convention: a completed checkout must keep items added meanwhile in another tab; owner confirmed the rule "remove only the purchased quantity"; FIXED_LOCALLY, NOT COMMITTED, frontend only):** *Analysis:* the server already did this (BIZ-019: checkout syncs with `deleteOmitted: false` and deletes only the order's own cart rows; mobile banking `removePaidLines()`), but the browser undid it: `OrderConfirmation.tsx` called `clearCart()` on mount, and for a logged-in customer the cart-store subscription in `app.tsx` then synced the empty cart, whose `cart.sync` (`deleteOmitted` true) deleted the other-tab items the server had kept; a guest's other-tab items were wiped from localStorage; and open tabs never reloaded the shared localStorage cart. *Change:* new `resources/js/lib/purchased-cart-lines.ts` (per-tab sessionStorage record of `{item_key, quantity}` written by `Checkout.tsx` on submit, cleared for Buy Now, read once, ignored after 3 h); `cartStore.removePurchasedLines()` subtracts exactly the bought quantities and clears the coupon (no Meta RemoveFromCart); the `clearCart()` effect is removed from `OrderConfirmation.tsx` and `app.tsx`'s navigate handler does the cleanup on `order/OrderConfirmation` (not for `keepCart`): a logged-in cart with nothing unsaved is replaced by the server's shared `cart` prop, a guest cart (or a logged-in cart with unsaved changes, which is then saved) is reloaded from localStorage first and has only the purchased lines removed; a `storage` listener reloads the cart when another tab changes it, without re-saving it from this tab. Tests: new `MoneyRulesTest` case (the confirmation page's shared `cart` prop still holds the other-tab item and `keepCart` is false), `MoneyRulesTest` + `BuyNowCheckoutTest` 46 green; the store/record logic checked with a throwaway Vite-bundled script outside the repo (5/5: 1×A bought keeps B and the extra A, record used once so a refresh removes nothing, never negative, coupon cleared, stale record ignored); full suite 3010: 2935 passed, 3 failed (the local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio); Pint, ESLint (0 errors), Prettier, `tsc`, `npm run build` clean. **Not yet checked in a real browser with two tabs** (no browser automation in the project). No migration, no backend change.

**Meta Pixel + Conversions API for seven events (2026-11-11, owner request with a full spec; owner decisions asked first: keep tracking every visitor (FE-013, no consent gate), Purchase is sent when the order is placed (COD included), keep the `order-{order_number}` Purchase event id, staff-created orders are sent as `phone_call` without the staff member's IP/browser/cookies; FIXED_LOCALLY, NOT COMMITTED, includes a migration):** *Audit:* a Purchase-only CAPI already existed (`FacebookConversionsApiService` + `SendFacebookPurchaseEventJob` from `Order::booted()`, credentials in Setting via Admin › Marketing, id `order-{order_number}`, confirmation page pushes `fb_event_id`). Found: (1) the service caught every error, so the job's 3 retries never ran and a timeout lost the Purchase for good; (2) `_fbp`/`_fbc` were never readable server-side because Laravel tried to decrypt these browser-written cookies; (3) admin manual/converted orders sent Purchase with the staff member's IP and user agent; (4) landing-page orders had no browser Purchase; (5) Graph API pinned to v21.0 (Meta lists it ending 2027-01-21; current v26.0); (6) `lib/analytics.ts` gtag functions are never called (left alone). GTM container `GTM-PRKGGNHM` v2 (owner's export, not in the repo): only the base Pixel tag (ID `1395344526007424`), firing on Consent Initialization + Initialization + All Pages, i.e. up to 3 PageViews per load, and no History Change PageView; no event tags. *Change:* new `meta_tracking_events` outbox (migration `2026_11_11_134427`, unique `(event_name, event_id)`, no raw email/phone stored, IP/UA/fbp/fbc kept, pruned after 30 days by `data:prune`); `MetaEventRecorder` is the only writer: `recordPurchase()` from `Order::booted()` `created` inside the order transaction (rollback = no Purchase), `recordBrowserEvent()` for the endpoint; `SendMetaTrackingEventJob` claims the row atomically, retries timeouts/429/5xx/`is_transient` at 1m/5m/15m/30m/1h with the same event id, records permanent rejections, final failures go to Failed Jobs; `meta:dispatch-pending` (every 10 min) re-queues rows whose job was lost and gives up after 6 days; `FacebookConversionsApiService::send()` builds the payload from the row plus the order/account at send time (hashed em/ph/fn/ln/ct/st/zp/country/external_id, unhashed IP/UA/fbp/fbc; Purchase custom_data from order items, `content_ids` = product ids), API version from `config('services.meta_conversions_api.api_version')` (`META_CAPI_API_VERSION`, default v26.0); old job class kept as a shim for jobs already queued at deploy. `POST /tracking/meta` (`tracking.meta`, web group/CSRF, hardcoded `meta-tracking` limiter 60/min per user or IP) accepts only ViewContent/AddToCart/RemoveFromCart/AddToWishlist/InitiateCheckout/Search with a UUID event id, a same-host `event_source_url` and product ids/quantities or search text; prices, names, totals and coupons are resolved server-side (`CartManager::resolvePrice()`); Purchase is refused. `bootstrap/app.php`: `_fbp`/`_fbc` excluded from cookie encryption; fbc built from `fbclid` when the cookie is missing. Frontend: `resources/js/lib/meta-tracking.ts` (one UUID per action, dataLayer push with `event: meta_*` + `meta_event_name` + `event_id` after clearing stale fields, keepalive POST retried once with the same body); wired in `cartStore` (AddToCart on add, RemoveFromCart on remove/removeBundle), `BundleCard` (one AddToCart per bundle), `wishlistStore` (AddToWishlist once the add sticks), `ProductDetail` (ViewContent once per product visit), `Checkout` (InitiateCheckout once per arrival), `SearchResults` (Search once per search text), `OrderConfirmation` + `LandingConfirmation` (browser Purchase `order-{number}`, once per order per browser, not for cancelled). New `docs/META_TRACKING_GTM_SETUP.md` (base tag fix, History Change PageView, 15 variables, one regex trigger, one Custom HTML tag passing `{eventID}` and `trackCustom` for RemoveFromCart, Test Events checklist). Tests: `MetaTrackingEndpointTest` (19), `MetaTrackingEventDeliveryTest` (10), `FacebookConversionsApiServiceTest` (rewritten to the new API, 9) and `FacebookConversionsApiOrderDispatchTest` (rewritten, 6: one Purchase per order, not configured, rollback, cookies kept, staff order as phone_call, leftover old job) all green; `QueueWorkerScheduleTest` count 13 -> 14; full suite 3009: 2933 passed, 3 failed (the local-`.env` `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` trio) after the schedule-count fix; Pint, ESLint (0 errors), Prettier, `tsc`, `npm run build` clean. No JS test runner exists (none added: new dependency), so the dataLayer side is only covered by type-checking. Not checked in a real browser; no real Meta delivery verified (needs the owner in Events Manager › Test Events). **Deploy note:** includes a migration (new table only) and one new scheduled command. **Owner actions:** set the Admin › Marketing Pixel ID to `1395344526007424`, a Conversions API token, switch it on; apply the GTM guide; run the Test Events checklist and record the evidence here.
