Product media is deliberately kept (order `product_snapshot.image`) and is NOT changed here (step 2, deferred). *Shared rule* — new `App\Support\PublicFileReferences`: `UPLOAD_DIRECTORIES` (folder => grace days; 7, `newsletter-images` 90), `uploadPath()` (only `/storage/<upload folder>/…`, no `..`), `isReferenced()` (LIKE over every text column of every table in the *current* database — MySQL's unqualified listing also returned other databases' tables, 802 vs 78 — with an explicit `escape '!'` since MySQL and SQLite disagree on backslash escaping, and both plain and JSON-escaped `\/` forms), `allReferencedPaths()` (streams values mentioning `storage`, regex-extracts paths); ignores Laravel bookkeeping tables and `activity_log` (audit history isn't a use). Dev timing: 35 ms per file, 56 ms for all. *Step 1* — new trait `App\Concerns\CleansUpReplacedFiles` on Category, SubCategory (`image`), Brand (`logo`), Blog (`featured_image`), HeroSlide, PromotionalBanner, ProductBundle (`image`), each listing `PUBLIC_FILE_COLUMNS`: on `updated` when the column changed (new upload, outside URL, cleared) and on a permanent delete (soft delete keeps the file — restorable), after commit, if the file exists and nothing references it, it's deleted; failures logged, never thrown. `BlogController::destroyMany` now deletes per model (the query-builder delete fired no events; also audits each). *Step 3* — new `storage:audit` command: report (per folder files/size/unused; unknown folders like `banners` reported only), `--list`, `--quarantine` (moves unused files older than the folder's grace to the private disk `storage-quarantine/<Y-m-d_His>/` with `manifest.json`), `--restore=<batch>` (never overwrites a file uploaded since; batch name validated), `--purge` (deletes batches older than 30 days). Not scheduled — run by hand. Dev report: 1,491 unused (94.6 MB) after the 7-day grace; nothing moved. *Tests:* new `UploadedFileCleanupTest.php` (25: replace removes old for all 7 models, clearing/outside URL removes, other-field saves keep, permanent delete removes for all 7, trash keeps, shared/used-in-another-table kept, JSON-escaped mention kept, non-upload paths/`..`/placeholder untouched, rolled-back save keeps, blog bulk delete, admin category form replace) and `StorageAuditCommandTest.php` (7: report changes nothing; quarantine respects references incl. full URL in post HTML and a setting, grace periods incl. newsletter 90 days, unknown folders, manifest; old order's product photo kept; restore without overwriting; bad batch names refused; purge only >30 days). Full suite 3,155 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed; Pint clean. Remaining: `image-originals` removal (owner's call). Steps 2 + avatar: see next entry.

**Unused uploaded files, step 2 (product media) + avatar on self-deletion (2026-11-12, owner request; committed a400afb, pushed fead24e):** the "delete if nothing uses it, after commit" logic moved from `CleansUpReplacedFiles` into new `App\Support\UnusedUploadRemover::removeIfUnused(...$values)` (upload folders only, file exists, `PublicFileReferences::isReferenced()` false, inside `DB::afterCommit` so a rolled-back transaction drops it, failures logged), now shared by the trait, products and avatars. *Products:* `ProductController::syncMedia()` (runs inside `saveAtomically()`'s transaction; it wipes and recreates `product_media`) diffs the current paths against the saved list and hands the dropped ones to the remover — a photo an old order's `product_snapshot.image` shows, or another product's media row, keeps its file; the `saveAtomically()` docblock no longer says replaced files are always kept. `Product::booted()`: a permanent delete reads its media paths in `deleting` (the rows go by FK cascade, no events) into a private `$mediaPathsToRemove`, and `forceDeleted` hands them to the remover once the row is gone (only products without order history can be permanently deleted). *Avatar:* `AccountProfileController::destroy()` — the no-history `forceDelete()` branch now removes the uploaded avatar afterwards (social-login URLs ignored); the anonymize branch keeps it (restore snapshot). *Tests:* new `tests/Feature/Admin/ProductMediaFileCleanupTest.php` (7: removed photo deleted while kept/new stay; unchanged save keeps all; old order's photo kept; photo shared with another product kept; failing save keeps the files it would drop; permanent delete removes media files; placeholder untouched); `OrderHistoryProtectionTest.php` +3 (no-history self-deletion removes avatar; anonymized keeps it; social avatar URL ignored). Targeted run 88 passed (these + ProductSaveAtomicityTest — incl. its existing "failed update keeps the original file" and "ordered product's old file kept" cases — ProductMediaSelectionTest, OrderHistoryProtectionTest, UploadedFileCleanupTest). Full suite later passed on the merged code before pushing (3,179 passed).

**Production storage clean-up run + Admin › Storage Clean-up page (2026-11-12; owner ran the command over SSH, then asked for it in the CMS; page committed and pushed b362495, deploy succeeded and the owner confirmed the page works):** *Production evidence (owner-supplied SSH output):* `storage:audit` reported 70 files / 86.9 MB, 22 unused / 30.0 MB (10 old hero-slide files 22.7 MB incl. an .mp4 and a .gif; 7 categories; 2 products; 1 each brands, content-images, sub-categories); `--quarantine` moved all 22 into batch `2026-11-12_131125`; a re-run showed 48 files / 56.9 MB, 0 unused. Batch awaits the owner's site check; purge on/after 2026-12-12. *Page:* the command's logic moved into new `App\Services\StorageCleanupService` (`report()`, `quarantine()`, `batches()` with files/size/`purgeable_at`, `restore()`, `deleteBatch()` — refuses a batch younger than `PURGE_AFTER_DAYS` (30), `purgeOldBatches()`; batch names must match `[0-9_-]+` and have a manifest); `StorageAuditCommand` is now a thin wrapper (same options/output; its 7 tests unchanged and passing). New `Admin\StorageCleanupController` (index, `quarantine` requires `confirmed`, `restore`, `destroy`; each audited as `storage.quarantined/restored/purged`) on routes `admin.storage-cleanup.*` in an `admin.mother` group (actions `throttle:mother-actions`, batch param constrained), exempted with a documented reason in `AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest`; sidebar "Storage Clean-up" (HardDrive, `motherOnly`) under Error Log. Page `admin/storage-cleanup/index.tsx`: plain-language explanation of what it does and the 3-step flow, summary cards, per-folder table (unknown folders "not checked"), expandable unused-file list, "Move N unused files aside" (ConfirmDialog), batches table with Put back and Delete (disabled until 30 days, with the date). *Tests:* new `tests/Feature/Admin/StorageCleanupPageTest.php` (8: report + batches props; quarantine moves only unused and is audited; confirmation required / nothing-to-move message; restore puts files back and is audited; delete refused under 30 days and allowed after, audited; unknown batch 404; non-date batch name 404; non-mother admins 403 on all four). Full suite 3,187 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed; Pint, ESLint, Prettier, tsc clean. The page itself not opened in a browser (no admin login used).

**CSP blocked the Meta Pixel's form fallback; Pixel "currency invalid" warning (2026-11-12, owner-reported production console errors; CSP part committed and pushed 449cf9a):** (1) `Sending form data to 'https://www.facebook.com/tr/' violates … "form-action 'self'"` — the Pixel submits a hidden form to facebook.com/tr for larger events (e.g. Purchase), which SEC-013's `form-action 'self'` blocked, so those browser hits were lost. `SecurityHeaders::contentSecurityPolicy()` now sends `form-action 'self' https://www.facebook.com` (that host only); new test in `ProductionTransportSecurityTest` pins the exact value. 31 security-header tests pass. (2) `[Meta Pixel] Parameter 'currency' is invalid for event 'Purchase'` — the site only ever pushes `currency: 'BDT'` (`meta-tracking.ts` CURRENCY, top-level, as `docs/META_TRACKING_GTM_SETUP.md` §4's tag reads via `{{DLV - currency}}`), and no other code pushes a Purchase (the older `event: 'purchase'` push was removed in 6d1f84f); so the bad value comes from the GTM container (a tag/variable not set up as the guide says, or a second Purchase tag). Not fixable in code; owner given GTM Preview steps to find which tag sends it and what value.

**Security › "Permanently delete a customer" (2026-11-12, owner request for database clean-up; committed, pushed with the merge below):** owner decisions: delete **every** order with no exceptions (deliberately overriding DB-001 order-history protection and PAY-RETURN-001 append-only courier evidence, for this one mother-admin action); unfinished orders put stock back; referred customers are only unlinked and points others earned stay; keep a minimal non-personal audit record. New `App\Services\CustomerErasureService`: `summary()` (orders, unfinished, payments, refunds, returns, reviews, addresses, files), `confirm()` (first mother password via `MotherPasswordGate`, shared lockout; single-use 5-minute session marker bound to customer + admin), `erase()` (marker pulled, second password, then one transaction: for each order `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` if pending/processing/shipped — idempotent, respects lost/restock-skipped — and `CouponService::release()`; then order_refunds, return_requests, payments, coupon_uses, courier_delivery_evidence and order_delivery_verifications (query builder: their models refuse deletion and CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest fixes who may use them), courier_submissions, meta_tracking_events, reviews, the customer's own referral_point_transactions, referred users' `referred_by_id` → null, orders (items/history cascade), incomplete_orders, pending_checkouts, notifications, sessions, password_reset_tokens / newsletter_subscribers / contact_messages by email, then the user (addresses, carts, wishlists, questions, votes, social accounts, stock reservations cascade); avatar via `UnusedUploadRemover` and review photos via `ReviewImageStorage` after commit; `activity_log` entry `customer.erased` with admin, user id, order and file counts only). No emails, no loyalty reversal; the customer's older audit-trail entries, inventory logs and order_deletion_audits are kept. New `Admin\CustomerErasureController` (JSON search by name/email/phone/id, 20 results incl. anonymized/soft-deleted; JSON summary; JSON confirm; DELETE destroy → redirect + flash) on `admin.settings.security.customer-erasure.*` in an `admin.mother` group (confirm/destroy `throttle:mother-actions` + session blocking), documented in `AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest`. UI: new `components/admin/CustomerErasureCard.tsx` rendered at the bottom of the Security page under "Database clean-up" only when `canEdit` (mother): search list, chosen customer + "This will permanently delete" counts, Delete permanently → password modal (1 of 2) → password again (2 of 2). Bug caught by the tests: `DB::table('users')->whereKey()` is a dynamic `where key = …` on a raw query (SQLite treats `"key"` as a string, so nothing was deleted); now `where('id', …)`, none elsewhere in app. *Tests:* new `tests/Feature/Admin/CustomerErasureTest.php` (10: full erasure of a customer with everything incl. courier evidence, refund, return, coupon, reviews+photos, avatar, newsletter — stock restored for the processing order, coupon released, referred customer unlinked, other customer untouched, minimal audit without email; summary; search by name/email/phone/id; no confirmation refused; wrong password at either step; confirmation single-use and bound to the customer; expired confirmation; shared lockout; delivered order doesn't restock; non-mother 403 on all four). SQLite FKs are enforced in tests, so the deletion order is checked against the RESTRICT constraints. Full suite 3,211 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed; Pint, ESLint, Prettier, tsc clean. Not opened in a browser (no admin login used).

**Out-of-range dates crashed admin saves (2026-11-12, tester report: Manage flash sales › edit › year 9999 › save → database error; committed, pushed with the merge below):** flash sale `starts_at`/`ends_at`, coupon `starts_at`/`expires_at` and blog `published_at` are MySQL TIMESTAMP columns (1970 – 2038-01-19) but were validated only with `date`, so 9999 passed validation and failed at the database. New rule `App\Rules\StorableDateTime` (MIN 2000-01-01 00:00:00, MAX 2037-12-31 23:59:59, message "Choose a date between 1 January 2000 and 31 December 2037."; skips empty/unparseable values, which `date`/`nullable` handle) added to those five fields in `FlashSaleRequest`, `CouponRequest`, `BlogRequest`. Frontend: new `resources/js/lib/storable-date.ts` (`STORABLE_DATETIME_MIN/MAX`) as `min`/`max` on every `datetime-local` input of flash-sales, coupons and blogs create/edit (6 pages), so the picker flags it before submit. Products have no admin publish-date field; courier-readiness dates (mother-only tool) left as they are. *Tests:* new `tests/Feature/Admin/StorableDateValidationTest.php` (11: flash sale start/end 9999, end 2038, start 1999 refused with the message and nothing saved; editing to 9999 refused and unchanged; 2000-01-01 → 2037-12-31 accepted; coupon start/expiry 9999 refused; blog 9999 refused; empty optional dates still allowed); with the existing scheduling/timezone/coupon tests 32 passed. Full suite 3,221 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed; Pint, ESLint, Prettier, tsc clean.

**Profile email change only for accounts without Google/Facebook (2026-11-12, owner decision while merging teammate commit 331aa10; FIXED_LOCALLY, committed, pushed with the merge):** 331aa10 ("image upload and email field fixed") removed customer email changes entirely (server ignored `email`, field read-only), which failed 12 existing tests (SEC-009 code-to-current-address flow, SEC-003 code binding, unverified typo correction — CLAUDE.md's reason account profile routes skip `verified`) and so would block every deploy. Owner's rule: an account with **no** Google/Facebook login connected can change its email (verified: 6-digit code to the current address; unverified: changes straight away and must be verified again — unchanged SEC-009 behaviour); a connected account can't. New `User::canChangeEmail()` (no `social_accounts` row). `ProfileUpdateRequest`: email `required|email|max:150|unique` when it can change, else `sometimes|nullable`, plus `after()` refusing a different address (case-insensitive) for a connected account with "Your email comes from your Google or Facebook sign-in, so it can't be changed here." `AccountProfileController::update()` restores the email-change branch only when `canChangeEmail()`; `confirmEmailChange()` cancels the pending change and refuses if Google/Facebook was connected meanwhile; `edit()` sends `emailLocked`. `account/Profile.tsx`: editable email (with the original hints) unless `emailLocked`, then read-only with the reason; 331aa10's other changes kept (click-photo upload, aria-label, removed Upload New button). 331aa10's new test rewritten for connected accounts (Google/Facebook, verified/unverified); added: connected account can save the rest of its profile (same email, other capitals, no email), unconnected account gets the code, connecting Google while a change is pending stops it, `emailLocked` prop. Also fixed a flaky customer-erasure search test by ranking an exact customer-number match first (`orderByRaw('id = ? desc')`). Full suite 3,230 passed, 72 skipped, 0 failed; Pint, ESLint, Prettier, tsc clean.

**Cart line could go above stock via the wishlist (2026-11-12, tester report: product with 20 in stock, all 20 in the cart, then Add to Cart from the wishlist page made it 21; FIXED_LOCALLY, NOT COMMITTED; no migration; supersedes the "Not changed" note in the wishlist Add All entry above):** *Cause:* nothing on the add path compared against what the cart already held. The cart store's `addItem()` (used by every product card incl. the wishlist page's, the product page and bundles) added blindly; the cart page's stepper capped at stock but adding didn't; server-side `CartManager::mergeItemPayloads()` (wishlist Add All, `cart.store`, AI assistant, login merge) summed quantities without a cap. *Fix:* (1) `cartStore.addItem()` caps a plain (non-bundle) line at its variant's/product's `stock_quantity` (same value the cart page stepper uses) and now returns the units actually added; `ProductCard` shows a warning toast 'No more stock — Your cart already has all available X.' instead of 'Added to cart' when 0 were added, `ProductDetail` the same, or an info toast when only part of the chosen quantity fitted. Bundle lines unchanged (their own set limit). (2) `mergeItemPayloads()` caps a plain line at stock but never below what it already held (same rule as `mergeDuplicateRows()`); a line whose product has 0 stock is left as before (not silently dropped — checkout reports it). (3) `WishlistController::addAllToCart()` skips a product whose cart line already holds all its stock: it stays on the wishlist, counted in new JSON field `skipped_already_in_cart`, message '1 item is already in your cart with all available stock, so it stayed on your wishlist.' (plural form too), `info` flash. Checkout's own stock check is unchanged. *Known gap:* the AI assistant's add-to-cart reply still says 'Added N' when the merge capped it. *Tests:* new in `WishlistActionsTest` (full line skipped and stays saved, 19/20 line goes to 20) and `CartPersistenceTest` (`cart.store` 18+5 → 20, +1 → 20; sold-out line of 2 untouched); `CartConcurrencyTest` 'stock is still enforced at checkout, not when adding to the cart' renamed and changed (intended behaviour change): adding 2 with stock 1 now gives 1, and checkout still refuses a line stock later fell below. `--filter='Cart|Wishlist|Checkout|Assistant|Bundle|Login'` 563 tests: 555 passed, 8 skipped, 0 failed; Pint clean; ESLint 0 errors (3 pre-existing hook warnings in ProductDetail), Prettier and tsc clean for the touched files. `CartConcurrencyRaceTest` (MySQL-only) not run; its merges use stock 50 with at most 2 per line, so the cap doesn't change them. Not checked in a live browser; full suite not run.

## FSA-02 notes (= SEC-001, 2026-11-05 audit)

**Status: CLOSED, 2026-11-05. Code deployed; sender rules configured in production by the owner. Owner closed this at their own instruction, without a separate post-change real-payment confirmation — if a real bKash/Nagad payment is later seen to fail to verify, that's this same finding reopened, not a new one.**

- **Root cause:** `SmsParserService::senderAllowed()` treats a `null`/empty `sender_regex` as "allow any sender" — the allowlist mechanism existed in code but was opt-in, and no `sms_patterns` row had ever had one set, so in practice *any* SMS whose body matched the identifier/amount/TrxID pattern was accepted regardless of who sent it. Anyone texting the shop's forwarder number a fabricated "payment received" message, from their own ordinary phone, could mark an order as paid.
- **Why this took two attempts to get the value right:** first asked the owner for the sender, and received a transaction ID (`TrxID`/`TxnID` values) instead — a different field entirely, extracted from inside the SMS body. Clarified the distinction (sender = who the *text message itself* is from; shown at the top of the phone's own messaging app / SMS thread, never written inside the message content) and asked again. Second attempt returned the Nagad message's own `Sender: 018xxxxxxxx` body field — which is the *paying customer's* phone number (Nagad's own courtesy inclusion in the notification text), not the SMS sender — using that value would have made the rule match only that one customer and reject every other genuine payment. Clarified again, explicitly asking what the phone's own Messages app shows as "from" for that conversation: `bKash` and `NAGAD`, exactly the short alphanumeric IDs these providers are expected to send from.
- **Fix applied (data only, no code change — the enforcement mechanism was already deployed):** owner set, via Admin › SMS Patterns:
  - The `bkash` pattern (identifier `TrxID`): `sender_regex` = `(bkash)`
  - Both `nagad` patterns (identifiers `Money Received.` and `Cash In Received.`): `sender_regex` = `(nagad)`
  - Deliberately a substring match, not an exact `^...$` match: tolerant of the forwarder app relaying the sender field slightly differently than the phone's own display, while a normal customer phone number (digits only) can never contain those letters either way. Case-insensitive by `SmsRegex::run()`'s own `/i` flag, applied to every pattern regardless of casing typed.
  - The admin form's `SafeSmsRegex` rule requires a capture group in parentheses even for a sender field that's only ever used for a boolean match (`SmsRegex::matches()`, never `firstCapture()`) — without it, `(` `)`, the form rejects the value as invalid. Confirmed via `SmsRegex::problem()`.
- **Verified before handing the values to the owner (local, read-only):** `SmsRegex::problem('(bkash)')` / `problem('(nagad)')` both `null` (valid); `SmsRegex::matches('(bkash)', 'bKash')` and `matches('(nagad)', 'NAGAD')` both `true`; `matches('(bkash)', '01797117509')` and `matches('(nagad)', '01837418940')` (the real customer phone numbers from the owner's own example messages) both `false` — confirms a forged SMS from an ordinary phone is still rejected under the new rule.
- **Closed by owner instruction, 2026-11-05**, before a real payment was confirmed to still verify correctly since the rule was saved — a deliberate choice, not an oversight. If a real bKash/Nagad payment is later seen to fail to verify, treat it as this same finding reopened.

## FSA-01 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `PlaceOrderRequest` and `AddToCartRequest` validated `variant_id` only with `exists:product_variants,id`. `SyncCartRequest` and `Admin/CreateOrderRequest` already checked ownership. `CartManager::resolvePrice()` priced from `product_id` (`FlashSaleItem::findActiveFor()` matches a product-wide sale against any variant id), while `InventoryService` locked and decremented the variant by id alone, and the order line took that variant's SKU.
- **Fix, all layers fail closed:**
  - New `app/Rules/VariantBelongsToProduct.php` (data-aware; derives the sibling `product_id` key, so it works for `variant_id` and `items.N.variant_id`). Applied in `PlaceOrderRequest` and `AddToCartRequest`. Same message as `SyncCartRequest`'s existing closure, which was deliberately left unchanged.
  - `CartManager::resolvePrice()` loads the variant scoped to the product before any flash-sale lookup: a mismatch is `ModelNotFoundException` (404), the same outcome as the existing missing-variant path.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` drops a cart row whose variant belongs to another product, as it already did for deleted products, so a row stored before the fix is never priced or checked out.
  - `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` throws `InsufficientStockException::forMismatchedVariant()` before touching stock. Every order path (COD, mobile banking, landing page, admin) already catches this exception and rolls back. Exception: `Admin\IncompleteOrderController::placeOrder()` catches no stock exception at all (pre-existing, unchanged), so there a mismatch rolls back as a 500.
- **Files:** `app/Rules/VariantBelongsToProduct.php` (new), `app/Http/Requests/PlaceOrderRequest.php`, `app/Http/Requests/AddToCartRequest.php`, `app/Support/CartManager.php`, `app/Services/InventoryService.php`, `app/Exceptions/InsufficientStockException.php`, `tests/Feature/VariantProductOwnershipTest.php` (new).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):**
  - `VariantProductOwnershipTest` 8/8 (24 assertions): foreign variant via `cart.store`; authenticated and guest `checkout.store` (no order, stock unchanged); valid pairs still work through both endpoints (variant price charged, stock decremented); a pre-existing mismatched cart row is not checked out; service guards in `resolvePrice()` and `InventoryService`.
  - The audit's proof-of-concept probe (a ৳50,000 variant ordered for ৳100) now fails to create an order.
  - Regression sweep of 70 cart, checkout, flash-sale, bundle, inventory, stock, landing-page, mobile-payment, wishlist, coupon, loyalty, referral and order test files: 709 tests, 679 passed, 30 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed.
  - MySQL race files grepped: only `CartConcurrencyRaceTest.php:170` passes a variant, and it pairs it with its own product.
- **Full SQLite suite (2026-11-03, together with FSA-03/04):** 1687 tests, 1624 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed. **Not run:** MySQL tests (no MySQL-specific behaviour changed).
- **Follow-up found during FSA-05:** a mixed cart containing a pre-fix mismatched row was refused by `lockForCheckout()` instead of dropping that row. This was fixed under FSA-05 (see FSA-05 notes), so for a clean FSA-01 commit `lockForCheckout()` belongs with FSA-05.
- **Remaining:** commit and deploy, each with separate approval. After deploy, the owner runs the read-only mismatch query from report §6 against production to find any orders placed through this hole earlier.

## FSA-03 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(checkout): secure order confirmation and preserve bundle attribution (FSA-03, FSA-12)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `routes/commerce.php`'s `order.confirmation` closure had no middleware and no ownership check, and rendered `$order->load('items.product')`. `Order` had no `$hidden`, so the full shipping address including phone, totals, coupon and points data, courier ids and `guest_token` went to anyone who knew the order number. Order numbers are printed in emails, SMS and courier labels.
- **Fix:**
  - New `app/Support/OrderConfirmationAccess.php`:
    - `allows()`: true for the logged-in owner (`orders.user_id`), or for a session holding an unexpired grant for that order number.
    - `grant()`: stores `order_number => expiry` in the session, 24 hours, at most 20 entries, expired entries pruned.
    - `payload()`: an explicit allow-list of exactly the fields `OrderConfirmation.tsx` renders. These are order number, status, payment method, total, `created_at`, and shipping name, email and address lines. Excluded: phone, items, user id, tokens, courier ids.
  - Route: `abort_unless(allows, 404)`; it uses a 404 so a guessed number reveals nothing. It renders `payload()` and adds `throttle:order-tracking`, the existing admin-tunable "order lookup" limiter, default 30/min.
  - Grants are recorded at every redirect that follows a successful placement or payment:
    - `CheckoutController`: COD success and the cached `order_token` replay. The token is a per-render secret, so a replay comes from the original buyer.
    - `MobilePaymentController`: `show()` and `submit()` when the pending checkout is already converted, and after a successful submit.
    - `PaymentController` (legacy ZiniPay) is auth-only and owner-checked, so the owner rule covers it unchanged.
  - `Order::$hidden = ['guest_token']`, defence in depth for every serialized order (account order pages, etc.). `LandingPageController::confirmation()` already passes `guestToken` explicitly to its holder, so the landing-page flow is unchanged.
- **Files:** `app/Support/OrderConfirmationAccess.php` (new), `routes/commerce.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/CheckoutController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php`, `app/Models/Order.php`, `tests/Feature/OrderConfirmationAccessTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `OrderConfirmationAccessTest` 9/9 (50 assertions) on SQLite:
  - The owner sees only the allow-listed fields: no phone, items, user id or `guest_token`, checked in the page HTML too.
  - The owner from a new session is allowed.
  - A guest in the placing session is allowed.
  - An anonymous visitor gets 404, and another logged-in customer gets 404.
  - A guest grant expires after 24 hours.
  - A cached-token replay re-grants access without a second order.
  - A guest who pays by mobile banking is allowed, and a fresh session gets 404.
  - `account.orders.show` never contains `guest_token`.
- **Gaps and limits:**
  - A guest who returns to the confirmation page after 24 hours, or from another device, gets 404 and must use order tracking (phone or email). This is intended.
  - The invoice link on the confirmation page still points at `account.orders.invoice` (auth-only, owner-checked), unchanged.
  - The frontend `Order` TypeScript type is broader than the payload; the page reads only allow-listed fields.

## FSA-04 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `Product` and `ProductVariant` had no `$hidden`. Every storefront surface serializes the models whole: product detail (`toArray()`, variants, related products), listings and search, the shared `cart` prop on every page, Buy Now on checkout, and the order confirmation. So `cost_price` reached every visitor.
- **Fix:** `protected $hidden = ['cost_price']` on both models. This is one rule for every current and future serialization; per-controller column lists would miss new props. `Admin\ProductController::edit()`, the only screen that renders cost (`edit.tsx`, `ProductVariantManager.tsx`), calls `makeVisible('cost_price')` on the product and each variant. Admin create posts cost without reading a model. Saving is unaffected, because `$hidden` only affects serialization; mass assignment, the audit log and `ProductStockToken` read attributes directly.
- **Files:** `app/Models/Product.php`, `app/Models/ProductVariant.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ProductController.php`, `tests/Feature/CostPriceVisibilityTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `CostPriceVisibilityTest` 5/5 (50 assertions):
  - Product detail has no product or variant `cost_price`, in props or anywhere in the page.
  - Listing and search pages show the product but contain no `cost_price`.
  - Cart page and checkout Buy Now, including the shared cart prop, contain no `cost_price`.
  - The admin edit page still receives the product and variant `cost_price`.
  - The audit's earlier probe proved the leak existed on product detail.
- **Full SQLite suite after FSA-01/03/04:** 1687 tests, 1624 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed; it touched only the new lines. MySQL race files reference none of the changed routes or attributes.
- **Remaining:** cached pre-fix arrays (layout, search-suggestion and catalog-filter caches) are cleared by deploy's `cache:clear`. Admin activity-log views still show `cost_price` changes, which is admin-only and intended.

## FSA-05 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** listings, search, home, brands and landing pages already filter `active()->published()`, but three entry points did not:
  - `ProductController::show()` bound the product by slug with no visibility check.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` dropped only deleted products.
  - Checkout's Buy Now preview (`resolveBuyNowItem()`) and `AddToCartRequest` accepted any existing product id.
- **Fix:**
  - New `Product::isPubliclyVisible()`, the instance form of `active()->published()`: active, `published_at` set and not in the future.
  - `ProductController::show()` returns 404 for a hidden product.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` also drops hidden products. A stale cart row or a submitted hidden item is never priced or sold, exactly like a deleted product today: an all-hidden cart gets the existing 422 "Your cart is empty"; a mixed cart orders only the visible lines (the established `CartStaleProductTest` behaviour).
  - `PlaceOrderRequest` validation deliberately left unchanged: `Checkout.tsx` has no display for per-item errors, so a stale client cart would be stuck with an invisible error, whereas the drop path is already handled.
  - `AddToCartRequest` rejects a hidden or deleted product with "This product is not available." (`Rule::exists` with the same conditions).
  - Buy Now ignores a hidden product, so checkout redirects home.
- **Defect found and fixed during FSA-05, which also affected FSA-01:** `CartManager::lockForCheckout()` (MED-08) re-checked the locked cart with only `whereHas('product')`, not the full `validItems()` filter. A mixed cart containing any row checkout deliberately dropped (a foreign variant from FSA-01, or a hidden product) therefore failed with "Your cart changed…" every time, and the row was never removed, so the customer was stuck. It still failed closed. `lockForCheckout()` now applies `validItems()` to the locked rows, so the two can't diverge. `CartConcurrencyTest` and `CartStaleProductTest` pass.
- **Admin preview:** none exists. Logged-in admins are redirected off the storefront by `RedirectAdminToDashboard`, so there was nothing to preserve.
- **Not changed (out of scope, noted):**
  - Admin manual orders and incomplete-order conversion can still order hidden products; that is an admin decision.
  - A mobile-banking `PendingCheckout` staged before a product was hidden still converts at payment, because the customer has already paid.
  - The wishlist page still lists hidden products, but they can't be added to the cart or bought.
- **Test-support changes:**
  - `database/factories/ProductFactory.php` now defaults `published_at` to yesterday. Admin create and update always set `published_at = now()` for an active product, so the old "active but never published" default was a state real data can't have, and it is hidden under the storefront's own rule. Tests wanting a draft pass `published_at => null`.
  - `tests/Feature/ReferralProgramTest.php:468,490`: two time-travel tests that move the clock back to 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01 now pin the fixture's `published_at` to 2026-01-01. Otherwise the product is correctly "not yet published" at the travelled-to time.
- **Files:** `app/Models/Product.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/CheckoutController.php`, `app/Support/CartManager.php`, `app/Http/Requests/AddToCartRequest.php`, `database/factories/ProductFactory.php`, `tests/Feature/ProductVisibilityTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/ReferralProgramTest.php`.
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):** `ProductVisibilityTest` 18/18:
  - A visible page loads.
  - Product page 404, add-to-cart rejection, authenticated checkout with no order and stock untouched, guest mixed checkout charging only the visible line, and Buy Now redirect: each for the inactive, draft and future-scheduled states.
  - A future product becomes visible after its time.
  - A cart row hidden after adding can't be checked out.
- **Full suite:** 1705 tests, 1642 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed. MySQL race files contain no visibility-dependent fixtures or `lockForCheckout` call-site assumptions. MySQL tests were not run.

## FSA-06 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Option A approved by owner (refuse cancel of paid orders, direct to the refund flow). Committed locally (`fe6fd58`); not pushed, not deployed.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` (`:406-451`) checked `status` (`UNCANCELLABLE_STATUSES` plus the FSA-11 `SHIPPED` guard) but never `payment_status`. A mobile-banking order (created `paid` at checkout, per `OrderService.php:58`) could be cancelled while still paid; `RefundService::assertRefundable()` then refuses to refund an already-`cancelled` order, stranding the money with no ledger path. `DashboardController::collectedRevenueQuery()`'s cancelled-order exclusion also silently dropped it from revenue reporting (the `LOW-08` cross-reference already flagged this interaction).
- **Fix:** a `$lockedOrder->payment_status === PaymentStatus::PAID` check inside `cancel()`'s existing locked transaction, placed after the FSA-11 `SHIPPED` check (so a shipped+paid order still gets the shipped message) and before the courier-unresolved check. New `OrderController::PAID_CANCEL_MESSAGE` constant. `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`'s Cancel Order card condition gained `order.payment_status !== 'paid'`. `RefundService` untouched — no refund logic duplicated.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/ShippedOrderCancellationTest.php`.
- **Tests:** `ShippedOrderCancellationTest` 10/10 (7 existing + 3 new parametrized cases: paid pending/processing orders refused with nothing reversed; pre-existing shipped-order and unpaid-COD-can-still-cancel coverage unchanged).
- **Known gap, not implemented:** no frontend test framework exists in this repo (no vitest/jest, no `.test.tsx` files, no `test` script in `package.json`) — the JSX condition change was verified by reading, not by an automated render assertion.
- **Conflict found while implementing FSA-07 (2026-11-03), resolved the same day (owner-approved Option 1):** `tests/Feature/MobilePaymentMatchingTest.php:310` ("a transaction consumed by an order that is later cancelled still cannot be reused for a new order") originally cancelled a paid mobile-banking order via `admin.orders.cancel`, which the FSA-06 fix now correctly refuses. The test's actual subject — a transaction id already consumed by one order can never be reused for another, regardless of that order's later status — is independent of *how* the order became cancelled, so the fix is test-setup-only: the cancelled state is now established directly against the model (`$order->update(['status' => OrderStatus::CANCELLED])`), with an inline comment explaining why (FSA-06 now correctly blocks the admin-endpoint path this test used to use). The duplicate-transaction assertion itself (`assertSessionHasErrors('transaction_id')`, order count stays 1, `$sms->matched_order_id` unchanged) is untouched. No FSA-06 production code was changed to accommodate this. Now 17/17.
- **Second, identical conflict found by the 2026-11-03 final pre-release audit (fresh full-suite run), fixed as a dedicated regression-cleanup task, same day:** this was the third and fourth times the same root cause surfaced — a pre-existing test that cancels a *paid* order via `admin.orders.cancel` and expects success, which FSA-06 now correctly refuses. Confirmed by direct code reading that the production rule (`PaymentStatus::PAID` → refuse, unchanged) is correct; only the stale tests needed fixing, per owner-approved scope.
  - `tests/Feature/OrderCancellationAtomicityTest.php`'s "a cancelled order cannot then be refunded, and its redeemed points are credited only once" cancelled a paid order via `admin.orders.cancel` expecting `assertSessionHas('success')`. Fixed by cancelling via the courier-report entry point instead (`CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync($order, 'steadfast', 'cancelled_parcells')`) — one of this same test file's other two documented cancellation entry points — which FSA-06 doesn't touch, preserving the test's actual point (a cancelled order cannot then be refunded, points credited exactly once) regardless of which entry point cancelled it. Confirmed fixed: now 10/10 on SQLite.
  - `tests/Feature/LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` (MySQL-only, skips on SQLite) had the identical conflict baked into its shared `createRedeemedPointsRaceOrder()` fixture (hardcoded `payment_status: 'paid'`) in three of its eight call sites — the three that race `cancel()` itself against another action (duplicate cancel, cancel vs. mark-refunded, cancel vs. a courier report). The other five call sites (duplicate mark-refunded, ledger-rollback, deadlock-retry, and two courier-webhook races) never call `cancel()` and genuinely need the paid fixture for `markRefunded()` to apply at all, so the fixture itself was left at its default and the function was given an optional `$overrides` parameter instead; only the three affected tests now pass `['payment_status' => 'unpaid', 'payment_method' => 'cod']`.
  - No production application code was changed for either fix — both are test-only corrections to match the already-approved FSA-06 business rule.
- **MySQL execution of the `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` fixture correction (2026-11-03, owner-provided local credentials — the "the three tests are still unexecuted" statement from the final pre-release audit and the first regression-cleanup task is now superseded by this):**
  - **Environment:** local MySQL 8.0.46 server (`127.0.0.1:3306`), confirmed via `SELECT VERSION()`. This is a local development MySQL instance, distinct from production (which is a remote MariaDB 11.8.9 instance this workspace has no credentials for at all — see FSA-06's own production-verification caveat below; this local run proves nothing about production).
  - **Isolated test database:** a dedicated `shoppydrive_test_fsa` database was created (`utf8mb4`/`utf8mb4_unicode_ci`), matching `Tests\SafeTestDatabase`'s required `shoppydrive_test_` prefix — confirmed directly: `SafeTestDatabase::assertSafe('mysql', config('database.connections.mysql'))` returned without throwing for this database name, and the guard remained enabled throughout (it is unconditionally invoked from `TestCase::setUpTraits()` before every test). A scoped user (`shoppydrive_test_fsa_user`@`127.0.0.1`) limited to `shoppydrive_test_fsa.*` only was also created and confirmed, by direct test, unable to access the real `ShoppyDrive` development database (`Access denied ... to database 'ShoppyDrive'`).
  - **One privilege gap, handled transparently:** the race tests' own lock-monitoring query (`SELECT ... FROM information_schema.INNODB_TRX WHERE trx_state = 'LOCK WAIT'`) requires the global `PROCESS` privilege, which MySQL cannot scope to a single database — the dedicated scoped user therefore couldn't run them. Granting that privilege was treated as a sensitive action requiring explicit owner approval and was not pushed through. Instead, the test execution used the explicitly owner-authorized local MySQL `root` credentials directly, with `DB_DATABASE` (and the test file's own `LOYALTY_RACE_TEST_DATABASE` env var) both forced to `shoppydrive_test_fsa` — so every migration, write and query in the run was still confined to that one isolated database, never `ShoppyDrive` or any other.
  - **Confirmed untouched:** the real `ShoppyDrive` development database was checked before and after this run — 73 tables, unchanged.
  - **Results:** the 3 adjusted cases run individually — **3/3 passed, 21 assertions**. The complete `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` file run afterward (per the file's own convention of running the full suite once the targeted cases are confirmed) — **12/12 passed, 70 assertions**. Each `php artisan test` invocation paid a one-time `migrate:fresh` cost against the freshly-created schema (not cached between separate process invocations), which is why these runs took several minutes of wall-clock time; this is a local-environment performance characteristic, not a test or application concern.
  - **Not claimed:** production verification. This is local MySQL 8.0.46 evidence only — production is a separate, remote MariaDB 11.8.9 instance this workspace cannot reach, and no claim is made that these results transfer directly to it (the same caveat the 2026-11-03 full-system audit already recorded for the 8 already-deployed findings' own MySQL verification).

## FSA-07 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Two of three owner-approved increments built; the third (durable PendingCheckout↔SMS linking, MED-20 Tier 2 style) was explicitly not built per owner instruction.**

- **Root cause:** `MobilePaymentController::submit()` checks stock, coupon cap and `PendingCheckout` expiry only at submit time, after the customer has already paid. Every failure path throws inside the `DB::transaction()`, rolling back the order — but the `received_sms` row (committed independently, earlier) survives unmatched, and the `PendingCheckout` row survives retry-able. Confirmed via existing coverage (`tests/Feature/InventoryEnforcementTest.php:288`) that retry already works mechanically; the real gap is recoverability/visibility, not data loss.
- **Increment 1 — expiry grace for proven payment (implemented):** in `MobilePaymentController::submit()`, both the outer and the locked inner expiry check now also pass when a `ReceivedSms` matching the submitted `(provider, transaction_id)` has `received_at` between the `PendingCheckout`'s own `created_at` and its original `expires_at` (new private `hasProofOfPaymentWithinWindow()`). `expires_at` itself is never written anywhere by this change — not extended globally or per-row. An SMS received after the original `expires_at` is still refused. All existing matching rules (amount, duplicate-use, wrong-provider) run exactly as before once past this check.
- **Increment 2 — admin unmatched-SMS badge (implemented):** `Admin\ReceivedSmsController::poll()` (the existing 5-second toast-polling endpoint, `useSmsPolling.ts`) now also returns `unmatched_count` — unmatched SMS older than `UNMATCHED_STALE_MINUTES = 30` (comfortably shorter than the 2-hour checkout window; chosen because normal customers complete the TrxID step in minutes). Surfaced as the existing sidebar-badge pattern (same component already used for `unseenOrdersCount`) on the "Received SMS" nav item. No new route, table, or dashboard.
- **Increment 3 — durable linking (deliberately not built):** matches this tracker's own `MED-20` Tier 2 precedent (a `PendingCheckout` with no `order_number` is ambiguous between "abandoned" and "paid but failed," and resolving that needs a schema decision this tracker doesn't make unilaterally). Not implemented per explicit owner instruction.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ReceivedSmsController.php`, `resources/js/hooks/useSmsPolling.ts`, `resources/js/layouts/admin-layout.tsx`, `tests/Feature/MobilePaymentMatchingTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/SmsPollReconciliationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `MobilePaymentMatchingTest` 17/17 (all green, including the FSA-06 conflict resolved per that section's notes above) — new: late-submission-within-window accepted and `expires_at` unchanged; SMS received after original expiry still refused; genuinely-expired-with-no-SMS still refused. `SmsPollReconciliationTest` 3/3 (new): stale unmatched counted, matched excluded, recent-unmatched excluded, existing SMS list unchanged.
- **Not claimed:** FSA-02 (SMS sender authenticity) remains unresolved — this work does not touch or depend on sender trust, and does not make FSA-02 better or worse.

## FSA-08 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Block-at-admin-save approved by owner.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\LandingPageController::store()`/`update()` validated `product_id` only with `exists:products,id`. `LandingPageController::completeLandingPageOrder()` always builds `variant_id => null`, and `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` (`:76-78`) throws deterministically for any variant product ordered with no `variant_id` — every order through a landing page for such a product fails 100% of the time (COD fails immediately with no money collected; mobile banking is FSA-07's deterministic case, money already collected first).
- **Fix:** a closure validation rule (`LandingPageController::productHasVariantsRule()`) added to both `store()` and `update()`, rejecting a `product_id` where `Product::whereKey($value)->whereHas('variants')->exists()`. No variant selector or funnel redesign built, per instruction.
- **Pre-implementation read-only check (as instructed), on the local dev MySQL database — not production:** `SELECT lp.id, lp.title, lp.slug, lp.product_id, lp.is_active, p.name, variant_count ...` found **one existing affected row**: landing page id `4`, slug `test`, `product_id` `64` ("Chomolokko Products", 4 variants), **`is_active = 1`**. **Not modified, deactivated, or deleted** — read-only per instruction.
- **Consequence for that existing page, not silently assumed away:** `update()` re-validates `product_id` on every save regardless of whether it changed, so this specific page can no longer be saved via the normal edit form (including an unrelated change like fixing a typo) until its product is changed to a variant-less one — confirmed by a dedicated test (`LandingPageVariantRejectionTest`: "saving other fields on a landing page already pointed at a variant product is blocked until the product changes"). It can still be deactivated or deleted without hitting this validation, since `updateStatusMany()`/`destroyMany()` don't validate `product_id` at all — confirmed by reading `UpdatesStatusInBulk`. **Owner decision needed:** leave page `4` as-is (broken, active, un-editable until repointed), deactivate it via the existing bulk-action UI, or repoint/delete it. No action taken pending that decision. **This is a local development-database record only — whether the same or a similar row exists on production is unknown and not claimed either way; that would need a separate, explicit production check.**
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/LandingPageController.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LandingPageVariantRejectionTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `LandingPageVariantRejectionTest` 4/4 (new: create rejected for a variant product, create still works for a variant-less one, repointing an existing page at a variant product rejected, saving unrelated fields on an already-variant-pointed page blocked); `LandingPageCustomContentValidationTest` 5/5 (regression, unaffected — its fixture product has no variants).

## FSA-09 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(auth): prevent social login account takeover (FSA-09)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `SocialAuthController::callback()` linked a social identity to any existing `User` with the same email. The only refusal (SEC-01) was Google's explicit `email_verified === false`. A missing claim, and every Facebook login, linked. The local account's own `email_verified_at` was never consulted, and the password and sessions of whoever created that account survived. Two related paths:
  - New Facebook accounts were created `email_verified_at = now()` on Facebook's word.
  - `connectToCurrentUser()` let an unverified account attach providers (`settings/connections` requires only `auth`). A pre-registrant could plant a Facebook link that outlived the real owner verifying the address.
- **Provider guarantees, as integrated:**
  - Google (Socialite `GoogleProvider`, OpenID userinfo) returns a boolean `email_verified` claim in the raw payload.
  - Socialite's `FacebookProvider` requests `name, email, gender, verified, link, picture`. `verified` is a deprecated account-level flag, not an email-verification claim, and nothing in the integration proves email ownership.
- **Fix (`app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SocialAuthController.php`):**
  - `providerVerifiedEmail()`: true only for Google with `email_verified === true`. This **supersedes SEC-01's "missing claim still links" and "Facebook unaffected" decisions**; the two tests encoding them were updated, not deleted.
  - Existing account matched by email:
    - If the provider doesn't vouch, nothing is linked. Google keeps its existing message. Facebook gets "log in with your password or Google, then connect Facebook from your account settings".
    - If it vouches and the account is verified, it links as before; password, sessions and `has_password` are untouched.
    - If it vouches and the account is **unverified**, `claimUnverifiedAccount()` runs under a row lock in the existing transaction: email marked verified; password replaced with a random one and `has_password=false`, the same state as social-created accounts, so the owner can set one in Settings › Connections; `remember_token` cycled; social links attached while unverified deleted; pending OTP cleared; `referred_by_id` cleared only if the account has no orders, trashed ones included, which keeps `ReferralService`'s write-once assumption since an unverified account can't check out.
    - **Session invalidation:** every customer route has `auth.session` (Laravel's `AuthenticateSession`), which compares the session's stored password hash and the remember cookie's hash against the current password. Replacing the password logs out every other session and remember-me login on its next request. No file-session enumeration is needed (the session driver is `file`).
  - New account: verified only if the provider vouches. Otherwise it's created unverified, the 6-digit code is sent (`sendEmailVerificationNotification()`), and the user is redirected to `verification.notice`, the same destination as `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`. The registration bonus and referral capture are unchanged.
  - Already-linked identities (`social_accounts` match) log in exactly as before.
  - `connectToCurrentUser()` refuses an unverified account: "Verify your email address before connecting …".
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SocialAuthController.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/SocialLoginTest.php` (2 tests rewritten for the new rule, 1 fixture given `email_verified: true`), `tests/Feature/Auth/SocialAccountClaimTest.php` (new).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):**
  - `SocialAccountClaimTest` 9/9:
    - A verified account links to verified Google and keeps its password.
    - A pre-registered unverified account is claimed: verified, password changed, `has_password=false`, remember token changed, referrer cleared, the planted Facebook link removed, Google linked. The registrant's real session, captured before the claim and replayed after it, is logged out, and the old password is refused at `/login`.
    - Unverified account plus Google claim false or missing is not linked and untouched.
    - Facebook never links or claims an unverified account.
    - A new verified Google account is created verified.
    - A new Facebook account is unverified, gets the code mail and is sent to `verification.notice`.
    - An already-linked Facebook identity still logs in.
    - An unverified account can't connect a provider.
  - A scratch control test (outside the repo) confirmed the same session replay stays logged in when no claim happens, so the logout is caused by the password rotation.
  - `SocialLoginTest` 12/12.
  - Auth, Settings, referral, loyalty, connections and anonymization sweep: 165 tests, 153 passed, 12 skipped (MySQL-only).
  - Full suite: 1714 tests, 1651 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed. Pint passed. No MySQL race file touches social auth. MySQL tests were not run.
- **Behaviour changes users will notice:**
  - Signing in with Facebook for an email that already has an account now requires logging in another way first and connecting Facebook in Settings.
  - New Facebook sign-ups enter a 6-digit email code once.
  - A Google sign-in that claims an unverified account leaves it with no password until the owner sets one.
- **Remaining concerns:**
  - **Existing data:** Facebook-created accounts made before this fix were marked verified on Facebook's word. They now count as verified, and a later verified-Google login would link to them. Finding these needs a production query (users with a Facebook `social_accounts` row created at registration and no password, `has_password = 0`). Their handling is a separate decision.
  - Social links already planted on accounts before this fix can't be told apart from legitimate ones.
  - The claim does not send `WelcomeMail`; that is only sent by the code-verification flow.
- **Legacy-account follow-up, implemented 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch) — the "Existing data" query above, built and tested, not just proposed:**
  - **Scope decision:** this finding's own two open risks ("existing data," "social links already planted") describe a population that can't be safely auto-remediated — nothing in the data distinguishes a pre-fix Facebook link created by its legitimate owner from one created by an attacker exploiting the now-closed gap, and retroactively force-logging-out, re-verifying, merging or deleting accounts on a heuristic would risk locking out legitimate customers and losing their order/loyalty history, both explicitly ruled out. So this adds **visibility, not an automatic action**: a read-only audit identifying the exact population, so the owner can decide case by case.
  - **Heuristic, chosen to be as precise as the data allows:** `app/Support/UnprovenSocialVerificationAudit::find()` returns every `User` with `email_verified_at` set, `has_password = false`, a `facebook`-provider `social_accounts` row, and not anonymized. A new account created through today's fixed code can **never** match this — `providerVerifiedEmail()` never returns true for Facebook, so a Facebook-only signup is now always left unverified until OTP — so every row this finds genuinely predates the fix; this is not a loose "old accounts" guess.
  - **Command:** `php artisan audit:unproven-social-accounts` (`App\Console\Commands\AuditUnprovenSocialAccountsCommand`) lists the matching accounts (id, name, email, verified-at, created-at) in a table and changes nothing — confirmed by test.
  - **Deliberately not done, per scope:** no account is notified, re-verified, logged out, merged, deleted or modified in any way by this command or the audit class; no migration; no change to `SocialAuthController`'s login flow itself (that flow is already correct for every event going forward — this only covers data from before it existed).
  - **Files:** `app/Support/UnprovenSocialVerificationAudit.php` (new), `app/Console/Commands/AuditUnprovenSocialAccountsCommand.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Auth/UnprovenSocialAccountAuditTest.php` (new).
  - **Tests:** `UnprovenSocialAccountAuditTest` 7/7 — a pre-fix-shaped Facebook account (verified, no password, Facebook link) is found; a normally-registered password-verified account is not; an unverified account is not, regardless of a Facebook link; a verified Google-only no-password account is not (Google already proves ownership, so it's not in the at-risk population); an anonymized account that would otherwise match is excluded; the command lists exactly the matching account(s) and leaves its `email_verified_at`/social-account row completely unchanged; the command reports "no accounts found" cleanly when none match.
  - **Outstanding owner decision (unchanged by this follow-up, now actionable with real data instead of a proposed query):** what to do with whatever accounts this command finds on the real database — notify them, force re-verification on next login, or leave them as-is. This command answers "which accounts," not "what to do about them."
- **Follow-up review and `--summary` mode, 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, same pass that also covers FSA-11 and MED-18):**
  - **Command re-reviewed against five specific safety criteria, all confirmed by reading the code, not assumed:** (1) strictly read-only — neither `UnprovenSocialVerificationAudit` nor the command contains a single `save()`/`update()`/`delete()` call; (2) modifies no user or social-account record — confirmed, same reason; (3) exposes no password/token/unnecessary PII — the default table mode selects only `id`, `name`, `email`, `email_verified_at`, `created_at`, never a password hash or social token; (4) never auto-verifies, merges, or disables an account — no such code exists anywhere in either class; (5) selection criteria correctly identify the legacy-risk population — re-confirmed against `SocialAuthController::callback()`'s actual pre-fix diff (`8cb8ff7`): the old code set `email_verified_at = now()` unconditionally for a new Facebook signup and linked to any existing-email account with zero gate, so `email_verified_at` set + `has_password = false` + a Facebook `social_accounts` row is exactly, and only, producible by that old path — the fixed code can never produce it for a new signup.
  - **New `--summary` flag**, added specifically because this task asked for an aggregate-only report that never selects or prints `name`/`email` at all (not merely withholds them from output): `UnprovenSocialVerificationAudit::summarize()` runs a separate query selecting only `id`/`created_at`, returning `{total, earliest_created_at, latest_created_at, by_month}` — a creation-month breakdown is the one aggregate the existing data meaningfully supports. `php artisan audit:unproven-social-accounts --summary` prints only these aggregates.
  - **Step B (determine the affected count) — blocked, exact reason:** this workspace's `.env` has `DB_CONNECTION=sqlite` only; no MySQL/MariaDB credentials for the real production database exist here or anywhere accessible to this session (re-confirmed; same blocker as the earlier MySQL-verification task, which already established there is no path to production DB access from this environment). Per explicit instruction, the local SQLite dev/test database was **not** substituted and reported as if it were a production count — running the command here would only ever report on test fixtures, not real customers, and is not meaningful evidence either way. **Exact command for the owner to run on production:** `php artisan audit:unproven-social-accounts --summary` (via SSH, from the deployed app's directory) — prints only the total count, the earliest/latest affected account's creation date, and a by-month breakdown; never a name, email, or any other per-row identifying detail.
  - **Limitation on identifying the vulnerable period precisely:** the heuristic identifies accounts whose *current state* could only have arisen pre-fix — it cannot further narrow *when* within the pre-fix window each one was created beyond what `created_at` already shows (surfaced via the new by-month breakdown). It also can't distinguish a legitimate pre-fix Facebook signup from one created via the actual exploit — that distinction isn't recoverable from data alone, which is exactly why remediation (below) treats every match the same way rather than trying to triage further.
  - **Tests:** `UnprovenSocialAccountAuditTest` now 12/12 (5 new) — `summarize()` never selects name/email even internally (confirmed by asserting the planted name/email strings are absent from the summary's own JSON-encoded output, not just checking the command's printed text); reports all-zero cleanly when nothing matches; groups correctly by creation month; the `--summary` flag prints only aggregate counts and explicitly does **not** print a planted name/email (`doesntExpectOutputToContain`); the flag reports "no accounts found" cleanly. Pint clean (one auto-fix applied: import ordering/fully-qualified-type on the new `Builder` return type, re-verified green after).
  - ~~**Recommended remediation (one approach, for owner decision):** route a matching legacy account through the existing OTP flow on its next Facebook login.~~ **Superseded 2026-11-03 by an explicit owner decision — see the policy update immediately below.** The owner confirmed social login is itself the authentication method and must never be followed by a mandatory email-OTP challenge, for any account, old or new. This recommendation is kept here, struck through, only as the historical record of what was proposed and explicitly rejected — do not implement it.
- **Login-policy update, 2026-11-03 (explicit owner decision, same pass) — "Email OTP must never be required after a successful Facebook or Google social login," applied to new and existing logins on both providers, including previously created Facebook-linked accounts:**
  - **Change:** `SocialAuthController::callback()` previously ended with `if (! $result['user']->hasVerifiedEmail()) { sendEmailVerificationNotification(); return redirect()->route('verification.notice'); }` after `Auth::login()` — this proactive, immediate post-login OTP challenge is removed entirely for the social-login path. Every successful social authentication now redirects straight to `config('fortify.home')`.
  - **What this does not change, by design:** nothing here sets `email_verified_at` — an account created via a provider that doesn't vouch (any new Facebook signup, or a Google signup without an `email_verified: true` claim) is still created/left genuinely unverified, exactly as before. The existing `verified` middleware on `account.php`/`settings.php` and `EnsureCheckoutAccess` is completely untouched — an unverified user (social- or password-created) is still redirected to `verification.notice` the moment they reach a route that actually requires verification. Only the *proactive* redirect immediately after a successful social login is gone; this is the same lazy gate a manual, unverified, password-registered customer already goes through today. Manual registration's own immediate OTP requirement (`RegisterResponse`) is a completely separate code path, never touched by this change.
  - **Every other FSA-09 safeguard is unchanged:** `providerVerifiedEmail()` (Google-only, explicit `true` claim required to link by email), `claimUnverifiedAccount()` (password/session/remember-token/planted-link cleanup when a provider-verified claim takes over a pre-registered unverified account), the anonymized-account check, and `connectToCurrentUser()`'s unverified-can't-connect-a-provider rule are all byte-for-byte untouched — this change only replaces the final redirect decision at the very end of `callback()`.
  - **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SocialAuthController.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/SocialAccountClaimTest.php` (1 test rewritten, 2 new).
  - **Test rewritten (not weakened — its real security assertions are intact):** "a new Facebook account must verify its email with the code flow" → "a new Facebook account is created unverified but logs straight in, with no OTP challenge." Still asserts the account is genuinely unverified (`hasVerifiedEmail() === false`) and the Facebook social account is created; only the redirect target (now `fortify.home`, not `verification.notice`) and the mail assertion (now `assertNotSent`, not `assertSent`) changed, matching the new approved behavior.
  - **New tests:** "an already linked but still-unverified Facebook identity logs straight in, with no OTP challenge" — the exact "previously created Facebook-linked account" case the owner named explicitly, proving the policy applies there too, not just to brand-new signups. "a new Google account with a missing `email_verified` claim is created unverified but logs straight in, with no OTP challenge" — the Google equivalent.
  - **Full regression confirmed:** `SocialAccountClaimTest` 11/11, `SocialLoginTest` 12/12 (unaffected — none of its assertions depended on the removed redirect). Manual registration's OTP requirement independently re-confirmed untouched: `RegistrationTest`, `AuthenticationTest`, `EmailVerificationCodeTest`, `EmailVerificationTest` — 53/53 combined, none of which this change touches.
  - **Residual risk, explicitly accepted by this decision, not mitigated:** an account an attacker successfully linked to under the pre-fix bug (a standing `social_accounts` row pointing at either a hijacked existing account or a phantom new one) still authenticates exactly as it did before this change — there is no re-verification checkpoint for it, anywhere, ever, as a direct consequence of "never require OTP after social login" applying uniformly to old and new logins alike. The read-only `audit:unproven-social-accounts` command remains the only mitigation: visibility, for the owner to act on manually (notify / investigate / leave), never an automatic gate.
  - **Not done, per explicit instruction:** no account deleted, merged, disabled, or mass-verified; no new mandatory step added to either provider's flow; no change to manual registration.

## FSA-10 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Both owner-approved remediation options implemented together (require `verified`, default to pending moderation).**

- **Root cause:** `app/Http/Controllers/ReviewController.php:44` hardcoded `is_approved => true` on every new review, unconditionally — the admin moderation queue (`Admin\ReviewController::approve()/reject()`, `ReviewApprovedMail`, the dashboard's `pending_reviews` count) was fully built but unreachable for new reviews. `routes/catalog.php`'s `product.reviews.store` route required `auth` but not `verified`, unlike every other customer-write route in the app (`routes/account.php`, `routes/settings.php`).
- **Fix:** `routes/catalog.php:16` — added `verified` to the route's middleware, matching the existing convention. `ReviewController.php:44` — changed `is_approved => true` to `is_approved => false`. No other file needed changing: `ProductController::show()` already filters the public review list/count/average by `is_approved`, and `LoyaltyPointsService::grantReviewBonus()` already no-ops on a non-approved review (both pre-existing, confirmed by reading, not modified).
- **Preserved unchanged, as instructed:** no delivered-purchase requirement to submit a review; `resolveVerifiedPurchaseOrderId()`; the verified-purchase bonus-eligibility rule; `LoyaltyPointsService`; `ProductController`'s approval filters; review ownership and `order_id`/`user_id` mass-assignment protection.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/ReviewController.php`, `routes/catalog.php`, `tests/Feature/ReviewSubmissionTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/MassAssignmentProtectionTest.php`, `tests/Feature/LoyaltyPointsTest.php`.
- **Pre-existing tests updated to match the approved behavior change (not weakened — both previously locked in the auto-approve behavior this finding fixes):**
  - `MassAssignmentProtectionTest.php` ("a customer cannot submit a pre-approved or mis-attributed product review") — the submitted `is_approved` field flipped from `false` to `true` (the actual attack the test's title describes — forcing instant publication — needs `true`, not `false`, now that pending is the real default) and the assertion flipped to `toBeFalse()`. The `user_id`/`order_id` spoof-protection assertions are untouched.
  - `LoyaltyPointsTest.php` ("submitting a review through the controller resolves verified-purchase status automatically") — its `points_balance` assertion flipped from `15` to `0` (the bonus now awaits admin approval); its actual subject, `order_id` resolution, is untouched and still asserted.
- **Tests:** `ReviewSubmissionTest` 7/7 (new) — verified customer gets `is_approved: false`; unverified customer is redirected to `verification.notice` and creates no review; a pending review is excluded from the product page's review list and rating breakdown; a verified-purchase review stays pending and grants no bonus; admin approval publishes it, grants the bonus exactly once (double-click safe), and sends `ReviewApprovedMail` exactly once; admin rejection keeps it unpublished; the dashboard's `pending_reviews` count reflects it. Re-ran `MassAssignmentProtectionTest` (8/8), `LoyaltyPointsTest` (12/12), `Admin\RemainingAdminAuditTest` (19/19, unaffected — already exercised pending reviews directly against the model) together: 47/47. `Admin\SecurityRateLimitsTest` 62/62 (its review-route case only inspects `gatherMiddleware()`, making no HTTP request — confirmed unaffected, re-run as a sanity check). Pint clean.
- **Known test-environment note, not a code change:** `public/build/manifest.json` did not exist in this worktree before this session (no prior `npm run build`), which made every Inertia-rendering GET-route test fail — including pre-existing ones like `Admin\DashboardLowStockTest`, unrelated to this change. Ran `php artisan ziggy:generate` then `npm run build` (standard local setup per `composer setup`/`CLAUDE.md`) to fix this for the whole worktree; no application code was touched to do so.
- **Test-only guard-switching note:** three new tests cross between a customer (web guard) and an admin (admin guard) session within one test, which this app's global `RedirectCustomerFromAdmin`/`RedirectAdminToDashboard` middleware actively defends against. `auth('web')->logout()` reliably clears the web→admin direction; the admin→web direction needed `$this->withoutMiddleware(RedirectAdminToDashboard::class)` instead, since a plain guard `logout()` didn't reliably propagate to the next simulated HTTP call in this test environment — the redirect itself is exercised by that middleware's own dedicated tests, not these.

## FSA-14 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Token-protected unsubscribe + confirmation flow + status-oracle removal + generic mail-template management link (Option 2, owner-approved) all implemented. Committed locally (`c94e9ef`); not pushed, not deployed.**

- **Root cause:** `app/Http/Controllers/NewsletterController.php:43-66` let anyone unsubscribe or check the subscription state of an arbitrary email with no proof of ownership — both routes (`routes/web.php:50-52`) carried no `auth` and no token check, despite a `token` column already existing on `NewsletterSubscriber` (generated on subscribe, never read anywhere in the codebase before this fix).
- **Fix — token-protected unsubscribe, scanner-safe confirmation flow (A + B):**
  - `GET /newsletter/unsubscribe` (new, `newsletter.unsubscribe.confirm`) — read-only, never changes state. Looks up `NewsletterSubscriber::where('email', ...)->where('token', ...)`; renders one of three states on the new `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Unsubscribe.tsx` page: `invalid` (missing/wrong/mismatched token, or email not found — all three collapse to the same generic message, so this never discloses whether an arbitrary email is in the list), `already_unsubscribed`, or `confirm` (shows a "Confirm unsubscribe" button).
  - `POST /newsletter/unsubscribe` (existing route, now token-gated) — `NewsletterController::unsubscribe()` requires both `email` and `token` (`required` validation) and only updates a row where both match `AND is_active = true`; a wrong, missing, or cross-subscriber token matches zero rows, so nothing is leaked or changed. Redirects back to the GET confirm page, which then naturally renders `already_unsubscribed`.
  - CSRF: standard Inertia `useForm().post()`, same as every other form in the app — no special handling needed.
- **Fix — removed the public status oracle (D):** `status()` and its route deleted outright (not replaced by anything). `resources/js/components/layout/Footer.tsx` and the `NewsletterForm` in `resources/js/pages/Home.tsx` both had the same debounced "live status check as you type, toggle Subscribe/Unsubscribe" widget depending on it — both simplified to a single, always-"Subscribe" action; `No spam. Unsubscribe any time via the link in our emails.` replaces the removed toggle copy. No dead `fetch('/newsletter/status...')` calls or unused `isSubscribed`/`checkStatus` state left behind (confirmed by grep after the edit).
- **Preserved unchanged, as instructed (E):** `subscribe()` is untouched — new subscriptions, the `already_subscribed` response, and inactive-subscriber reactivation all still work exactly as before, with no token requirement to subscribe. `Admin\NewsletterController`/`SendNewsletterChunk` (FSA-13's chunked-BCC sending) untouched.
- **Investigated per instruction — no concern found:** reactivation (`subscribe()` on an existing inactive row) cannot create a duplicate record (`newsletter_subscribers.email` is a DB-level `unique()` column, confirmed in the migration, independent of app logic) and keeps the subscriber's original token rather than issuing a new one — intentional, since the identity being resubscribed is the same person/address. `subscribe()` was not touched by this fix, so no new consent-bypass risk was introduced; the existing self-service opt-in model is unchanged.
- **Fix — generic management link, preserving FSA-13's BCC chunking exactly (Option 2, owner-approved):** `App\Jobs\SendNewsletterChunk::handle()` sends one message per chunk of up to 50 subscribers, all BCC'd, sharing one identical rendered body — a personalized `?email=...&token=...` link is structurally impossible there, so neither `SendNewsletterChunk`, `Admin\NewsletterController::send()`, `NewsletterCustomMail`, nor `NewsletterProductMail` were changed. Instead:
  - `custom.blade.php` and `product-promotion.blade.php` each gained one static, non-personalized `<a href="{{ route('newsletter.manage') }}">Manage subscription / Unsubscribe</a>` link in the footer, identical for every recipient of a chunk.
  - `GET /newsletter/manage` (new, `newsletter.manage`) renders `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Manage.tsx` — a single email field.
  - `POST /newsletter/manage` (new, `newsletter.manage.send`) — `NewsletterController::manageSubmit()` looks up an **active** subscriber by email and, only if one exists, queues `App\Mail\NewsletterUnsubscribeLinkMail` (new, `ShouldQueue` + `afterCommit()`, one-to-one to that subscriber's own address) containing their own `newsletter.unsubscribe.confirm` link built from their own stored token — reusing the existing GET-confirm/POST-unsubscribe flow unchanged. The response (`'newsletter_status' => 'manage_requested'`) is identical whether or not a subscriber was found, an inactive subscriber is treated the same as no subscriber (no email sent either way), and the token is never put in the response, a log, or anywhere else — only ever into that one personalized email.
  - New dedicated rate limiter `newsletter-manage` (`AppServiceProvider`, mirroring the existing `password-reset` limiter's shape exactly, since this is the same "lookup by email + conditionally send mail" risk): both a per-network (IP) limit and a per-email limit, so neither a single visitor mass-requesting links for many addresses nor one target address being flooded regardless of source network is possible. New `SecurityRateLimits::FIELDS` entries `newsletter_manage_per_network_times/minutes` and `newsletter_manage_per_email_times/minutes` (same defaults as `password_reset_per_network`/`password_reset_per_email`), surfaced in Admin › Security (`security.tsx`) under the existing "Newsletter" section. The GET page itself carries the existing lightweight `throttle:newsletter-check` (also now relabeled in that UI — see below).
  - Fixed stale admin-settings copy: the `newsletter_checks` row in `security.tsx` described the now-removed "live status check while typing" feature; its label now correctly describes what that bucket actually limits today — opening an unsubscribe confirmation link.
