- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/NewsletterController.php`, `app/Mail/NewsletterUnsubscribeLinkMail.php` (new), `resources/views/emails/newsletter/unsubscribe-link.blade.php` (new, extends the shared `emails.layouts.transactional`), `resources/views/emails/newsletter/custom.blade.php`, `resources/views/emails/newsletter/product-promotion.blade.php`, `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Unsubscribe.tsx` (new), `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Manage.tsx` (new), `resources/js/components/layout/Footer.tsx`, `resources/js/pages/Home.tsx`, `resources/js/pages/admin/settings/security.tsx`, `app/Support/SecurityRateLimits.php`, `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php`, `routes/web.php`, `tests/Feature/NewsletterTest.php`, `tests/Feature/NewsletterManageTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/SecurityRateLimitsTest.php` (route rename `newsletter.status` → `newsletter.unsubscribe.confirm`, plus new rows/tests for `newsletter-manage`).
- **Fix — token rotation on reactivation (post-review hardening):** before this, `token` was generated once on first `subscribe()` and never touched again — the same token stayed valid forever across any number of unsubscribe/resubscribe cycles, so a leaked old link would still work even after the real owner resubscribed, with no self-service way to invalidate it. `subscribe()`'s reactivation branch now also sets `'token' => Str::random(64)` alongside `is_active`/`subscribed_at`/`unsubscribed_at`, so every previously issued link stops working the moment the subscriber resubscribes. The already-active branch (early `already_subscribed` return) and new-subscriber creation are both untouched — confirmed by a dedicated regression test for each.
- **Fix — queue-dispatch failure fails safe (post-review hardening):** `manageSubmit()`'s `Mail::to(...)->queue(...)` call is now wrapped in a `try/catch (Throwable)`, scoped to only that one call. Before this, an uncaught exception there (a queue/mail outage) would 500 — but **only** on the "subscriber found" branch, since that was the only branch calling it — making a queue outage a timing/status side channel that could confirm a submitted email is an active subscriber. On failure it now logs `'[Newsletter] Failed to queue an unsubscribe-management email.'` with only the exception class name as context (no submitted email, no subscriber email, no token, no URL) and falls through to the exact same `'manage_requested'` response as every other outcome. No subscription state is touched by this method in any case — it never writes to `NewsletterSubscriber`.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/NewsletterController.php`, `app/Mail/NewsletterUnsubscribeLinkMail.php` (new), `resources/views/emails/newsletter/unsubscribe-link.blade.php` (new, extends the shared `emails.layouts.transactional`), `resources/views/emails/newsletter/custom.blade.php`, `resources/views/emails/newsletter/product-promotion.blade.php`, `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Unsubscribe.tsx` (new), `resources/js/pages/newsletter/Manage.tsx` (new), `resources/js/components/layout/Footer.tsx`, `resources/js/pages/Home.tsx`, `resources/js/pages/admin/settings/security.tsx`, `app/Support/SecurityRateLimits.php`, `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php`, `routes/web.php`, `tests/Feature/NewsletterTest.php`, `tests/Feature/NewsletterManageTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/SecurityRateLimitsTest.php` (route rename `newsletter.status` → `newsletter.unsubscribe.confirm`, plus new rows/tests for `newsletter-manage`).
- **Tests:** `NewsletterTest` 16/16 — correct email+token shows the confirm page and changes nothing by itself; confirming via POST unsubscribes and the link then shows "already unsubscribed"; wrong token, missing token, and a different subscriber's token each leave every subscriber untouched; an invalid-token or nonexistent-email GET renders the same generic `invalid` state; `GET /newsletter/status` now 404s and the route no longer exists; reactivation issues a different 64-char token; the pre-reactivation token no longer unsubscribes (renders `invalid`); the fresh token works end-to-end; an already-active subscriber re-subscribing keeps the same token. `NewsletterManageTest` 8/8 — both templates' rendered HTML contains the generic link; the manage page is publicly reachable; an active subscriber's request queues `NewsletterUnsubscribeLinkMail` addressed to their own email with their own token; a nonexistent email and an already-inactive subscriber both get the identical generic response with nothing queued; **a mocked queue-dispatch failure (`Mail::shouldReceive('queue')->andThrow(...)`) still returns the identical generic response, leaves the subscriber untouched, and logs an error whose message+context contain neither the subscriber's email nor their token** (asserted via `Log::spy()`); a full request-link → confirm-GET (no change) → tampered-token POST (no change) → real-token POST (unsubscribes) loop. `Admin\SecurityRateLimitsTest` 66/66. `Admin\NewsletterSendTest` 8/8 — re-run unmodified as an FSA-13 regression check: still green, confirming `SendNewsletterChunk`'s one-message-per-50-subscriber BCC behavior, batching, and attachment cleanup are untouched. Pint clean. `npm run build` succeeded. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Acknowledged residual risk, not resolved by this fix:** the unsubscribe link necessarily puts `email`+`token` in a GET query string (mailed to the subscriber). Whether the production web server's access-log configuration (Hostinger shared hosting, Apache/Nginx — not part of this repository, not deployed from it) captures full request query strings is **unverified from this codebase** and is **not claimed to be safe**. This is the same category of trade-off Fortify's own password-reset links already make elsewhere in this app, not a risk newly introduced here, but it remains open rather than resolved.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification (local only, matches every other FSA-14 and most other findings in this tracker); that the access-log risk above is resolved.

## FSA-15 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved: block customer self-service cancellation on any courier involvement, with a clear support-contact message. Committed locally (`9c8a9b2`); not pushed, not deployed.**

- **Root cause:** `routes/account.php:113-131` (an inline route closure, the only place in this codebase with real business logic outside a controller class) let a customer cancel any `status = PENDING` order with no row lock and no check for courier involvement. `App\Services\CourierSubmissionService::claim()` allows booking a courier for a `PENDING` order (its `UNSENDABLE_STATUSES` is only `[CANCELLED, REFUNDED, DELIVERED]`), and `markSent()`/`recordConsignmentOnOrder()` write only the consignment columns (`pathao_consignment_id`, `steadfast_consignment_id`, etc.) — **never `order.status`**. Status only becomes `SHIPPED` later, asynchronously, via `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()` (`pathao:sync-statuses`, every 15 minutes, or the Steadfast webhook). So a `PENDING` order can have a fully booked, live courier consignment for a real window of time, during which this route had zero protection.
- **Fix:** the closure now wraps its logic in `DB::transaction()` with `Order::whereKey($order->id)->lockForUpdate()->first()` — the exact same "Order row first" locking convention `CourierSubmissionService`'s own docblock documents and that `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` already uses. Under that lock it refuses (same message for all three conditions, matching the approved wording): `pathao_consignment_id !== null`, `steadfast_consignment_id !== null`, or `CourierSubmissionService::hasUnresolvedSubmission($lockedOrder->id)`. Ownership and the `status === PENDING` requirement are preserved; the legitimate-cancel body (status update, history row) is otherwise unchanged, so `Order::booted()`'s existing `updated` hook still fires stock restoration, coupon release, loyalty reversal and `OrderCancelledMail` exactly as before. The closure was kept as a closure (not moved to a controller) — Laravel resolves closure parameters via the container exactly like controller methods, so injecting `CourierSubmissionService` needed no refactor, per the "avoid unnecessary refactoring" instruction.
- **Concurrency analysis (verified, not assumed):** `CourierSubmissionService`'s own docblock states its lock order is "Order row first, then submission rows," and `claim()`, `markSent()`, `markFailed()`, and `markUnknown()` all lock the Order row (`Order::whereKey(...)->lockForUpdate()`) inside their own `DB::transaction()` before touching anything. Because this fix locks the *same* Order row the *same* way, MySQL's InnoDB row-level locking serializes it against every one of those methods: whichever transaction (this cancel, or a courier claim/resolution) acquires the lock first runs to completion — including `claim()`'s insert of the `SUBMITTING` `CourierSubmission` row, which happens *inside* that same locked transaction, before the lock is released — before the other can even begin. There is no window where this check can read a stale "no unresolved submission" state while a claim is concurrently being created, because the claim's own row-lock-then-insert-then-commit happens atomically with respect to this transaction's lock-then-check. This is the identical guarantee the already-approved `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` relies on for the same `hasUnresolvedSubmission()` check — no broader architectural change was needed, and none was made.
- **Files:** `routes/account.php`, `tests/Feature/CustomerOrderCancellationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `CustomerOrderCancellationTest` 13/13 (new) — a genuinely pending, no-courier order still cancels and reverses stock/coupon/points/mail exactly once; blocked on a resolved Pathao consignment, a resolved Steadfast consignment, an unresolved `SUBMITTING` submission, and an unresolved `UNKNOWN` submission, each leaving every ledger untouched and sending no cancellation mail; a `SENT` submission with `active_order_id` already cleared is still caught by the consignment-column check (proving that check — not `hasUnresolvedSubmission()` alone — is what closes the real gap); a different customer gets 403; every non-pending status (`processing`, `shipped`, `delivered`, `cancelled`, `refunded`) is refused; a second cancel attempt against an already-cancelled order changes nothing further. `SteadfastWebhookTest` 31/31, `ShippedOrderCancellationTest` 10/10, `Admin\CourierSubmissionTest` 37/37, `Admin\CourierSubmissionHardeningTest` 21/21, `Admin\PathaoCourierOrderTest` 12/12 (combined with SteadfastCourierOrderTest), `SyncPathaoOrderStatusesTest` 2/2, `Admin\SecurityRateLimitsTest` 66/66 — all unaffected. `CourierSendConcurrencyRaceTest` 15 skipped (MySQL-only by design, not run on SQLite; unaffected since this fix touches no code that test exercises). Pint clean.
- **Separate finding, out of scope for FSA-15, NOT fixed here — admin-side blind spot:** `Admin\OrderController::cancel()`'s existing (already-committed, FSA-06/FSA-11) guard set never directly checks `pathao_consignment_id`/`steadfast_consignment_id` — it relies on `status === SHIPPED` (set only by the later async sync) and `hasUnresolvedSubmission()` (which goes false the instant `markSent()` clears `active_order_id`). So the exact same timing gap this fix closes for the customer route also exists, unmodified, on the admin cancel path: between a courier booking resolving (`SENT`) and the status sync later promoting the order to `SHIPPED`, an admin can still cancel an order with a live, resolved consignment. **Not touched, not fixed — `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` and the FSA-06/FSA-11 commits remain exactly as committed.** Recommended follow-up investigation: add the same two consignment-column checks to `Admin\OrderController::cancel()`'s existing guard chain (and to `markRefunded()`, which shares the `hasUnresolvedSubmission()` guard at `:499` and likely has the identical gap) — a business decision and a separate implementation, deliberately not bundled into FSA-15.
- **Separate finding, out of scope for FSA-15, NOT fixed here — pre-existing test regression discovered during verification:** `tests/Feature/OrderCancellationAtomicityTest.php`'s test `'a cancelled order cannot then be refunded, and its redeemed points are credited only once'` **fails at HEAD, independent of any FSA-15 change** — confirmed by running it in isolation with no FSA-15 files touched. It builds an order with `payment_status: paid, payment_method: mobile_banking` and expects `admin.orders.cancel` to return session `success`, but FSA-06 (committed earlier this session as `fe6fd58`) now correctly refuses to cancel any `payment_status = PAID` order (`OrderController::PAID_CANCEL_MESSAGE`), so the first assertion in that test fails with session `error` instead. This test was never updated when FSA-06 was implemented and reviewed — it wasn't in that session's focused test list. **Not fixed here** — it's a test-only artifact of an already-committed, approved finding (FSA-06), unrelated to FSA-15, and fixing it would mean touching FSA-06-era test code without a fresh approval for that specific change. Flagging it as a separate, immediate follow-up: the fixture needs either a non-paid payment status (to actually exercise what the test's name describes) or an explicit expectation update acknowledging FSA-06's refusal.

## FSA-17 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03.**

- **Root cause:** `app/Models/User.php`'s `$hidden` listed `password`, `remember_token`, `email_verification_code` but not `two_factor_secret`/`two_factor_recovery_codes`. `Laravel\Fortify\TwoFactorAuthenticatable` (the trait `User` uses) writes both as Laravel-encrypted ciphertext via `Fortify::currentEncrypter()->encrypt()`, and never adds them to `$hidden` itself — that's the consuming app's responsibility, and this app never did it. `HandleInertiaRequests::share()` (`:109`) passes the raw model as `'user' => $request->user()` on every page.
- **Second exposure surface found during investigation, not called out in the original one-line audit entry:** `Admin\CustomerController::index()`/`show()` send the same unhidden columns to any admin viewing the customers section too (`User::withCount('orders')->...->paginate(20)` with no column restriction, and the full route-model-bound `$user` respectively) — not just the customer's own page.
- **Fix:** both columns added to `User::$hidden`. `$hidden` only affects `toArray()`/`toJson()` serialization, never direct property access — confirmed by test that `hasEnabledTwoFactorAuthentication()`, `recoveryCodes()`, and a freshly-reloaded model's raw `two_factor_secret` attribute are all unaffected.
- **Not a plaintext leak either way** — the serialized values were already ciphertext (`APP_KEY`-dependent), so this is a defence-in-depth hardening exactly as the original audit characterized it, not a fix for an active plaintext disclosure.
- **No database change:** no migration, no re-encryption, no existing stored value touched.
- **Files:** `app/Models/User.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/TwoFactorSerializationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `TwoFactorSerializationTest` 6/6 (new) — a customer's own `auth.user` page prop omits both fields; `admin.customers.index`/`admin.customers.show` omit both for a 2FA-enabled customer; other expected `User` fields (`id`, `name`, `email`) still serialize correctly and `password`/`remember_token` stay hidden as before; `hasEnabledTwoFactorAuthentication()`/`recoveryCodes()` still work on both the original instance and a freshly reloaded one. `TwoFactorChallengeTest` 2/2, `AuthenticationTest` 9/9, `Settings\SecurityTest` 6/6 — all unaffected regression. Pint clean.

## Product decision: mandatory 2FA login verification disabled (not an FSA audit finding)

**Superseded 2026-11-07 by SEC-014 (owner decision): 2FA setup and management are removed too, for customers and admins. The "preserved" and "deliberately not done" points below describe the 2026-11-03 state and are kept as history.**

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Explicit owner product decision, separate from and unrelated to FSA-17 — recorded here distinctly, not as part of that finding.**

- **Decision:** neither admin nor customer login may require a 2FA challenge. Password credentials (customer) or admin credentials (admin) alone are always sufficient to log in, even for an account with 2FA already configured. 2FA setup/management (Settings › Security, QR code, recovery codes) is explicitly preserved — only the login-time enforcement is removed.
- **Admin guard:** required **zero code change**. Confirmed by reading `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/AdminAuthController.php` and `app/Models/Admin.php` directly — admin login has never used Fortify or had any `two_factor_*` column; it's hand-rolled (`Auth::guard('admin')->attempt()`). This matches this project's existing, already-recorded decision (admin 2FA was previously declined as its own proposal — see memory `feedback_no_admin_2fa`) and remains unaffected either way.
- **Customer guard — root cause of the enforcement:** Fortify's own login pipeline (`vendor/laravel/fortify/.../AuthenticatedSessionController::loginPipeline()`) runs `RedirectsIfTwoFactorAuthenticatable` (default implementation `Laravel\Fortify\Actions\RedirectIfTwoFactorAuthenticatable`) **before** `AttemptToAuthenticate`, whenever `Features::twoFactorAuthentication()` is enabled in `config/fortify.php` (it is). That action independently re-validates credentials, and if the resolved user has a confirmed `two_factor_secret`, stores `login.id` in session and redirects to `two-factor.login` instead of completing login.
- **Fix:** `FortifyServiceProvider::configureAuthenticationPipeline()` (new private method, called from `boot()`) calls `Fortify::authenticateThrough(...)` — Fortify's own documented customization point — with the exact same default pipeline array Fortify itself would build, minus `RedirectsIfTwoFactorAuthenticatable::class`. `AttemptToAuthenticate` (confirmed by reading its source) independently calls the same `Fortify::$authenticateUsingCallback` and logs the user in directly — it has no 2FA logic of its own, so removing only the redirect step cleanly disables the challenge with no other side effect on password checking, login throttling, username canonicalization, or session preparation.
- **Deliberately not done, and why:** removing `Features::twoFactorAuthentication()` from `config/fortify.php`'s feature list entirely was considered and rejected — Fortify's own route registration (`vendor/laravel/fortify/routes/routes.php:133`) conditionally registers *every* 2FA route (enable, confirm, disable, QR code, recovery codes) only `if (Features::enabled(...))`, so disabling the feature flag would also have destroyed the Settings › Security management page this decision explicitly says to preserve. Keeping the feature enabled and only trimming the login pipeline is the smaller, correct-scoped change.
- **Files:** `app/Providers/FortifyServiceProvider.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/AuthenticationTest.php` (one test rewritten), `tests/Feature/Auth/TwoFactorChallengeTest.php` (one test rewritten), `tests/Feature/Auth/TwoFactorLoginPolicyTest.php` (new).
- **Pre-existing tests updated to match the approved policy change (not weakened — both directly asserted the now-intentionally-removed challenge):**
  - `AuthenticationTest.php`'s `'users with two factor enabled are redirected to two factor challenge'` → rewritten to `'...can still log in using only their password, with no challenge'`, asserting `assertAuthenticated()` + the normal post-login redirect, instead of the old `assertGuest()` + challenge redirect.
  - `TwoFactorChallengeTest.php`'s `'two factor challenge can be rendered'` → rewritten to `'logging in with two factor enabled never reaches the challenge screen'`: login completes directly, and visiting `two-factor.login` afterward is bounced away by that route's own `guest` middleware (already fully authenticated) — proven by test, not assumed.
- **Tests:** `TwoFactorLoginPolicyTest` 4/4 (new) — a customer without 2FA logs in normally; a customer with a confirmed 2FA secret logs in with password alone, no `login.id` in session; an admin logs in normally (no 2FA involved, confirming the admin guard is genuinely untouched); the 2FA QR-code management route stays reachable and functional. `AuthenticationTest` 9/9, `TwoFactorChallengeTest` 2/2, `Settings\SecurityTest` 6/6, `Auth\EmailVerificationCodeTest` 11/11, `Auth\SocialLoginTest` 12/12, `Admin\AdminLoginThrottleTest` 4/4, `Admin\AdminAuthorizationTest` 16/16, `Admin\SecurityRateLimitsTest` — all unaffected regression, confirming password authentication, email verification, admin login/throttling, and unrelated security controls are unchanged. Pint clean.
- **Follow-up from the final review, implemented (text-only):** `resources/js/pages/settings/security.tsx` previously told the user "You will be prompted for a secure, random pin during login..." in both the enabled and not-yet-enabled states — actively misleading once login no longer enforces 2FA. Both strings replaced with "Two-factor authentication settings can be managed here, but login verification is currently disabled." No component, route, backend logic, or 2FA data was touched — confirmed by diff (text-only change) and by re-running `TwoFactorLoginPolicyTest`/`TwoFactorSerializationTest`/`AuthenticationTest`/`TwoFactorChallengeTest`/`Settings\SecurityTest` (all still green), `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build`.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification (local only).

## FSA-18 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner approved FSA-18 plus two adjacent corrections discovered during investigation, in the same pass.**

- **Root cause:** `app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php:26` eager-loads the `reviews` relation capped at the 50 most recent approved reviews (`->latest()->limit(50)`), for display purposes. The old code (`:55-58`) computed `$ratingBreakdown` by looping over that same capped collection in PHP — so for any product with more than 50 approved reviews, the breakdown reflected only the 50 most recent, a window that silently rotates as new reviews arrive (which specific reviews get excluded keeps changing over time, not a fixed historical cutoff).
- **Fix (FSA-18 itself):** the breakdown is now a `GROUP BY rating` SQL query (`$product->reviews()->where('is_approved', true)->selectRaw('rating, COUNT(*) as count')->groupBy('rating')->pluck('count', 'rating')`) — a fresh relation query, not the capped eager-loaded collection — then mapped onto the five star keys (`5..1`, including zero counts) in a fixed order. The 50-review eager-load/display cap is completely untouched.
- **Adjacent correction 1 — product header average rating:** `ProductController::show()` never called `withCount()`/`withAvg()` on the primary `$product` at all (only on `$relatedProducts`), so `product.average_rating` was always `undefined` and the frontend (`ProductDetail.tsx:530`) always rendered its hardcoded `'5.0'` fallback — wrong for *every* product, not just ones with >50 reviews. Fixed via `$product->loadAvg(['reviews as average_rating' => fn ($q) => $q->where('is_approved', true)], 'rating')` — the instance-level equivalent of the `withAvg()` convention already used identically in `ProductListingController`, `HomeController`, `SearchController`, `FlashSaleController`, and `BrandController`. `loadAvg()` returns `null` when there are no matching rows, which is exactly what the existing frontend `?.toFixed(1) || '5.0'` fallback needs — that fallback is unchanged, per instruction, confirmed by test that `average_rating` is `null` (not `0`) for a product with zero approved reviews.
- **Adjacent correction 2 — Reviews tab badge:** the tab badge (`ProductDetail.tsx:817`) read `{reviews.length}` — the same capped-at-50 array — so it also silently capped at "50" past that point. Backend now also loads `reviews_count` via `$product->loadCount(['reviews as reviews_count' => fn ($q) => $q->where('is_approved', true)])` (the instance-level `withCount()` equivalent, same convention), and the one-line frontend change reads `{product.reviews_count ?? 0}` instead. No pagination, no "load all reviews" — this is a single flat aggregate query, not related to the capped display list at all.
- **Consistency:** the breakdown, `average_rating`, and `reviews_count` are three independent queries, but each filters `is_approved = true` on the same `reviews` table for the same product — provably the same population, not just assumed consistent. Confirmed by test that unapproved reviews affect none of the three.
- **Performance:** 3 additional queries added to the page (`loadCount`, `loadAvg`, the `GROUP BY` breakdown), each a single flat aggregate — none loop per-review, none scale with review count (no N+1). Measured via `DB::enableQueryLog()` on a 60-review fixture: the whole `product.show` page issues 27 queries total (every eager-load, the attribute build, and `$relatedProducts`' own `withCount`/`withAvg` included) — the 3 new ones are a small, fixed addition to an already-multi-query page, not a new order of magnitude.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php`, `resources/js/pages/catalog/ProductDetail.tsx`, `tests/Feature/ProductRatingBreakdownTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `ProductRatingBreakdownTest` 5/5 (new) — exactly 50 approved reviews counted correctly (boundary); 51 mixed-rating reviews counted past the old cap; the exact reproduction case (45 older 5-star + 6 newest 1-star = 51) proving every approved review counts regardless of recency, while the displayed review list stays capped at exactly 50; unapproved reviews excluded from the breakdown, `reviews_count`, and `average_rating` alike; zero-approved-reviews reports `average_rating: null` and `reviews_count: 0` (not a fabricated value), preserving the frontend's existing fallback. `ReviewSubmissionTest` 7/7, `ProductVisibilityTest`, `CostPriceVisibilityTest`, `ProductAvailableStockTest` — all green, confirming no regression to unrelated product-page behavior. Pint clean. `tsc --noEmit` clean. `eslint` on the changed frontend file: 0 errors (3 pre-existing, unrelated `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings at lines 118/195/211, nowhere near this change). `npm run build` succeeded.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification (local only).

## FSA-19 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03.**

- **Root cause:** `app/Http/Controllers/SmsTestController.php:48` (file has `declare(strict_types=1)`) called `hash_equals(config('services.sms_forwarder.token'), $token)` directly. `hash_equals()`'s native signature is strictly `(string, string): bool`; if the config value is ever `null` (a stale `config:cache` predating this key, or the key missing entirely — `config('services.services.php')`'s own default is `env('SMS_FORWARDER_TOKEN', '')`, a string, so this specifically requires a cache/config-loading edge case, not a normal missing `.env` line) — passing `null` throws an uncaught `TypeError`, surfacing as an HTTP 500 instead of the endpoint's own uniform 401 contract. Verified directly: `php -r 'declare(strict_types=1); hash_equals(null, "x");'` throws `TypeError: hash_equals(): Argument #1 ($known_string) must be of type string, null given`. The crash only fires when a caller supplies *some* non-empty bearer token (PHP's `||` short-circuits `! $token` first) — so once a server reaches this state, every request carrying any Authorization header 500s until the config is fixed.
- **Pre-existing, already-safe precedent found during investigation:** `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:180-189`'s `sms-forwarder` rate limiter already casts both sides to `string` and excludes empty values before its own `hash_equals()` call — proof this exact defensive shape was already established elsewhere in the codebase, just not applied to the controller's own redundant re-check.
- **Fix:** `$expectedToken = (string) config('services.sms_forwarder.token');` then `if ($expectedToken === '' || ! $token || ! hash_equals($expectedToken, $token)) { ... 401 ... }` — mirrors the rate limiter's shape exactly. The response, status code (401), and JSON body are unchanged; no new status code was introduced for the misconfiguration case, since the endpoint's existing contract already returns one uniform 401 for every auth-failure mode and introducing a distinct status would create a new signal distinguishing "misconfigured" from "wrong token" to anyone probing the endpoint.
- **Preserved unchanged, as instructed:** the request payload format, `ReceivedSms` creation/parsing/matching/reconciliation logic, the `sms-forwarder` rate limiter itself (not touched), and no new authentication mechanism was introduced. No token value — configured or submitted — is logged or included in any response at any point, before or after this fix.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/SmsTestController.php`, `tests/Feature/SmsForwarderAuthenticationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `SmsForwarderAuthenticationTest` 5/5 (new) — a missing (`null`) server token with a caller token present returns 401 and creates no `ReceivedSms` row; a missing server token with no caller token returns 401; an empty (`''`) server token with a caller token present returns 401; the correct server token with the correct caller token still returns 200 `{"status":"ok"}` and correctly creates/parses a `ReceivedSms` row exactly as before; the correct server token with a wrong caller token still returns 401. `MobilePaymentMatchingTest` 17/17, `SmsPatternSafetyTest` 37/37, `Admin\SecurityRateLimitsTest` 66/66 (including its own existing `sms-forwarder` rate-limit coverage) — all green, confirming legitimate SMS forwarding, transaction matching, duplicate-delivery handling, and rate limiting are all unaffected. Pint clean.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification (local only).

## FSA-20 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved limits: 5MB images, 50MB videos, 10 new files per submission.**

- **Root cause:** `app/Http/Requests/Admin/ProductRequest.php`'s `images.*` rule validated content type only (an explicit raster MIME allowlist plus `video/*`, already correctly excluding SVG from an earlier fix) — no `max:` size rule existed anywhere, and the `images` array itself had no count limit. The only real limit was whatever PHP's own `upload_max_filesize`/`post_max_size` happened to allow.
- **Investigated first, as instructed — no reliable production PHP config evidence exists in this repository.** Searched for `php.ini`, `.user.ini`, `.htaccess` upload directives, and any CI/docs reference to `upload_max_filesize`/`post_max_size`: none found beyond this one audit-report line itself. This local environment's CLI `php -i` shows very permissive values (`upload_max_filesize=100M`, `post_max_size=128M`, `memory_limit=-1`) that almost certainly do **not** reflect Hostinger's actual production PHP-FPM/web-SAPI configuration, which is not deployed from this repo and is not observable from here. **Production compatibility with the new 50MB video limit is not verified and is not claimed to be.**
- **Fix:** `ProductRequest.php` now checks `$value->getSize()` against a per-type cap — 5MB (`5 * 1024 * 1024` bytes) for the four already-allowed raster image MIME types, 50MB (`50 * 1024 * 1024` bytes) for anything `video/*` — inside the same existing closure, after the type is already known (a single `max:` rule can't express two different caps on one mixed-type field). The type decision driving which cap applies still comes from `UploadedFile::getMimeType()` (content-sniffed, not the extension or client-supplied header) — unchanged from the existing SVG-rejection behavior. A new `images` array-level closure separately counts only `UploadedFile` instances in the submitted array and rejects more than 10 — a plain string (an existing media path kept as-is) is never counted, so editing a product that already has more than 10 media items, without uploading anything new, is unaffected (confirmed by test).
- **Frontend (advisory only, not the security boundary):** `resources/js/components/ui/multi-media-upload.tsx` mirrors the same three limits (5MB/50MB/10-new-files) using the browser-reported `File.type`/`File.size`, purely to save a legitimate admin an upload-then-rejection round trip. `File.type`/`File.size` are trivially spoofable client-side, so the backend validation above remains authoritative and independently re-checks everything regardless of what the frontend allowed through.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Requests/Admin/ProductRequest.php`, `resources/js/components/ui/multi-media-upload.tsx`, `tests/Feature/Admin/ImageUploadSecurityTest.php` (extended).
- **Deliberately not done, per scope:** no image-processing/resizing/compression library introduced (none existed before, none added); `CategoryRequest`, `SubCategoryRequest`, `ProductBundleRequest`, and `PromotionalBannerController` — which share the same `RasterImageOrUrl` rule and have the identical missing-size-limit gap, found during the investigation — were **not** touched; no migration.
- **Tests:** `ImageUploadSecurityTest` 26/26 (9 new, 17 pre-existing unmodified and still passing) — an image at exactly 5MB accepted, one byte over rejected with the size-specific message; a video at exactly 50MB accepted, one byte over rejected; exactly 10 new files accepted (all 10 `ProductMedia` rows created); 11 new files rejected with nothing stored; a product with 12 pre-existing media rows is still fully editable with no `images` key submitted at all, and its media count is untouched; existing media kept as plain string paths round-trip correctly on update; a mixed batch of 2 good images + 1 oversized one is rejected as a whole, with `ProductMedia::count()` staying 0 and `Storage::disk('public')->allFiles('products')` empty — confirming no partial storage and no orphaned files, exactly as `saveAtomically()`'s existing all-or-nothing design already guaranteed. `ProductSaveAtomicityTest`, `ProductStockConflictTest`, `ProductVariantManagementTest`, `ProductCryBabyTest`, `VariantAndAttributeUniquenessTest`, `ProductDescriptionSanitizationTest` — all green (97/97 combined), confirming no regression to unrelated product create/update behavior. Pint clean. `tsc --noEmit` clean. `eslint` on the changed frontend file: clean (the file is in this project's pre-existing `resources/js/components/ui/*` ESLint-ignore list, unrelated to this change). `npm run build` succeeded.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification; that the 50MB video limit is compatible with production's actual PHP upload configuration.

## FSA-21 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved: normalize common BD mobile formats server-side, then enforce the existing strict regex; frontend UX updated to match.**

- **Root cause:** `LandingPageController::placeOrder()` validated `phone` with only `['required', 'string', 'max:20']`, unlike every other order-creation/identification path (`PlaceOrderRequest`, `ConvertIncompleteOrderRequest`, `TrackIncompleteOrderRequest`), which all require `regex:/^01[3-9][0-9]{8}$/`. No shared phone-validation helper exists anywhere in the app (confirmed by `find app -iname "*phone*"` returning nothing) — each of the 7 call sites inlines its own rule. Garbage (`"N/A"`, a 5-digit string, unsupported symbols) previously created a real `Order`, and the stored value was only ever digit-stripped (not shape-validated) by `PathaoCourierService`/`SteadfastCourierService` before being sent to the courier API, risking a courier-booking failure discovered after the order was already confirmed to the customer. The frontend compounded this: the main checkout's phone `<Input>` (`Checkout.tsx`) is `type="tel"`/`inputMode="numeric"`/digit-stripping/`pattern`-constrained; the landing page's was a bare, unconstrained text field.
- **Investigated and confirmed out of scope / not actually affected:** `Coupon::guestRedemptionCount()` does an exact string match on `shipping_address->phone`, but landing-page orders never apply a coupon (`coupon_id`/`coupon_code` are hardcoded `null` in `completeLandingPageOrder()`), so this path isn't reachable from landing pages today. `IncompleteOrder` abandoned-cart matching is only ever populated by `TrackIncompleteOrderRequest`/`ConvertIncompleteOrderRequest` (both already strict-regex), never by landing pages, so it isn't affected either. `OrderTrackingController::matches()` already normalizes both sides to last-10-digits before comparing, so it's resilient regardless.
- **Fix:** before validating, `placeOrder()` now calls a new private `normalizeBangladeshiPhone()` helper on the raw input and merges the result back into the request. The helper trims whitespace, strips **only** spaces and hyphens (never every non-digit character, so garbage can't be accidentally reshaped into something that passes), then replaces a leading `+880` or `880` prefix with a local `0`. The `phone` rule changed from `max:20` to `regex:/^01[3-9][0-9]{8}$/` (identical to `PlaceOrderRequest`, with the same custom error message), so only the normalized value is ever stored, matching the owner's requirement that the database never hold an unnormalized value.
- **Frontend:** `resources/js/pages/landing/LandingPage.tsx`'s phone `<input>` now has `type="tel"`, `inputMode="tel"`, `maxLength={20}` (generous enough for `+880-170-000-0000`-style input, not restricted to 11 chars), and a permanent helper line ("Enter your mobile number (e.g. 017XXXXXXXX or +88017XXXXXXXX)"). `onChange` is unchanged (no character stripping), so a customer typing `+` or `-` is never fought by the input — normalization happens server-side only, per the owner's explicit requirement.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/LandingPageController.php`, `resources/js/pages/landing/LandingPage.tsx`, `tests/Feature/LandingPagePhoneValidationTest.php` (new).
- **Deliberately not done, per scope:** no shared `Rule`/helper class extracted (the normalizer is a private method on this controller only); `ProfileUpdateRequest`, `RegisterRequest`, `AddressController` — which share the same `max:20`-only pattern but weren't named by this finding — were **not** touched; no migration; no change to `Coupon`/`IncompleteOrder`/`OrderTrackingController` matching logic (confirmed not reachable from this flow, see above).
- **Tests:** `LandingPagePhoneValidationTest` 11/11 (33 assertions) — a plain local number is accepted and stored unchanged; `+880`-prefixed, bare-`880`-prefixed, hyphen-separated local, space-separated local, and hyphen-separated international numbers are all accepted and stored as the normalized `01700000000`; alphabetic input (`"N/A"`), a too-short number, a too-long number, a wrong-operator-prefix number, and a number with unsupported symbols are all rejected with a `phone` validation error and create no `Order`. Sweep of existing landing-page-adjacent tests (`LandingPageCodDuplicateOrderTest`, `LandingPageOrphanedProductTest`, `LandingPageShippingTest`, `MobilePaymentMatchingTest`, `Admin/IncompleteOrderManagementTest`, `OrderTrafficAttributionTest`, `InvoiceDownloadTest`): 74/74 green, no regression (all pre-existing fixtures already used a strict-format phone). Pint clean. `tsc --noEmit`: no errors in the changed file. `eslint` on the changed file: 2 pre-existing warnings (`react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`, unrelated `useEffect` at a different line, not introduced by this change), 0 errors.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification; that a courier (Pathao/Steadfast) actually accepts the normalized value end-to-end (no live/sandboxed courier API call was made — out of scope for this finding, which is about validation, not courier integration).

## FSA-22 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved: minimal fix only (guest→logged-in direction), no session-grant mechanism.**

- **Root cause:** `MobilePaymentController::show()` (line 52) and `submit()` (line 99) both ran `abort_if(auth()->id() !== $pending->user_id, 403)`. `PendingCheckout.user_id` is snapshotted once at creation (`CheckoutController.php:486`, `auth()->id()`) — `null` for a guest. If the same guest later authenticates in that browser before returning to confirm the transaction ID, `auth()->id()` becomes their real id while `$pending->user_id` stays `null`, so the strict `!==` comparison fires and locks the guest out of the exact checkout they just started, potentially after already having sent real money (same failure shape as FSA-07, triggered by an auth-state change instead of a stock/coupon race). The check was introduced undocumented in an early bulk commit (`bd0fc62f`) with no stated intent, unlike the analogous, deliberately-designed `OrderConfirmationAccess` (`app/Support/OrderConfirmationAccess.php`), which already solves this exact class of problem for order confirmation via a session grant.
- **Fix:** both `abort_if` calls now read `abort_if($pending->user_id !== null && auth()->id() !== $pending->user_id, 403)`. Ownership is enforced only when the checkout was staged under a specific logged-in identity; a guest-originated one (`user_id === null`) is accessible by its unguessable, 2-hour-lived token alone, regardless of the current request's auth state — mirroring how `guest_token` already works for order confirmation elsewhere in this app. A logged-in user can never produce `user_id === null` at creation time (it's always `auth()->id()`), so this branch is structurally unreachable for logged-in-originated rows — the fix cannot weaken their protection.
- **Scope, as approved:** minimal fix only. The symmetric case — a logged-in customer stages the checkout, then logs out before paying — is deliberately left exactly as before (still a 403, requiring re-login to resume). No session-grant mechanism, new column, or migration was introduced.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php`, `tests/Feature/MobilePaymentOwnershipTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `MobilePaymentOwnershipTest` 8/8 (21 assertions) — a guest-originated pending checkout is viewable and payable by `show()`/`submit()` after the guest logs in as any user; still viewable with no auth at all (baseline); a different logged-in user is still refused by both `show()` and `submit()` on another logged-in user's pending checkout; an unauthenticated request is still refused by both after the original owner logs out; the original owner themselves remains unaffected throughout. Sweep of adjacent tests (`MobilePaymentMatchingTest`, `GuestCheckoutTest`, `LandingPagePhoneValidationTest`, `CheckoutCodDuplicateOrderTest`, `SmsPatternSafetyTest`, `ReferralProgramTest`, `MobileBankingBundleAttributionTest`, `LoyaltyPointsReservationTest`, `FlashSalePricingTest`, `CouponValidationTest`, `InventoryEnforcementTest`, `IpBlockingTest`, `OrderConfirmationAccessTest`, `Admin/PrunePendingCheckoutsCommandTest`, `Admin/SecurityRateLimitsTest`): 278/278 combined, no regression. `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest` (MySQL-only) skips on SQLite as expected and does not call `show()`/`submit()`, so it's unaffected by this change regardless. Pint clean.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification; that the symmetric logged-in→logged-out case (explicitly out of approved scope) behaves any differently than before.

## FSA-23 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved: minimal fix only — validation allow-list change, nothing else.**

- **Root cause:** `PlaceOrderRequest::rules()`'s `payment_method` rule was `in:cod,zinipay,mobile_banking`, but `CheckoutController::completeCheckout()` only branches on `if (payment_method === COD) {...}`, else unconditionally runs the mobile-banking block — there is no `zinipay` case anywhere in it. `App\Services\ZiniPayService` has exactly one public method, `verifyPayment()` — confirmed by reading the whole file and grepping every call site — no invoice-creation/initiation method exists anywhere, and no controller calls one. `routes/commerce.php:40` already comments the two remaining ZiniPay routes (`payment.callback`, `payment.cancel`) as "kept for historical pending checkouts" — ZiniPay has already been fully decommissioned as a live checkout option. So a `payment_method=zinipay` submission (reachable only via a hand-crafted request — the frontend's `Checkout.tsx:424` type is already `'cod' | 'mobile_banking'` only, never offering `zinipay`) passed validation, then was silently absorbed into the mobile-banking flow, ending up as an `Order` with `payment_method: mobile_banking` despite the caller specifying `zinipay`.
- **Fix:** `PlaceOrderRequest.php`'s rule narrowed to `in:cod,mobile_banking`. A `zinipay` submission now fails validation the same way any other invalid value does — no change needed in `CheckoutController` (the silent fallthrough was a symptom, not a separate bug; it simply can't be reached once `zinipay` fails validation first).
- **Deliberately not touched, per approved scope:** `app/Enums/PaymentMethod.php` (still has `ZINIPAY = 'zinipay'`, needed for historical `Order`/`Payment` rows and the enum display), `app/Http/Controllers/PaymentController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/ZiniPayWebhookController.php`, the historical callback/cancel routes, and all existing historical ZiniPay order/payment records — confirmed by diff that none of these files changed.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Requests/PlaceOrderRequest.php`, `tests/Feature/CheckoutPaymentMethodValidationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `CheckoutPaymentMethodValidationTest` 5/5 (14 assertions) — `payment_method=zinipay` is rejected with a `payment_method` validation error and creates no `Order`/`PendingCheckout`; COD checkout still succeeds; mobile-banking checkout still stages a `PendingCheckout`; an arbitrary invalid value (`bogus`) is still rejected as before. Searched all 9 files referencing `zinipay`/`ZiniPay` beforehand (`LoyaltyPointsReservationTest`, `OrderHistoryProtectionTest`, `ReturnFullRefundConcurrencyRaceTest`, `OrderRefundTest`, `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest`, `InvoiceDownloadTest`, `Admin/SecurityRateLimitsTest`, `Admin/PrunePendingCheckoutsCommandTest`, `Admin/OrderPermanentDeletionTest`) and confirmed none of them submit `payment_method=zinipay` through `checkout.store` — they all create historical rows directly via factories/`DB::table()`/`Payment::create()` or exercise the webhook/callback routes directly, bypassing this validation rule entirely. Ran them plus a broader checkout/payment sweep (`CheckoutCodDuplicateOrderTest`, `GuestCheckoutTest`, `MobilePaymentMatchingTest`, `MobilePaymentOwnershipTest`, `LandingPageCodDuplicateOrderTest`, `LandingPagePhoneValidationTest`, `CouponValidationTest`, `ReferralProgramTest`, `MobileBankingBundleAttributionTest`, `FlashSalePricingTest`, `InventoryEnforcementTest`): 380/380 combined, no regression. Pint clean.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification.

## FSA-24 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Owner-approved: exact 16-key allow-list, scope limited to the file-cache copy only.**

- **Root cause:** `LayoutCache::settings()` cached `Setting::all()` — every row, with no filtering — under `Cache::rememberForever()`, no TTL. The `settings` table's `value` column is where courier/gateway/webhook secrets live (per `Setting.php`'s own `getActivitylogOptions()` docblock), so this file-cache entry (`storage/framework/cache/data/...`, the `file` driver this app uses on Hostinger shared hosting) held a second, indefinitely-persisted plaintext copy of every secret. Traced every consumer: `HandleInertiaRequests::share()`'s shared `settings` Inertia prop already reads through a strict, hand-picked allow-list (confirmed no secret reaches HTTP — the audit's own "positive re-confirmation"), and `Setting::shippingRateFor()` reads exactly two keys. Grepped every `Setting::find(`/`Setting::where('key',` call site in `app/` and confirmed every actual secret read (`PathaoCourierService`, `SteadfastCourierService`, `SteadfastWebhookController`) bypasses `LayoutCache` entirely via direct, uncached queries — so `LayoutCache::settings()` was caching secrets that nothing in the application ever reads back out of it.
- **Fix:** added an explicit `PUBLIC_SETTING_KEYS` constant (exactly the 16 approved keys) to `LayoutCache.php` and changed `settings()`'s query from `Setting::all()` to `Setting::whereIn('key', self::PUBLIC_SETTING_KEYS)->get()`. Mirrors the existing `Setting::RICH_TEXT_KEYS` allow-list pattern on this same table, for the identical stated reason (a blocklist of secret keys could miss a future one; an allow-list fails safe). `Cache::rememberForever()`, the cache key, and `FlushesLayoutCache`'s invalidation are all unchanged — only the inner query narrowed.
- **Deliberately not touched, per approved scope:** `settings.value` DB column encryption, casts, migrations, and every direct secret-reading service/controller (`PathaoCourierService`, `SteadfastCourierService`, `SteadfastWebhookController`, etc.) — confirmed by diff that only `app/Support/LayoutCache.php` and its test file changed. No setting was removed or renamed.
- **Files:** `app/Support/LayoutCache.php`, `tests/Feature/LayoutCacheTest.php` (extended).
- **Tests:** `LayoutCacheTest` 14/14 (8 pre-existing unmodified + 6 new) — a secret-looking key (`pathao_client_secret`) is absent from the rebuilt cached array while a public key is present; two different secret values (`steadfast_api_key`, `steadfast_webhook_secret`) are absent from the array's entire JSON-serialized contents, not just as top-level keys; all 16 approved public keys round-trip correctly when configured, including `Setting::shippingRateFor()` returning the correct saved rates for both zones; existing category/homepage/keyword LayoutCache behavior is unaffected by the narrower settings allow-list; and, using the same real file-cache-store harness as the pre-existing persistence test, a secret value is confirmed **absent from the raw cache file read directly off disk**, while the public `site_name` value is present in it — the most direct proof the fix does what it claims, not just that the constant lists the right keys. Ran the full pre-existing `LayoutCacheTest` suite plus `Admin/ShippingRateSettingsTest`, `CheckoutOrderPlacementTest`, `Admin/RemainingAdminAuditTest`, `ReferralProgramTest`, `GuestCheckoutTest`, `HandleInertiaRequestsSsrExclusionTest`: 76/76 combined, no regression. Pint clean.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification.

## LP-INVOICE-STATUS-001 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED, 2026-11-03. Part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch.**

- **Root cause:** `routes/account.php`'s invoice route already enforces two rules before rendering a PDF — refuse `cancelled`/`refunded` orders, and refuse a COD order that hasn't reached `delivered` — matching the business rule that an invoice must never imply payment was received when it wasn't. `LandingPageController::downloadInvoice()` (the guest landing-page equivalent, routed as `lp.invoice`) only did `Order::where('guest_token', $guestToken)->firstOrFail()` and rendered the PDF unconditionally — neither rule applied. This was not a missing business decision; the rule already existed and was simply never carried over to the second route that needed it.
- **Fix:** added the identical two checks (status `in_array(['cancelled', 'refunded'])`, and `$isCod && $status !== DELIVERED`) to `downloadInvoice()`, reusing `OrderStatus` the same way `routes/account.php` does. The route's existing access control (the unguessable `guest_token`, throttled by `invoice-download`) is unchanged — this only adds the payment-status gate, not a new authorization layer.
- **Preserved exactly:** order payment/shipping status remains the sole source of truth (no new accounting entries, no invoice-status field invented); no historical invoice record is touched (invoices are rendered on demand from the live order, never stored); idempotent by construction (rendering is read-only).
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/LandingPageController.php`, `tests/Feature/InvoiceDownloadTest.php` (extended).
- **Tests:** `InvoiceDownloadTest` 36/36 (19 pre-existing unmodified + 11 new, plus the file's existing ~6 non-parametrized tests unaffected) — a cancelled or refunded guest landing-page order (COD or mobile banking) is refused; an undelivered guest landing-page COD order (pending/processing/shipped) is refused; a prepaid guest landing-page order (mobile banking, any status including before delivery) still downloads. The pre-existing "a guest landing page order downloads by its private token" test (a delivered order) is untouched and still passes.
- **Not claimed:** functional/production verification.

## MED-18 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED, 2026-11-03. Part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch.**

- **Root cause:** `config/session.php`'s `'secure' => env('SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE')` had no default — with the key unset (confirmed absent from `.env.example` too), this resolves to `null`, which Laravel/Symfony treats as falsy, so session cookies are never marked `Secure` unless an admin explicitly sets the env var, regardless of environment. Production's actual `.env` was **not** inspected or assumed either way — this changes only the code-level default for when that key is absent.
- **Fix:** `'secure' => env('SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE', config('app.env') === 'production')`. Defaults to secure cookies in production with zero configuration required, stays insecure (so plain HTTP keeps working) in `local`/`testing`, and an explicit `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` value in `.env` still overrides either way. Uses `config('app.env')`, not `app()->environment()` directly — the same documented reasoning as `RedirectToHttps` (MED-18A): a config-file default is computed once at boot and never re-read per request, so it has to be tested by re-requiring the file fresh after flipping `config('app.env')`, which only works cleanly against `config('app.env')`, not the Application instance's cached environment.
- **Preserved exactly, as instructed:** no change to session lifetime, domain, SameSite, or encryption — only the `'secure'` line.
- **Files:** `config/session.php`, `tests/Feature/SessionSecureCookieDefaultTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `SessionSecureCookieDefaultTest` 4/4 — production with no `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` set defaults to secure; `local` and `testing` both default to insecure; an explicit `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=false` override still wins even in production.
- **Production verification still required (owner, post-deploy):** confirm the actual served `Set-Cookie` header on `https://shoppydrive.com` includes `Secure` — e.g. `curl -sI https://shoppydrive.com/ | grep -i set-cookie`, or inspect cookies in a browser's dev tools — and separately confirm (read-only) whether production's real `.env` already sets `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` to something unexpected that would override this new default. Neither check was performed from here; production `.env` was never accessed.
- **Not claimed:** that production's actual `.env`/served cookies have been inspected, or that this change has been verified against them.
- **Live production verification performed 2026-11-03 (part of the follow-up "FSA-09/FSA-11/MED-18" task) — Step B done with real evidence, Step A still blocked:**
  - **Live HTTP evidence (the actual gold-standard check, now done):** `curl -sS -D - -o /dev/null https://shoppydrive.com/` against the real production domain. The response set five cookies, including the real Laravel session cookie (`shoppydrive-session`) and `XSRF-TOKEN`. Every cookie in the response carried `secure`; every cookie except `XSRF-TOKEN` (which Laravel always sets non-`httponly` by design, so the JS-side CSRF header can read it) carried `httponly`; every cookie carried `samesite=lax`. **So right now, in production, today, the session cookie already has `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, and `SameSite=Lax` — independent of whether this local branch's code fix has been pushed yet.** Cookie values are deliberately not reproduced here, per the no-exposure instruction — only attribute presence/absence was recorded.
  - **Why this doesn't settle Step A:** this one piece of evidence is consistent with *either* of two different causes, and external HTTP alone can't distinguish them: (a) production's real `.env` already sets `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` to a truthy value explicitly, or (b) `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` is unset there too, and Symfony's cookie layer auto-detects `secure` from the live request's actual HTTPS scheme when the config value is literally `null` — exactly the behavior this tracker's own older MED-18 entry (historical section, below) already flagged: "Symfony's Secure-cookie auto-detection already correctly reflects the real request scheme in production regardless of this env var's literal value." Both explanations produce identical, currently-observed behavior over HTTPS. This local code fix (defaulting to `true` in `config('app.env') === 'production'`) is a safety net that holds even in a scenario where the auto-detection fallback *wouldn't* apply (e.g. behind a misconfigured proxy that makes Laravel see the request as plain HTTP) — it doesn't contradict or depend on either explanation being the real one.
  - **Step A (SSH into production to read `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE`'s actual configured value) — still blocked, exact blocker:** no SSH credentials or access to the real Hostinger production server exist in this workspace or environment; `deploy.yml`'s SSH credentials are GitHub Actions repository secrets, never exposed to a local session. No attempt was made to find, extract, or guess any such credential. **Exact safe command for the owner to run themselves** (reads one line only, never the whole `.env`, never prints a value that could be a secret since this one isn't): `ssh <user>@<host> -p <port> "grep -E '^SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=' <deploy_path>/.env || echo '(not set — relies on Symfony auto-detection or this new code default)'"`.
  - **Conclusion:** Step B (live cookie evidence) is now genuinely verified, not assumed. Step A (the explicit production `.env` value) remains unverified — the exact command above is ready for the owner to run.

## FSA-16 notes

**Status: RESOLVED, 2026-11-03.**

- **Nature of this finding:** documentation/process only — the tracker's own deployment-status claims had drifted from what `git`/`origin/main` actually showed. There is no application code, route, controller, or test associated with FSA-16, so it requires no application-level or production functional verification, unlike every code-level FSA finding in this tracker.
- **Evidence the drift was already corrected:** commits `d1f258c` ("docs: consolidate ShoppyDrive forensic audit remediation tracking") and `17a1501` ("docs: add ShoppyDrive release handoff for future sessions") reconciled the tracker against git reality. Both are **confirmed, verified ancestors of `origin/main`** (`git merge-base --is-ancestor d1f258c origin/main` and the same for `17a1501` both return true) — not merely asserted. The tracker's current main-table "DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED" commit citations for FSA-01/03/04/05/09/11/12/13 (`985d73b`, `9c840f7`, `8cb8ff7`, `8d17320`, `1d16a71`, `0ef9d9a`) were independently spot-checked the same way and are all real ancestors of `origin/main` too — the reconciliation holds up under direct verification, not just restated trust.
- **Process note for the future, not a code change:** this remediation session's own branch, `fsa-remediation`, carries several local commits (FSA-02, FSA-06, FSA-07, FSA-08, FSA-10, FSA-14, FSA-15, and later FSA-17 through FSA-24) that are correctly marked `COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED`/"Not deployed" in this tracker, because `origin/main` has not moved since this session began. (An earlier revision of this tracker labeled these rows with a status word implying no commit existed, which was corrected during the 2026-11-03 final pre-release audit — every one of them was already a real local commit the whole time; only the label was wrong. See the audit's own findings for the exact correction.) **Whenever `fsa-remediation` is eventually pushed or merged, the deployment status of each of its included findings must be updated in that same release/reconciliation action** — not left stale the way the original FSA-16 drift happened. Do not mark any of these findings as deployed before that push/merge actually occurs.

## FSA-11 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(orders): prevent admin cancellation of shipped orders (FSA-11)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\OrderController::UNCANCELLABLE_STATUSES` was `[cancelled, refunded, delivered]`, so `shipped` was cancellable once no courier submission was unresolved. Only courier status sync (`CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`: Pathao `in_transit`, `at_delivery_hub`, … → `shipped`) ever sets `shipped`, so the parcel is physically with the courier.
  - Cancelling restored stock still in transit, plus coupon and points through `Order::booted()`.
  - The courier's later "delivered" was then ignored as terminal, recorded but not applied, so COD could be collected against a cancelled order.
- **Existing authoritative workflow (no new workflow added):**
  - Courier cancel and return reports are mapped to `cancelled`: Pathao `pickup_cancelled`, `return_*`, `returned`, `paid_return`, … and Steadfast `cancelled_parcells`. They arrive through the HMAC-verified Steadfast webhook, the `pathao:sync-statuses` poll, or the admin "Refresh status" buttons.
  - That transition runs the same idempotent reversals (stock, coupon, points) exactly once.
  - The courier-submission reconciliation actions (`confirmReturnedConsignment`, `confirmNotCreated`) only resolve booking attempts and never change order status.
- **Fix:**
  - `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` refuses `shipped` inside its existing locked transaction, after the uncancellable check and before any write, with `SHIPPED_CANCEL_MESSAGE` telling the admin to cancel or request a return in the courier's portal. Nothing is written: no status, reason, history, mail, stock, coupon, points or payment change.
  - `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx` shows that procedure instead of the cancel form for shipped orders; the server rule is the enforcement.
  - Pending and processing cancellation, the delivered/refunded/cancelled refusals, and the unresolved-submission guard are unchanged.
- **Late courier reports:** unchanged, already safe. Once an order is `cancelled`, a later delivered report leaves status, payment, `delivered_at`, stock, coupon and points untouched and records a history row ("order is already cancelled; status left unchanged") plus an `OBSERVATION` evidence row. Covered by a new test.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/ShippedOrderCancellationTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/CancelDeliveredOrderTest.php` (the test "admin can still cancel a shipped order" rewritten to expect refusal, because FSA-11 replaces that rule).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):** `ShippedOrderCancellationTest` 8/8:
  - Shipped cancel is refused for unpaid COD and for paid orders. Status and payment status are unchanged, no `stock_restored_at` or `coupon_released_at`, no reason, history or cancellation mail, and stock, points, coupon `used_count`, `CouponUse` and refund ledger rows are unchanged.
  - Pending and processing cancellation still reverses stock (+2), points (+60), coupon and the refund ledger exactly once.
  - Delivered is still refused.
  - An unresolved courier submission still blocks.
  - A Pathao return report cancels a shipped order and reverses everything once.
  - A late Pathao "Delivery" after a courier cancellation changes nothing and is recorded.
- **Sweeps:**
  - Order, courier, refund, inventory, stock and coupon sweep (all non-race files referencing them): 657 tests, 656 passed before the old shipped-cancel test was updated, then all green in the full run.
  - Full suite: 1722 tests, 1659 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint, Prettier, ESLint and `tsc` clean.
  - MySQL race files cancel only `processing` orders, so they are unaffected. MySQL tests were not run.
- **Remaining concerns:**
  - **Lost parcels (decision needed):** a parcel the courier loses may never produce a mapped cancel or return status, and the order then stays `shipped` with no admin way out. That needs a business rule, for example a mother-admin "courier confirmed lost" action with evidence and restock policy. Not invented here.
  - Orders with a consignment booked but not yet picked up (`pending`/`processing`) are still admin-cancellable, as before. The admin must also cancel at the courier, otherwise a later pickup and delivery is recorded but ignored.
- **Lost-parcel visibility follow-up, implemented 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch) — closes the visibility half of the "decision needed" concern above, not the business rule itself:**
  - **Investigated first, as required:** read both `PATHAO_MAP` and `STEADFAST_MAP` in full. Neither includes a distinct "lost" raw status — Steadfast's map is explicitly "the complete set of statuses Steadfast reports, as confirmed directly by Steadfast," and Pathao's has no lost/missing entry either. So there is no specific transition to map, and inventing one would mean guessing at a courier API value that doesn't exist in this integration.
  - **What `sync()` already does safely, confirmed by reading, not assumed:** any unrecognized raw status is still recorded (`CourierDeliveryEvidence`, `OrderStatusHistory`, the raw status column) in the same transaction, but `$statusChanged` stays `false` for a `null` mapping, so the order's own status, payment status, stock and coupon/points are never touched. This already satisfies "never treat a lost parcel as delivered," "no automatic refund," "no automatic stock restore," "preserve evidence," and "prevent duplicate effects" (the existing unchanged-raw-status no-op and the row lock already cover that) — none of those needed new code.
  - **The one real gap: visibility.** Nothing surfaced "this order's courier report is unrecognized" to an admin distinctly from routine history. Added `CourierOrderStatusMapper::isUnrecognized()` and `::isTerminal()` (the latter exposing the existing private `TERMINAL_STATUSES` list to callers outside the class, reusing one definition instead of duplicating it) and a new `Admin\OrderController::possibleLostParcel()` check: true only when the order is **not** already in a terminal status (cancelled/refunded/delivered) **and** its latest stored Pathao or Steadfast raw status doesn't map to anything recognized. Surfaced as `order.needsCourierInvestigation` and an amber warning banner on the admin order page, telling the admin to contact the courier and resolve manually through the existing refund/return workflows — it performs no action itself.
  - **Customer cancellation already can't bypass this:** confirmed `routes/account.php`'s customer cancel (FSA-15) already refuses whenever `pathao_consignment_id` or `steadfast_consignment_id` is set at all, which a parcel mid-transit (lost or not) always has — no change needed there.
  - **Files:** `app/Services/CourierOrderStatusMapper.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LostParcelInvestigationTest.php` (new).
  - **Tests:** `LostParcelInvestigationTest` 6/6 — an active order with an unrecognized Pathao status is flagged; same for Steadfast; a recognized status (`in_transit`) is not flagged; an order with no courier status at all is not flagged; a cancelled order with a stale unrecognized status is not flagged (terminal); a delivered order with a stale unrecognized status is not flagged (terminal).
  - **Deliberately not done, per scope:** no automatic refund, stock restore, status change or order closure of any kind; no new courier status mapping invented; no change to `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`'s own logic (it was already correct); no admin-facing index-page filter added (kept to the order detail page, where "investigate" actually happens).
  - **Outstanding owner decision (unchanged — this follow-up addresses visibility only):** the actual lost-parcel accounting/closure policy — whether a confirmed-lost parcel gets a mother-admin "confirmed lost" action, an automatic or manual restock, and/or a refund path, as the original finding's own "Lost parcels (decision needed)" note already said. This follow-up makes the condition impossible to miss; it doesn't decide what to do about it.
- **Accounting/closure policy investigation, 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, same pass as the FSA-09/MED-18 follow-up) — answers most of the 8 open questions from existing mechanisms, finds one genuine gap, implements no new code:**
  - **1. When is a parcel "confirmed lost"?** Never automatically. The existing `needsCourierInvestigation` flag (above) is the trigger for a human to look, not a determination by itself. A parcel is confirmed lost only when a mother admin records that determination through the **already-existing** `OrderDeliveryVerification` mechanism (`orders.deliveryVerifications.store`), using its existing `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` outcome — read in full in `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()`: for an undelivered order, it already requires *every* linked consignment to be verified as `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` before the order becomes refund-eligible. "Lost" is simply one real-world reason behind a "not delivered" determination — it does not need its own status, column, or admin action; the mechanism that already exists for this is already the right one.
  - **2. What evidence/confirmation is required?** Whatever the mother admin already has to supply for any `OrderDeliveryVerification` today (the existing UI/flow is unchanged) — e.g. the courier's own confirmation of loss, recorded as part of that existing verification action. No new evidence type or field was added.
  - **3. COD orders:** no payment was ever collected (`payment_status` stays `unpaid`), so there is nothing to refund — the correct closure is cancellation, not a refund action. **4. Prepaid orders:** once the consignment(s) are verified `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` and a current courier-readiness attestation exists, `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()` **already** returns `eligible: true` for the existing "Mark as Refunded" action (`RefundService::recordOrderRefund()`) — confirmed by reading its full logic, not assumed. **No new refund path or mechanism is needed for either case** — both route through flows that already exist and are already approved (PAY-RETURN-001).
  - **5. Stock restoration — the one genuine gap found, not invented:** read `Order::booted()`'s `updated` hook in full. It calls `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` **unconditionally** on *any* transition to `CANCELLED` or `REFUNDED` — there is no existing signal anywhere that distinguishes "the physical goods came back to the merchant" (a normal return/cancellation, where restocking is correct) from "the goods are gone and will never come back" (a confirmed-lost parcel, where restocking would silently inflate stock for inventory that no longer exists). This is not a new bug introduced by this finding — it is a pre-existing, blanket behavior of the refund/cancellation side effect that a lost-parcel policy would collide with if the existing cancel/refund actions were used as-is for a lost parcel. **This is the one point in the full policy that is genuinely ambiguous and needs an owner decision** — see below.
  - **6. Coupon and loyalty-point reversal:** already correct and already automatic for both the COD-cancel and prepaid-refund paths — the same `Order::booted()` hook that (over-)restocks also calls `LoyaltyPointsService::handleOrderCancelledOrRefunded()` on the same transition, and `CouponService::release()` already runs from that same existing cancellation path. Nothing new needed here; this part of the existing mechanism is correct for a lost parcel exactly as it is for a normal one.
  - **7. Duplicate courier events / repeated admin actions:** already handled by existing, already-tested mechanisms — `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`'s own row lock and unchanged-raw-status no-op prevent duplicate evidence/history from a repeated webhook; `RefundService`'s existing idempotent refund-amount-against-remaining-balance check prevents a double refund; `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()`'s `stock_restored_at` guard prevents a double restock. No new idempotency work is needed — reusing the existing refund/cancel paths inherits all of this for free.
  - **8. How the decision is recorded:** already covered — `OrderDeliveryVerification` rows and `CourierDeliveryEvidence` are both already append-only audit trails (the architecture doc's own description), and the existing cancellation/refund action already writes an `OrderStatusHistory` row. No new audit mechanism needed.
  - **Conservative-default check, item by item:** "ambiguous/unrecognized status must not auto-trigger a refund" — already true (`needsCourierInvestigation` is informational only). "Investigation state first" — already true (same flag). "Refund requires confirmed loss + authorized admin action through the existing refund process" — already true and already enforced by `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()`'s existing not-delivered-verification requirement. "Stock not automatically restored merely because reported lost" — **this is the one point current behavior does not satisfy**, per item 5 above. "Idempotent and auditable" — already true, per items 7–8.
  - **Owner decision required (the one real gap, not resolved here):** should marking a parcel lost (via the existing refund/cancel action) skip the automatic stock restoration that currently fires unconditionally on every `CANCELLED`/`REFUNDED` transition? Two safe options, neither implemented: **(a)** accept current behavior and require the admin to manually re-decrement stock as a separate, deliberate correction after a lost-parcel refund/cancel (zero code change, relies on admin discipline); **(b)** add an explicit, opt-in way to skip the restock step for this one case specifically (e.g. a checkbox/flag on the existing refund/cancel action, read by `Order::booted()`'s hook) — a real code change to a shared mechanism used by every other refund/cancellation too, so it needs explicit sign-off before touching it, not a decision to infer. **Recommendation:** option (b) is more correct and more auditable (no reliance on an admin remembering a manual step), but touches shared financial/inventory code used by every refund in the system, so it is deliberately left as a decision rather than implemented in this pass.
  - **No new application code was written for this investigation** — it answers the policy from reading `CourierEvidenceChain`, `RefundService`, `Order::booted()`, `InventoryService`, `LoyaltyPointsService`, and `CouponService` as they already exist, confirms most of the policy needs zero new code, and isolates the one point that does to an explicit, unimplemented decision — exactly as instructed for an ambiguous financial decision.
- **Skip-restock mechanism implemented, 2026-11-03 (owner-approved, same pass) — resolves the one gap identified above:**
  - **New column:** `orders.lost_confirmed_at` (nullable timestamp, migration `2026_11_03_190000`) — null for every ordinary order; set only by the new action below. Deliberately a dedicated column, not an overload of `stock_restored_at`'s existing meaning, so the audit trail stays honest (the field name says what actually happened).
  - **`InventoryService::restoreForOrder()`:** one extra OR-condition in its existing idempotency guard — `|| $locked->lost_confirmed_at !== null` — added to the existing `if ($locked === null || $locked->stock_restored_at !== null)` early-return. This is the entire change to this shared method; every other order's cancel/refund restocking is byte-for-byte unaffected, confirmed by test (below).
  - **New `App\Services\OrderLostParcelService::confirm()`:** mother-admin only (`$admin->is_mother`, `MotherPasswordGate` — same lock/verify pattern as `OrderDeliveryVerificationService`); requires a ≥10-character note (same convention as delivery verification's own note requirement); under a row lock, refuses if already confirmed lost (idempotent — no duplicate history, no duplicate effect of any kind); requires `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility($order)` to already report `courier_involved: true` and `eligible: true` — i.e. **exactly** the same evidence a refund already requires (every linked consignment verified `not_delivered`) — an unrecognized/ambiguous status alone is **not** accepted, satisfying that requirement directly by reusing the existing check rather than writing a new one. On success: sets `lost_confirmed_at`, writes one `OrderStatusHistory` note naming the admin and the note text. Never changes order `status` or `payment_status` itself — confirming lost is not a closure action; the admin performs the existing cancel/refund action separately afterward. Deliberately writes no `CourierDeliveryEvidence` row — that table's writers are a closed, arch-tested allowlist (`tests/Unit/CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest.php`) this service correctly isn't part of; the `OrderStatusHistory` note alone already satisfies "how the decision is recorded in order history."
  - **New route:** `POST admin/orders/{order}/confirm-lost` (`orders.confirmLostParcel`), `admin.mother` + `throttle:30,15` + 10-attempt session block — the identical middleware stack `orders.deliveryVerifications.store` already uses for the same class of sensitive, mother-only action.
  - **Admin order page:** `Admin\OrderController::confirmLostParcel()` thin controller wrapper (validate `password`/`note`, delegate, catch `CourierVerificationRefusedException` the same way `markRefunded()` already catches `CouldNotRecordRefundException`). `lost_confirmed_at` is automatically present in the existing `order.toArray()` payload (a real column now) — no frontend UI trigger was built in this pass (see limitation below).
  - **Files:** `database/migrations/2026_11_03_190000_add_lost_confirmed_at_to_orders_table.php` (new), `app/Models/Order.php`, `app/Services/InventoryService.php`, `app/Services/OrderLostParcelService.php` (new), `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `routes/admin.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LostParcelConfirmationTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest.php` (one exemption-list entry added during the final pre-commit review — see below).
